# Digestpedia — Master Index

> Content-oriented catalog. Updated on every ingest. Read this first when answering queries.

---

## Overview

- [[needed-sources]] — Download list: every paper, guideline, label, figure, or table that would close a named gap on a page but is not in `raw/`, grouped by type with the waiting page(s). Add the file, ingest, delete the row.
- [[overview]] — Pointer page: defers to `README.md` as the single source of truth for wiki-level documentation (scope, structure, therapeutic landscape, key frameworks, evidence sources)

---

## Disease Scripts

### Foregut and Motility Diseases

#### Esophageal

- [[eosinophilic-esophagitis]] — Eosinophilic esophagitis (EoE): Assessment, Differential Diagnosis, Diagnostics, Therapeutics (ADDT) — dysphagia, eosinophilic inflammation, proton pump inhibitor (PPI)/swallowed topical corticosteroids (STC)/diet/dupilumab; IMPACT compensatory behaviors per American College of Gastroenterology (ACG) 2025 Table 4 (incl. *turn away tablets/pills*); eosinophil count **does not** track severity; dilation sensitivity for strictures 14.7% (endoscopy misses most), 71% respond despite no perceived narrowing; persistent symptoms despite remission → evaluate for stricture/motility disorder (5 sources)
- [[infectious-esophagitis]] — Infectious esophagitis: ADDT — Candida (fluconazole 200–400 mg × 14–21 d), herpes simplex virus (HSV) (acyclovir/valacyclovir 10–21 d), cytomegalovirus (CMV) (ganciclovir up to 6 wk); endoscopic appearance + biopsy/brushing dx; immunocompromise workup (1 source)
- [[lymphocytic-esophagitis]] — Lymphocytic esophagitis: ADDT — peripapillary lymphocytosis histology, women >60, dx of exclusion (biopsy off PPI, r/o EoE/Crohn's); PPI/topical steroids/dilation (1 source)
- [[barretts-esophagus]] — Barrett's Esophagus: ADDT — Prague C&M criteria, intestinal metaplasia (IM) required, dysplasia grading (nondysplastic Barrett's esophagus (NDBE)/low-grade dysplasia (LGD)/high-grade dysplasia (HGD)/intramucosal carcinoma (IMC)); **American Gastroenterological Association (AGA) 2025 now integrated** — NDBE q3y (extend to q5y if <3 cm), high-definition white-light endoscopy (HD-WLE) + chromoendoscopy (**Strong**), structured biopsy rule (4-quadrant q2 cm without dysplasia history, **q1 cm with**), <1 cm columnar-lined esophagus (CLE) with IM → against surveillance, indefinite for dysplasia (IND)/LGD confirmation within 6 mo on high-dose acid suppression, no endoscopic eradication therapy (EET) for confirmed IND, wide-area transepithelial sampling with 3-dimensional analysis (WATS-3D)/p53/TissueCypher as knowledge gaps, BOSS randomized controlled trial (RCT); EET indications with AGA 2024 grades, endoscopic mucosal resection (EMR)-vs-endoscopic submucosal dissection (ESD) selection, post-complete eradication of intestinal metaplasia (CEIM) intervals (6 sources)
- [[distal-esophageal-spasm]] — Distal esophageal spasm (DES): Chicago v4.0 — ≥20% premature contractions, distal contractile integral (DCI) >450, normal integrated relaxation pressure (IRP); peroral endoscopic myotomy (POEM) for refractory dysphagia-predominant disease (case-by-case, after medical/botox/dilation) (5 sources)
- [[esophagogastric-junction-outflow-obstruction]] — Esophagogastric junction outflow obstruction (EGJOO): manometric finding (not a diagnosis) — Chicago v4.0 (elevated median IRP supine+upright + intrabolus pressure ≥20% supine swallows); exclude mimics (hiatal hernia/gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD)/opiates/obesity/artifact); less-invasive-first, POEM case-by-case (AGA 2024) (3 sources)
- [[functional-heartburn]] — Functional heartburn: retrosternal burning persisting on **double-dose PPI ≥3 months** with normal acid exposure, no major motor disorder, no mucosal pathology **and a negative reflux–symptom association**; the diagnostic triad required to make it (esophagogastroduodenoscopy (EGD) with biopsies + high-resolution manometry (HRM) + pH off PPI / pH-impedance on PPI); separation from nonerosive reflux disease (NERD) and reflux hypersensitivity; neuromodulators and brain–gut behavioral therapy — **escalating acid suppression or operating is ineffective and potentially harmful** (2 sources)
- [[hypercontractile-esophagus]] — Jackhammer esophagus: Chicago v4.0 — DCI >8000 in ≥20% swallows; not absolute contraindication to antireflux surgery (ARS) if objective GERD (5 sources)
- [[ineffective-esophageal-motility]] — Ineffective esophageal motility (IEM): Chicago v4.0 revised — ≥70% ineffective or ≥50% failed; fragmented peristalsis eliminated; assess peristaltic reserve pre-ARS (3 sources)
- [[esophageal-stricture]] — Esophageal stricture: ADDT — a **transmural, fibrotic** narrowing (mucosa + submucosa + muscularis propria all expand), so it outlives the inflammation that caused it and **no imaging separates inflammation from fibrosis**; focal stricture vs **narrow-caliber esophagus** typing (drives bougie-vs-balloon, and focal carries OR 4.6 for perforation); endoscopy is insensitive and a normal-looking scope does not exclude one; barium esophagram and EndoFLIP as the occult-stricture tests (pediatric esophagram Rec 18, very low/conditional); dilation is an **adjunct to anti-inflammatory therapy**, never monotherapy (Rec 15, low/conditional) — technique stays on [[esophageal-dilation]]; peptic stricture = EGD quality indicator 23 (acid suppression after dilation, target >98%, grade 2B) and an explicit exception to weaning PPIs; **do not scope just to recheck a dilated benign stricture**. Schatzki ring and anastomotic strictures deliberately absent — no ingested source (6 sources)
- [[esophageal-cancer]] — Esophageal/esophagogastric junction (EGJ) cancer: adenocarcinoma (distal/Barrett's) vs. squamous cell carcinoma (SCC) (mid/upper); progressive solid dysphagia + weight loss; EGD/endoscopic ultrasound (EUS)/positron emission tomography (PET) staging; National Comprehensive Cancer Network (NCCN) ADDT — endoscopic resection (T1a) vs perioperative fluorouracil, leucovorin, oxaliplatin, docetaxel (FLOT)±durvalumab/CROSS chemoRT + esophagectomy vs definitive chemoRT (SCC); metastatic biomarker therapy (HER2/PD-L1 combined positive score (CPS)/microsatellite instability–high (MSI-H)); treatment-algorithm Mermaid; **T1b substaging and nodal risk** — SM1 ceiling 200 µm squamous vs 500 µm adenocarcinoma, LNM by depth × cell type, Table 1 risk predictors, endoscopic/narrow-band-imaging depth prediction, and the T1b management position (esophagectomy mainstay; endoscopic resection only for low metastatic potential) (4 sources)
- [[esophageal-adenocarcinoma]] — Esophageal adenocarcinoma (EAC): metaplasia–dysplasia–carcinoma sequence from Barrett's; EET for T1a/HGD, esophagectomy for T1b sm2-3; **sm1 nodal risk now quantified (6%; 4.2% if well-differentiated and <2 cm)** and the **duplicated-muscularis-mucosae = T1a** staging trap; oncologic staging on esophageal-cancer (NCCN) (5 sources)

#### GE Junction

- [[achalasia]] — Achalasia: ADDT — HRM/Chicago Classification subtypes (I/II/III), pneumatic dilation (PD)/laparoscopic Heller myotomy (LHM)/POEM equivalence, POEM preferred for Type III, botulinum toxin reserve-only, post-POEM reflux + esophageal-cancer surveillance (hazard ratio (HR) 4.6); secondary/mimic achalasia (coronavirus disease (COVID)/Chagas/eosinophilic) (AGA 2024) (7 sources)
- [[gerd]] — GERD: ADDT — Lyon Consensus 2.0 diagnostic framework, empiric PPI trial, endoscopic (transoral incisionless fundoplication (TIF) 2.0/concomitant TIF (cTIF)) and surgical options, extraesophageal manifestations, refractory GERD workup, hiatal hernia size as key decision point, Barrett's esophagus (BE) risk after sleeve gastrectomy (8 sources)
- [[hiatal-hernia]] — Hiatal hernia: ADDT — sizing in **two** dimensions (axial length *and* transverse hiatal diameter; either >2 cm disqualifies TIF), endoscopy underestimates vs laparoscopy, HRM most sensitive (94.3%/91.5%), documentation omitted in 49% of reports; paraesophageal hernia = TIF contraindication; cTIF targets 2–3 cm intra-abdominal esophageal length (hard stop), not a hernia-size ceiling; Cameron lesions. **Scope-limited — no dedicated hiatal hernia guideline ingested (type I–IV classification, paraesophageal hernia (PEH) repair indications, volvulus absent)** (5 sources) **SAGES 2024 adds the surgical layer:** the type I–IV definitions, fundoplication during type II–IV repair (conditional, low certainty — objective reflux RR 0.31, recurrence 0.55, leak 0.33, against late dysphagia RR 2.94), **no recommendation either way on mesh**, the "most asymptomatic paraesophageal hernias are not asymptomatic" criteria (dyspnea, exercise intolerance, abnormal echo), watchful waiting and the gastric-volvulus discussion, and the RYGB-conversion criteria for recurrence without obesity

#### Gastric

- [[atrophic-gastritis]] — Atrophic gastritis: ADDT — H. pylori–associated atrophic gastritis (HpAG) vs. autoimmune gastritis (AIG) etiology, Operative Link on Gastritis Assessment (OLGA)/Operative Link on Gastric Intestinal Metaplasia Assessment (OLGIM) staging, Sydney protocol, pernicious anemia, type I gastric neuroendocrine tumors (NETs), surveillance intervals (2 sources)
- [[functional-dyspepsia]] — Functional dyspepsia (FD): ADDT — Rome V B1/B1a-postprandial distress syndrome (PDS)/B1b-epigastric pain syndrome (EPS) taxonomy and the Rome Clinical-Criteria threshold, ACG/CAG 2017 Statements 7-14 with grades and effect sizes (eradication NNT 12.5 -> PPI NNT 10, stop at 8 wk -> TCA NNT 6 **before** prokinetic -> psychological therapy NNT 3), SSRIs ineffective (RR 1.01), the prokinetic availability problem, gastric-emptying and accommodation data (70%-80% normal emptying; accommodation abnormal in up to 40%), and the gut-brain neuromodulator ranking (TCAs 2nd for FD, ahead of PPIs) (4 sources)
- [[gastroparesis]] — Gastroparesis: ADDT — 4-hour scintigraphy (>10% retention), Gastroparesis Cardinal Symptom Index (GCSI), AGA 2025's 12 graded recommendations (metoclopramide/erythromycin first-line; **against** domperidone, prucalopride, aprepitant, nortriptyline, buspirone, cannabidiol (CBD) as first-line; **against routine gastric peroral endoscopic myotomy (G-POEM) and gastric electrical stimulation (GES)**, reserved for refractory; surgical pyloric intervention = knowledge gap), definition of "refractory", EndoFLIP for pyloric distensibility index (DI); whole-gut (wireless motility capsule [WMC]) evaluation — treating coexisting slow colonic transit improves upper-gastrointestinal (GI) symptoms (4 sources)
- [[gastric-intestinal-metaplasia]] — Gastric intestinal metaplasia (GIM): ADDT — Correa cascade, H. pylori test-and-treat (32% gastric cancer (GC) relative risk (RR) reduction), against routine surveillance (AGA 2020) vs. q3y surveillance (ACG 2025/European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE)) for high-risk; incomplete-type/extensive GIM/family history = high risk; ACG 2025 quality-endoscopy + Sydney containering + dysplasia/ESD pathway (4 sources)
- [[gastric-adenocarcinoma]] — Gastric Adenocarcinoma: intestinal vs diffuse (Lauren), Correa cascade, EUS/computed tomography (CT)/PET + diagnostic laparoscopy staging; NCCN ADDT — endoscopic resection (T1a) vs perioperative FLOT + gastrectomy/D2 (± dostarlimab for MSI-H/mismatch repair–deficient (dMMR)) vs metastatic biomarker therapy (HER2→trastuzumab/trastuzumab deruxtecan (T-DXd), PD-L1 CPS→nivo/pembro, claudin 18.2→zolbetuximab); early T1a endoscopic-resection criteria (ESD-preferred); treatment-algorithm Mermaid (3 sources)
- [[helicobacter-pylori-infection]] — H. pylori: ADDT — epidemiology, pathogenesis, diagnosis; eradication regimens rebuilt against ACG 2024 Tables 5/6 + Recs 1–12 with correct doses and grades (optimized bismuth quadruple therapy (BQT) **Strong**; vonoprazan dual [amoxicillin 1,000 mg **t.i.d.**] and triple both **Conditional**; Talicia rifabutin triple as first-line; concomitant **not** suggested over BQT; **no recommendation** for vonoprazan regimens as salvage); resistance data (22.2% RCT, 31.5% pooled US); susceptibility-testing rules (4 sources)
- [[gastric-premalignant-conditions]] — Gastric premalignant conditions (GPMC): ADDT — Correa cascade, GIM surveillance, dysplasia/ESD referral, H. pylori eradication (2 sources)
- [[gastric-malt-lymphoma]] — Gastric mucosa-associated lymphoid tissue (MALT) lymphoma: ADDT — extranodal marginal zone B-cell lymphoma; nearly all H. pylori-positive; endoscopy ranges from subtle erosions to nodular masses (biopsy regardless); EUS for depth/nodal prognosis; eradication alone is primary therapy for early stage (75.2% complete remission); post-eradication EGD q3–6mo × 2y then q6–12mo (2 sources)
- [[peptic-ulcer-disease]] — Peptic ulcer disease (PUD): ADDT — H. pylori/nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID) etiology, ulcer biopsy (duodenal ulcer (DU) not routine, gastric ulcer (GU) individualized), EGD with Forrest classification, PPI twice daily (BID) 4–12 wk, follow-up EGD for gastric ulcers, balloon dilation for benign gastric outlet obstruction (4 sources)
- [[hereditary-diffuse-gastric-cancer]] — Hereditary diffuse gastric cancer (HDGC): ADDT — CDH1, 67%/83% M/F lifetime GC risk, prophylactic gastrectomy after age 20, pre-gastrectomy EGD low sensitivity, lobular breast cancer surveillance (3 sources)
- [[rumination-syndrome]] — Rumination syndrome: ADDT — Rome V gastroduodenal disorder of gut–brain interaction (DGBI) (B4); effortless regurgitation of bland recently-eaten food without nausea/retching; behavioral mimic of refractory GERD; postprandial high-resolution impedance manometry (HRIM) (intragastric pressure rise >30 mm Hg, sens 75–80%/spec 100%) after EGD; Therapeutics pending a primary source (2 sources)
- [[portal-hypertensive-gastropathy]] — Portal hypertensive gastropathy (PHG): ADDT — "snakeskin" mosaic mucosa; **the Baveno III score table lives here** (mosaic 1–2 + red markings 1–2 + GAVE 1–2; mild ≤3, severe ≥4) and is the threshold behind AASLD GS 48's "greater than mild"; prevalence by variceal burden (11% none / 35% small / 80–97% medium-large); exclude H. pylori first; acute bleeding 2.5–5% vs chronic blood loss 4–12% (defined as Hgb fall 2 g/dL over 6 mo); NSBB first → vasoactive therapy 2–5 d for acute → TIPS as rescue, with Baveno VIII 5.44 **demoting** endoscopic therapy to focal/salvage; oral iron first (no malabsorptive defect); PH-related polyps — biopsy discouraged (4 sources)
- [[gastric-antral-vascular-ectasia]] — Gastric antral vascular ectasia (GAVE): ADDT — antral endoscopic diagnosis associated with cirrhosis, chronic kidney disease and **systemic sclerosis**; **must be distinguished from PHG because the treatments diverge** (no NSBB role); the two different escalation triggers side by side (Baveno VIII 5.43 "requiring serial transfusion" vs AGA BPA 12 "inadequate response to iron"); **the EBL-vs-thermal head-to-head table lives here** (−2.30 units transfused, +0.59 g/dL Hgb, 2.63 vs 3.83 sessions) behind Baveno VIII's EVL-over-APC recommendation (LoE 1, strong) — a reversal of Baveno VII 6.25; no proven pharmacologic therapy (4 sources)
- [[cyclic-vomiting-syndrome]] — Cyclic vomiting syndrome (CVS): ADDT — Rome IV criteria; American Neurogastroenterology and Motility Society–Cyclic Vomiting Syndrome Association (ANMS-CVSA) mild vs moderate–severe strata (drives prophylaxis decision); 4 phases; workup to exclude mimics (no routine GES); tricyclic antidepressant (TCA) first-line prophylaxis + triptan/antiemetic abortive combos; emergency department (ED) management (3 sources)
- [[nausea-and-vomiting-of-pregnancy]] — Nausea and vomiting of pregnancy (NVP) & hyperemesis gravidarum: ADDT — Pregnancy-Unique Quantification of Emesis (PUQE) score, pyridoxine (B6) + doxylamine first-line, hydration/antiemetic ladder, hyperemesis gravidarum (HG) management (1 source)
- [[cannabinoid-hyperemesis-syndrome]] — Cannabinoid hyperemesis syndrome (CHS): ADDT — CVS subtype from chronic heavy cannabis use; proposed criteria (≥3 episodes/yr, use >1 yr & >4×/wk, resolution ≥6 mo off cannabis); compulsive hot-water bathing (supportive, not pathognomonic); topical capsaicin 0.1% + haloperidol acutely, cannabis cessation + amitriptyline long-term, avoid opioids (3 sources)
- [[bariatric-surgery-leak]] — Staple-line leak after bariatric surgery: ADDT — the early/intermediate/late timing classification that selects the therapy, CT with oral contrast and the limits of a negative study, and the four endoscopic strategies (closure, diversion with fully covered stents, internal drainage with double-pigtail stents, septotomy) with the anatomy and leak age each suits (1 source)

#### Small Bowel

- [[celiac-disease]] — Celiac Disease: ADDT — ≥6 biopsies (bulb + postbulbar), Marsh grading, immunoglobulin A (IgA) deficiency caveat, nonbiopsy dx in children, gluten-free diet (GFD) monitoring, refractory celiac disease type 1 vs. 2, enteropathy-associated T-cell lymphoma (EATL) risk (2 sources)
- [[small-intestinal-bacterial-overgrowth]] — Small intestinal bacterial overgrowth (SIBO)/intestinal methanogen overgrowth (IMO): ADDT — rebuilt on AGA's pathophysiologic risk-factor table; **diarrhea, not bloating, has the strongest association** (AGA 2020 reverses the common belief ACG states); breath-test protocol **plus AGA's explicit refusal to issue any cutoff** ("never fully validated compared with culture"; the 2-peak criterion "has not been validated"); **>10³ CFU/mL coliforms on fresh aspirate**; dual AGA/ACG antibiotic dose table (the two societies differ on all four drugs); 7–10 day course; **do not retest if symptoms respond** (2 sources)
- [[meckels-diverticulum]] — Meckel's diverticulum: ectopic gastric mucosa (10–60%) → ulceration/bleeding, a leading small-bowel bleeding cause in <40y; Tc-99m pertechnetate (Meckel) scan for dx; surgical resection (2 sources)
- [[bile-acid-diarrhea]] — Bile acid diarrhea/malabsorption: Type 1 (ileal)/2 (idiopathic, irritable bowel syndrome with diarrhea (IBS-D) overlap)/3 (post-cholecystectomy, chronic pancreatitis (CP), celiac); selenium-75 homocholic acid taurine (SeHCAT)/C4/FGF19 or empiric sequestrant trial; cholestyramine/colesevelam (4 sources)
- [[intestinal-methanogen-overgrowth]] — IMO: ADDT — archaea (*M. smithii*) not bacteria, so distinct from SIBO; constipation association; CH₄ ≥10 ppm breath-test threshold (vs H₂ rise ≥20 ppm for SIBO); rifaximin + neomycin — AGA corroborates the combination but grades it "one uncontrolled trial" and gives no doses (2 sources)
- [[short-bowel-syndrome]] — Short bowel syndrome (SBS)/intestinal failure: ADDT — residual small bowel **≤200 cm** (measurement convention: antimesenteric border, unstretched, duodenojejunal flexure → ileocecal junction/anastomosis/stoma; surgeons must report *residual*, not resected); anatomic groups 1–3 and the prognosis each carries; hydration targets (urine >1 L/d, urinary Na >20 mEq/L) and diet rules that **invert by anatomy** (colon in continuity → 60% complex carb/20% fat + mandatory oxalate restriction; end-jejunostomy → no fat restriction, oral rehydration solution (ORS) not hypotonic water); micronutrient monitoring table; **the parenteral nutrition (PN) weaning window — <6% wean if not off within 2 years of the last resection**; teduglutide gated by malignancy screening; intestinal transplantation (ITX) referral at PN failure. ⚠ Teduglutide dose, PN kcal/protein targets, and Table 1 microgram units are flagged gaps (1 source)

### Colorectal Diseases

- [[rectal-prolapse]] — Rectal prolapse: ADDT — full-thickness vs mucosal prolapse vs internal intussusception (the distinction that decides surgery); left-lateral then squatting/commode re-exam; circular vs radial folds separates it from prolapsing hemorrhoids; laparoscopic rectopexy preferred (Altemeier 27% recurrence); solitary rectal ulcer syndrome (SRUS) pathway (5 sources)
- [[acute-mesenteric-ischemia]] — Acute mesenteric ischemia (AMI): venous (mesenteric venous thrombosis [MVT]) built from ACG 2020 — anticoagulate, thrombophilia workup; AMI-vs-colon-ischemia distinction; **arterial AMI/nonocclusive mesenteric ischemia (NOMI) explicitly not covered by any ingested source** (2 sources)

- [[crohns-disease]] — Crohn's disease: ADDT — transmural inflammatory bowel disease (IBD), biologics (anti-tumor necrosis factor (TNF)/vedolizumab/interleukin-23 (IL-23)/Janus kinase (JAK)), perianal fistula, postoperative prevention, high-definition (HD)+chromoendoscopy colorectal cancer (CRC) surveillance (SCENIC), endoscopic balloon dilation of benign strictures ≤4 cm; inpatient Crohn's disease (CD) complications (obstruction/abscess/perianal); therapeutic endoscopy (endoscopic balloon dilation (EBD)/stricturotomy/fistula); IBD-in-malignancy drug management (14 sources)
- [[ulcerative-colitis]] — Ulcerative colitis: ADDT — proctitis-to-pancolitis, 5-aminosalicylic acid (5-ASA)/biologics/JAK/sphingosine-1-phosphate (S1P) modulators, acute severe ulcerative colitis (ASUC) management, ileoscopy (backwash vs CD ileitis), HD+chromoendoscopy dysplasia surveillance (SCENIC). ASUC day-3 prediction carries **both** instruments — the Oxford (Travis) index and the **Ho index** (3-day mean stool frequency + dilation ≥5.5 cm + albumin <30 g/L; bands 11% / 45% / 85%) (20 sources)
- [[perianal-fistulizing-crohns-disease]] — Perianal fistulizing Crohn's disease: simple vs complex criteria; drain and seton before immunosuppression; pelvic MRI and EUS comparable (>90%); infliximab 5 mg/kg wk 0/2/6 then q8wk (strong) vs conditional adalimumab/vedolizumab/ustekinumab/upadacitinib; antibiotic doses; fistulotomy vs seton by rectal mucosal status; diversion/proctectomy (3 sources)
- [[pouchitis]] — Ileal pouch inflammation post-ileal pouch–anal anastomosis (IPAA); subtype-driven Tx (intermittent/antibiotic-dependent/refractory, Crohn's-like, cuffitis); antibiotics → advanced therapy (AGA 2024); pouch neoplasia surveillance (American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ASGE) 2015) (7 sources)
- [[toxic-megacolon]] — Colonic dilation (transverse colon >5.5 cm) + systemic toxicity complicating fulminant C. difficile / acute severe ulcerative colitis (UC); medical escalation + colectomy indications (2 sources)
- [[radiation-proctopathy]] — Radiation proctopathy: acute (inflammatory, self-limiting) vs chronic (obliterative endarteritis/telangiectasias); endoscopy dx (biopsy discouraged); argon plasma coagulation (APC)/bipolar/heater probe/radiofrequency ablation (RFA) for chronic bleeding (1 source)
- [[irritable-bowel-syndrome]] — Irritable bowel syndrome (IBS): ADDT — Rome IV/V criteria, positive diagnostic strategy, calprotectin + celiac serology screen, low-fermentable oligosaccharides, disaccharides, monosaccharides, and polyols (FODMAP), soluble fiber, TCA, gut-directed psychotherapy; **IBS with constipation (IBS-C)/IBS-D rebuilt as side-by-side ACG-vs-AGA grade tables** — secretagogues (linaclotide/plecanatide/lubiprostone/tegaserod) plus **tenapanor 50 mg BID**; rifaximin/alosetron/eluxadoline (contraindicated post-cholecystectomy **and >3 alcoholic drinks/day**) and loperamide for IBS-D; AGA suggests against selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors (SSRIs); **two surfaced ACG-vs-AGA conflicts — antispasmodics and polyethylene glycol (PEG)** (6 sources)
- [[colorectal-cancer]] — CRC: TNM staging, surgical resection, adjuvant folinic acid, fluorouracil, oxaliplatin (FOLFOX), total neoadjuvant therapy (TNT) for rectal cancer, immunotherapy for MSI-H, post-resection surveillance (perioperative → 1y → 4y → 9y → q5y), Lynch universal testing, fecal immunochemical test (FIT) screening performance/thresholds, rectal cancer local surveillance; NCCN oncologic mgmt — universal mismatch repair (MMR)/microsatellite instability (MSI), RAS/BRAF/HER2/NTRK/DPYD, adjuvant FOLFOX/capecitabine and oxaliplatin (CAPOX) (IDEA duration), biomarker-/sidedness-directed metastatic therapy; early-T1 en-bloc/ESD/endoscopic full-thickness resection (eFTR) endoscopic resection; treatment-algorithm Mermaid; **malignant large bowel obstruction — bridge-to-surgery self-expandable metal stent (SEMS) vs emergency resection**, with the right-sided outcome table and its three binding qualifiers (severe complications unchanged, very-low Grading of Recommendations Assessment, Development and Evaluation (GRADE)/no RCTs, transverse colon counted as "right") (11 sources)
- [[early-onset-colorectal-cancer]] — Early-onset (young adult–onset) CRC, defined verbatim by the source as **CRC diagnosed at 18 – <50 years of age** — created by the 2026-09-07 cron but left out of this index until 2026-09-08. ADDT: germline multigene panel testing for **every** young-onset case regardless of family history, universal tumor MMR/MSI with BRAF/methylation reflex, the fertility-preservation discussion that must happen **before** chemoradiation, and the surgical-extent decision when a germline variant is found. ⚠ Its source predates the move of average-risk screening to 45 and argues neither side — [[usmstf-2021-crc-screening-age]] and [[acg-2021-crc-screening]] govern the screening-age question; this page governs what to do **once a young adult has CRC** (1 source)
- [[norovirus]] — Norovirus: ADDT — leading cause of epidemic viral gastroenteritis, prominent vomiting, multiplex GI polymerase chain reaction (PCR), supportive care, no antibiotics, outbreak hand-hygiene exception (2 sources)
- [[rotavirus]] — Rotavirus: viral cause of community-acquired diarrhea (no antibiotics), ORS/zinc/ondansetron supportive care, infant vaccine for all (2 sources)
- [[campylobacter-infection]] — Campylobacter: ADDT — most common bacterial enteritis, fluoroquinolone (FQ) resistance → azithromycin (Southeast Asia/India), Guillain-Barré association (2 sources)
- [[salmonella-infection]] — Salmonella (non-typhoidal): ADDT — no antibiotics in immunocompetent (prolongs carriage), treat severe/invasive/high-risk, endovascular-seeding imaging, typhoid distinction (2 sources)
- [[shigellosis]] — Shigella: ADDT — bacillary dysentery, azithromycin first-line, very low inoculum, empiric-therapy exception, no rifaximin (invasive) (2 sources)
- [[enterotoxigenic-e-coli]] — Enterotoxigenic Escherichia coli (ETEC): ADDT — most common traveler's diarrhea pathogen, fluoroquinolone/azithromycin + loperamide, rifaximin appropriate (non-invasive) (2 sources)
- [[shiga-toxin-producing-e-coli]] — Shiga toxin–producing E. coli (STEC/EHEC): ADDT — the bloody diarrhea you must **not** treat (Rec 35: avoid antimicrobials for O157, Shiga-toxin-2 producers, **or unknown genotype**); O157-by-culture vs non-O157-by-toxin-assay testing split, the usually-afebrile presentation and the ~10% of HUS cases without bloody diarrhea, daily platelet-trend monitoring days 1–14, public-health clearance and notifiability (2 sources)
- [[giardiasis]] — Giardia: ADDT — persistent/traveler's diarrhea, malabsorption, chlorine-resistant cysts; stool antigen enzyme immunoassay (EIA)/PCR, duodenal aspirate; tinidazole preferred (3 sources)
- [[cryptosporidiosis]] — Cryptosporidium: ADDT — watery diarrhea, stool antigen/acid-fast/PCR, nitazoxanide, chlorine-resistant oocysts, chronic disease + acquired immunodeficiency syndrome (AIDS) testing in immunocompromised (3 sources)
- [[entamoeba-histolytica-infection]] — E. histolytica: amebic dysentery + liver abscess; antigen EIA/PCR to separate from E. dispar; nitroimidazole then luminal agent (2 sources)
- [[lynch-syndrome]] — Lynch (hereditary nonpolyposis colorectal cancer [HNPCC]): ADDT — MMR variants (MLH1/MSH2/MSH6/PMS2), universal tumor MMR testing + BRAF/methylation reflex, MSI test performance (**specificity ~90%; sensitivity is gene-dependent — 80–91% MLH1/MSH2, 55–77% MSH6/PMS2**), gene-specific CRC risk tables, q1–2y colonoscopy from 20–25 (62–72% mortality reduction), colectomy-with-ileorectal anastomosis (IRA) preferred, endometrial biopsy/transvaginal ultrasound (TVUS) from 30–35, prophylactic hysterectomy/bilateral salpingo-oophorectomy (BSO) at 40–45, aspirin CAPP2 data, pembrolizumab for MSI-H (6 sources)
- [[colon-ischemia]] — Colon ischemia: ADDT — segmental watershed ischemia, CT with IV/oral contrast first-line, colonoscopy within 48h, mild/moderate/severe classification, antibiotics for mod/severe, surgical indications (isolated right colon ischemia (IRCI)/pancolonic/peritoneal signs), post-aortic surgery colon ischemia (CI) (1 source)
- [[familial-adenomatous-polyposis]] — Familial adenomatous polyposis (FAP)/attenuated FAP (AFAP)/MUTYH-associated polyposis (MAP): ADDT — APC vs MUTYH, Spigelman staging table with surveillance intervals, IRA vs. IPAA indications, thyroid/hepatoblastoma surveillance, sulindac/celecoxib chemoprevention, desmoid management; ASGE endoscopic management (clinical-polyposis testing threshold, pouch/IRA intervals, resect polyps >1 cm + antral, ampullary exam/biopsy, sulindac+erlotinib) (2 sources)
- [[peutz-jeghers-syndrome]] — Peutz-Jeghers syndrome (PJS): ADDT — STK11 (large deletions in **up to a third**, not the majority), mucocutaneous pigmentation, 132-fold pancreatic cancer risk, GI surveillance from age 8, **pancreatic surveillance annually from 35 alternating magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography (MRCP)/EUS + fasting blood glucose (FBG)/hemoglobin A1c (HbA1c) (US Multi-Society Task Force on Colorectal Cancer (USMSTF) 2022; the ACG Table 10 vs Rec 23 discrepancy is surfaced)**, intussusception management, sex cord tumor with annular tubules (SCTAT), everolimus (3 sources)
- [[juvenile-polyposis-syndrome]] — Juvenile polyposis syndrome (JPS): ADDT — SMAD4/BMPR1A, hereditary hemorrhagic telangiectasia (HHT) co-association in SMAD4 carriers, colonoscopy + EGD from age 12–15 (ACG 2015 and USMSTF 2022 concordant), surgical thresholds. *(POLD1 guidance removed — it belongs to polymerase-proofreading-associated polyposis, not JPS)* (2 sources)
- [[cowden-syndrome]] — Cowden/PTEN Hamartoma: ADDT — PTEN, major/minor criteria, glycogenic acanthosis pathognomonic, colonoscopy from age 35 (USMSTF 2022 supersedes ACG 2015 age 15) **repeating at ≤5-year intervals**, or 10 y younger than any relative with CRC; breast surveillance q6–12 mo clinical exam from 25; multi-organ surveillance schedule (2 sources)
- [[serrated-polyposis-syndrome]] — Serrated polyposis syndrome (SPS): ADDT — World Health Organization (WHO) criteria, no proven germline etiology, colonoscopy q1–3y, first-degree relative (FDR) surveillance (3 sources)
- [[mutyh-associated-polyposis]] — MAP: ADDT — biallelic MUTYH, attenuated polyposis phenotype; CRC risk 19% by 50 / 43% by 60, and 35% present with CRC without concurrent polyps; colonoscopy **q1–2y** (not annual); upper-GI surveillance from **30–35**; Rec 9 colectomy criteria (absolute vs relative, incl. adenomas >6 mm); Spigelman staging via FAP (2 sources)
- [[bmmrd-syndrome]] — Biallelic mismatch repair deficiency (BMMRD)/constitutional mismatch repair deficiency (CMMRD): ADDT — biallelic MMR germline mutations (autosomal recessive); childhood CRC (median age 16), brain tumors (70% penetrance, median age 9), lymphoma; café-au-lait macules mimicking neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1); PMS2 most common; annual colonoscopy from age 6, brain magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) q6mo from age 2; subtotal colectomy preferred; 8 USMSTF recommendations (all weak, low evidence) (1 source)
- [[hereditary-mixed-polyposis-syndrome]] — Hereditary mixed polyposis syndrome (HMPS): rare autosomal dominant attenuated colonic polyposis defined by its **mixed polyp phenotype** (characteristic admixed juvenile–adenomatous lesion), originally described in a large Ashkenazi Jewish kindred; **no formal diagnostic criteria exist**; *GREM1* promoter/whole-gene duplication → ↑GREM1 → bone morphogenetic protein (BMP)-pathway inhibition (15q13.3–q14 / CRAC1 locus); most families' genetic basis unknown. **Decision gap — no lifetime CRC risk, penetrance, or surveillance interval in either source** (2 sources)
- [[microscopic-colitis]] — Microscopic colitis: collagenous vs lymphocytic; ~10% of chronic-diarrhea referrals; normal-appearing mucosa → random right+left biopsies required (patchy; sigmoidoscopy alone can miss); who to scope (age >60, female, intense diarrhea) and why Rome criteria can't exclude it (32.5% overlap with IBS-D); nonresponsive-celiac workup; full AGA therapy — budesonide 9 mg induction with an 8-week stop, 6 mg maintenance for relapsers, mesalamine/bismuth/prednisolone alternatives, and the agents recommended against; refractory workup (NSAID/PPI/SSRI triggers, coexisting celiac/IBS). Treatment is identical for both subtypes; histologic thresholds not defined by the sources (5 sources)
- [[immune-checkpoint-inhibitor-colitis]] — Immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) enterocolitis: ADDT — the most common GI toxicity of checkpoint blockade (up to 40%; severe 2–5% PD-1/PD-L1 vs ~10% CTLA-4); **symptoms don't track with disease** — Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) grade is explicitly *not* predictive, colonic ulceration is the only established predictor of needing second-line therapy; stool lactoferrin/calprotectin triage (sens up to 90%); sigmoidoscopy usually adequate (95% left-colon involvement), EGD before full colonoscopy if negative; 20–30% have no inflammation at all; steroids 0.5–2 mg/kg → infliximab 5 mg/kg or vedolizumab 300 mg at weeks 0/2/6 (switch class on non-response, no washout); budesonide fails as prophylaxis but works for ICI microscopic colitis; retreatment risk ~30%; IBD patients 41% vs 11% GI immune-related adverse event (irAE) rate (1 source)
- [[clostridioides-difficile]] — Clostridioides difficile infection (CDI): ADDT — two-step testing (nucleic acid amplification test (NAAT)/glutamate dehydrogenase (GDH) + toxin EIA, treat discordant if suspicion high), severity thresholds (white blood cell count [WBC] >15k; Cr >1.5 mg/dL or >1.5× baseline; +shock/ileus/megacolon = fulminant), fidaxomicin-preferred first-line with vancomycin/metronidazole rules and doses, day-3–5 response rule, recurrence taper-pulse and EXTEND regimens, microbiota therapy triggers and high-risk criteria, fulminant triple therapy + fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT), suppressive vancomycin, oral vancomycin prophylaxis (OVP)/probiotics not advised, household prevention; IBD-specific guidance; SER-109 (ECOSPOR III 12% vs 40%) (5 sources)
- [[chronic-idiopathic-constipation]] — Chronic idiopathic constipation (CIC): ADDT — Rome IV criteria, over-the-counter (OTC) step (PEG/bisacodyl/fiber), Rx secretagogues (linaclotide/plecanatide/prucalopride) when OTC fails, lubiprostone conditional, exclude IBS-C/defecation disorder; refractory-constipation pathway (anorectal manometry (ARM)+balloon expulsion test (BET), transit testing, off-label agents, surgery as last resort) (5 sources)
- [[opioid-induced-constipation]] — Opioid-induced constipation (OIC): ADDT — Rome IV criteria (new/worsening constipation on initiating, changing or escalating an opioid + ≥2 of 6 features); Bowel Function Index (BFI; 3 items, 0–100 each, mean = total) with **BFI ≥30** as the escalation cutoff; "inadequate laxative response" = moderate/severe symptoms despite ≥1 laxative class for ≥4 days over 2 weeks; laxatives first-line for all (Strong/Moderate) run **scheduled, ≥2 classes** before failure can be called; peripherally acting μ-opioid receptor antagonists (PAMORAs) for laxative-refractory disease — naldemedine Strong/High (the only 52-week safety data), naloxegol Strong/Moderate, methylnaltrexone **Conditional**/Low, with the pooled RRs; lubiprostone and prucalopride carry AGA's formal **"no recommendation"** (an evidence gap, not a recommendation against), with the trial regimens that failed; avoid PAMORAs where the blood–brain barrier is compromised (3 sources)
- [[defecation-disorders]] — Defecation disorders/dyssynergia: ADDT — ARM + BET **both** required for diagnosis (BET sens/spec ~88%, positive predictive value (PPV) 64%, **negative predictive value (NPV) 97%** — a normal BET largely excludes dyssynergic defecation (DD)), digital rectal examination (DRE) 75% sensitive for dyssynergia, biofeedback cornerstone (Strong/Moderate), slow-transit secondary in 50%, sacral nerve stimulation (SNS) ineffective. Test mechanics live on [[anorectal-manometry]] (3 sources)
- [[hemorrhoids]] — Hemorrhoids: ADDT — Grade I–IV classification, fiber/fluid conservative, rubber band ligation preferred office treatment (Strong/Moderate), thrombosed external → excision within 4 days, Grade IV → hemorrhoidectomy; special populations (IBD/pregnancy/cirrhosis-vs-rectal-varices) (3 sources)
- [[anal-fissure]] — Anal fissure: ADDT — topical calcium channel blocker (CCB) first-line (67–90% healing, Strong/Low), Botox for CCB failure, lateral internal sphincterotomy gold standard for refractory (94–98% healing, Strong/High), lateral fissures → Crohn's/human immunodeficiency virus (HIV)/malignancy (3 sources)
- [[fecal-incontinence]] — Fecal incontinence: ADDT — urge vs. passive subtypes, loperamide/biofeedback first-line, sacral nerve stimulation for moderate-severe failures (Strong/Low), sphincteroplasty for acute sphincter injury, end stoma as last resort (3 sources)
- [[proctalgia-syndromes]] — Proctalgia syndromes: ADDT — levator syndrome (≥20 min pain + levator tenderness) vs. proctalgia fugax (PF; <20 min); biofeedback for levator (Strong/Very Low); reassurance only for PF (2 sources)
- [[acute-colonic-pseudo-obstruction]] — Acute colonic pseudo-obstruction (ACPO) / Ogilvie's syndrome: ADDT — colonic dilatation without mechanical obstruction; exclude obstruction by CT; cecal Ø >10–12 cm = perforation risk; conservative ×72h → neostigmine 2 mg IV (cardiac monitoring) → endoscopic decompression; surgery for perforation/peritonitis (1 source)
- [[colonic-volvulus]] — Colonic volvulus: ADDT — mechanical torsion (sigmoid > cecal); CT preferred; sigmoid → endoscopic detorsion + index-admission surgical consult (84–86% recurrence); cecal → surgery-first; perforation/peritonitis → surgery (1 source)


- [[mesenteric-artery-aneurysm]] — Mesenteric/splanchnic artery aneurysm: **splenic artery is the most common site (~60%)**, hepatic next; true vs pseudoaneurysm (pseudoaneurysms rupture more, and follow pancreatitis); computed tomography angiography (CTA) to characterize; **<2 cm asymptomatic → observe; >2 cm → treat even if asymptomatic**, plus the four treat-regardless-of-size settings (ACG Recs 19/20); post-repair imaging (1 source)
- [[fammm-syndrome]] — Familial atypical multiple mole melanoma (CDKN2A) and pancreatic cancer risk (2 sources)
- [[postinfectious-ibs]] — Postinfectious IBS (PI-IBS): IBS after acute infectious gastroenteritis; ADDT order; risk rises with acute-illness severity; DGBI framework link (4 sources)
- [[angioectasia]] — Angioectasia: most common small bowel bleeding (SBB) cause, arteriovenous malformation (AVM) dilations, association with aortic stenosis (Heyde's), endoscopic treatment (argon plasma coagulation) (1 source)
- [[heydes-syndrome]] — Heyde's syndrome: aortic stenosis + angioectasia + acquired von Willebrand factor (vWF) deficiency, left ventricular assist device (LVAD) parallel, aortic valve replacement (AVR) resolves coagulopathy (1 source)

---

### Hepatopancreaticobiliary Diseases

- [[ampullary-adenoma]] — Ampullary & nonampullary duodenal adenomas: ADDT — side-viewing scope + biopsy, EUS/endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography (ERCP) staging, endoscopic papillectomy (pancreatic duct [PD] stent + rectal NSAID prophylaxis), duodenal EMR, sporadic vs FAP/Peutz-Jeghers, post-resection surveillance (2 sources)
- [[cholangiocarcinoma]] — Cholangiocarcinoma: intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma (iCCA)/perihilar cholangiocarcinoma (pCCA)/distal cholangiocarcinoma (dCCA) — primary sclerosing cholangitis (PSC) risk, MRCP/EUS-guided fine-needle aspiration (EUS-FNA)/fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH), combined-modality tissue acquisition sensitivities, transplant needle-track-seeding caution, Mayo neoadjuvant liver transplantation (LT) protocol for pCCA, gem/cis first-line; NCCN oncologic mgmt — RNA-based molecular profiling (FGFR2/IDH1/HER2/BRAF/NTRK/RET/NRG1/MSI-H), gem+cis+checkpoint inhibitor 1st-line, adjuvant capecitabine (BILCAP), iCCA transplant exception (3 sources)
- [[gallbladder-cancer]] — Gallbladder cancer: most common biliary tract malignancy; often incidental post-cholecystectomy; **American Joint Committee on Cancer (AJCC) 8th-ed T/N/M table + stage groups now on-page, with the re-resection trigger and its criteria (the previously flagged decision gap — closed)**; imaging performance; neoadjuvant for advanced disease; adjuvant capecitabine; gem+cis+checkpoint 1st-line; HER2/BRAF/NTRK/RET/MSI-H profiling; gallbladder-polyp risk stratification (incl. the "other risk factors" definition) + cholecystectomy/surveillance thresholds (2 sources)
- [[pancreatic-cancer]] — Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma: ADDT — diagnosis, genetic-risk syndromes, high-risk surveillance (EUS/MRI), therapeutics (7 sources)
- [[familial-pancreatic-cancer]] — Familial pancreatic cancer (FPC) kindreds: ADDT — definition, inheritance, who/when to screen; **risk magnitude now on-page — 1–2 affected FDRs 4–7×, ≥3 FDRs 17–32×, 32% lifetime risk by age 85 (PancPRO)**; gene-panel role; ~2-fold risk for non-FPC family history; links [[pancreatic-cancer-screening]] for modality/interval rather than duplicating it (2 sources)
- [[autoimmune-pancreatitis]] — Autoimmune pancreatitis (AIP; type 1, immunoglobulin G4 [IgG4]-related) vs idiopathic duct-centric pancreatitis (IDCP; type 2): ADDT — the disease's whole problem is that it **mimics pancreatic cancer** (2–3% of resections for presumed pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC)); phenotype table, the five International Consensus Diagnostic Criteria (ICDC) cardinal features, serum IgG4 **≥2× upper limit of normal (ULN) = ICDC level 1 but only 53% sensitive** (99% specific — only 1% of PDAC exceeds it), IgG4 useless in IDCP and useless post-treatment; fine-needle biopsy (FNB) rather than fine-needle aspiration (FNA) (architecture needed: granulocyte epithelial lesion in IDCP, obliterative phlebitis in AIP); ductal narrowing *without* upstream dilatation; **steroids not stents** for mild uninfected jaundice; relapse 60% (AIP) vs <10% (IDCP) drives maintenance; **Figure 1 management algorithm now on the page** as a flowchart — prednisone 40 mg/day × 4 weeks, taper 5 mg/week, non-response → reconsider the diagnosis, and the four maintenance/relapse regimens. ⚠ **Not a clinical practice update (CPU) — an AGA expert column with no numbered best practice advice statements (BPAs)**; immunomodulator/rituximab doses and the maintenance duration are still not in the source (1 source)
- [[hereditary-pancreatitis]] — Hereditary pancreatitis: ADDT — PRSS1/SPINK1/CFTR/CTRC/CPA1/CEL/CASR/CLDN2 gene table (inheritance, mechanism, phenotype); genetic testing (Strong/Low, esp. <35 y) + minimum panel; **53-fold** pancreatic-cancer RR; surveillance from **age 40** (flat — no 10-yr-earlier modifier), annual EUS/MRI (4 sources)

- [[choledocholithiasis]] — Choledocholithiasis: most common cause of obstructive jaundice; ASGE 2019 high/intermediate/low criteria (stone on imaging, bili >4 + dilated duct, cholangitis = high; abnormal liver function tests (LFTs), age >55, dilated CBD = intermediate); high → ERCP, intermediate → MRCP/EUS; large-balloon dilation for large stones; Charcot's triad = cholangitis → biliary drainage <48 h (urgent <24 h only for severe — though even in Tokyo Guidelines (TG) grade III septic shock <24 h showed no survival benefit in a 2026 Digestive Disease Week (DDW) abstract; no benefit + more bleeding rushing mild-moderate) (5 sources)
- [[drug-induced-liver-injury]] — Idiosyncratic drug-induced liver injury (DILI): ADDT — diagnosis of exclusion, R-value classification (hepatocellular/cholestatic/mixed), Roussel Uclaf Causality Assessment Method (RUCAM) causality, Hy's Law (3 criteria) ~10% mortality, prognostic thresholds (Model for End-Stage Liver Disease (MELD) ≥19, nR-Hy's Law, Ghabril model), N-acetylcysteine (NAC) for early DILI-associated acute liver failure (DILI-ALF) (Strong/Low), against rechallenge, methotrexate/isoniazid monitoring thresholds, herbal and dietary supplements (HDS) second most common cause, ICI hepatotoxicity (3 sources)
- [[antibody-mediated-rejection-liver-transplant]] — Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) after liver transplant: ADDT — acute AMR (unexplained graft dysfunction + thrombocytopenia + hypocomplementemia; donor-specific antibodies (DSA) + biopsy with C4d >10%) vs chronic AMR; American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases–American Society of Transplantation (AASLD-AST) Recs 47–55 with grades; steroids/augmented immunosuppression (IS) first, intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIG) + plasma exchange ± anti-CD20 if refractory; retransplant for graft failure (1 source)
- [[hepatopulmonary-syndrome-portopulmonary-hypertension]] — Hepatopulmonary syndrome (HPS) and portopulmonary hypertension (POPH): ADDT — bubble echocardiography as most sensitive HPS screen; right heart catheterization (RHC) required when right ventricular systolic pressure (RVSP) >45 mm Hg or right ventricular (RV) abnormality; POPH mean pulmonary artery pressure (mPAP) 45 mm Hg + pulmonary vascular resistance (PVR) >3 WU on optimized therapy = LT contraindication; POPH MELD-exception criteria (mPAP <35 + PVR <5 WU, or mPAP 35–45 + PVR <3 WU); **HPS severity bands (PaO₂ cutoffs) absent from ingested sources** (3 sources)
- [[alcohol-associated-liver-disease]] — Alcohol-associated liver disease (ALD) spectrum: definite/probable/possible alcohol-associated hepatitis (AH) consensus criteria (American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases (AASLD) 2020); Maddrey discriminant function (MDF) ≥32 and MELD ≥20 severity thresholds; prednisolone 40 mg × 28d (STOPAH/individual patient data (IPD) meta-analysis); Lille score at day 7; IV NAC combination (potential benefit); pentoxifylline abandoned (AASLD 2020 guidance statement [GS] 19); acute kidney injury (AKI) >2.5 mg/dL = relative contraindication; early LT for severe AH (Mathurin + Lee multicenter data); no fixed abstinence interval; baclofen/acamprosate for alcohol use disorder (AUD) in ALD; phosphatidylethanol (PEth)/ethyl glucuronide (EtG) biomarkers; PNPLA3/TM6SF2/HSD17B13 genetics (8 sources)
- [[alcohol-associated-hepatitis]] — Alcohol-associated hepatitis (AH): ADDT — acute jaundice and liver failure on a background of heavy alcohol use, held separately from the [[alcohol-associated-liver-disease]] spectrum page, which keeps the score-comparison table; carries the AH-specific decisions — definite/probable/possible diagnostic criteria, corticosteroid eligibility and the Lille day-7 stop rule, infection and AKI as the contraindications that actually bite, and early liver transplantation (3 sources)
- [[acute-pancreatitis]] — 2-of-3 diagnosis, Revised Atlanta severity + ACG 2024 clinical organ-failure criteria (systolic blood pressure <90, PaO₂ <60, Cr >2 after rehydration, GI bleed >500 mL/24 h), **ACG 2024 fluid rates — no more than 1.5 mL/kg/h without hypovolemia, 10 mL/kg bolus if hypovolemic (the old 250–500 mL/h was superseded ACG 2013)**, early enteral nutrition, **ERCP Rec 5 — medical therapy *over* early ERCP in biliary acute pancreatitis (AP) without cholangitis (conditional/low)**, **against FNA of suspected infected necrosis (Rec 9)**, necrotizing AP step-up approach, ACG 2024 Table 4, endoscopic pancreatic fluid collection (PFC)/pseudocyst/walled-off necrosis (WON) drainage indications; idiopathic-AP/sphincter of Oddi dysfunction (SOD) framework (no diagnostic ERCP for single episode; EUS >40 y; treat SOD type 1/2, never scope type 3); moderate goal-directed lactated Ringer's (LR) resuscitation (WATERFALL RCT) (8 sources)
- [[hepatocellular-adenoma]] — 4 molecular subtypes; resect if >5cm/male/beta-catenin/pre-pregnancy; gadoxetate MRI; oral contraceptive pill (OCP) discontinuation (1 source)
- [[focal-nodular-hyperplasia]] — No malignant potential; gadoxetate uptake in hepatobiliary phase (key vs. hepatocellular adenoma (HCA)); no follow-up once confirmed (1 source)
- [[hepatic-hemangioma]] — Most common benign liver lesion; T2 bright + centripetal fill-in; no follow-up if <3cm and characteristic imaging (1 source)
- [[hepatic-cysts]] — Simple cysts: benign, no follow-up; complex cysts → MRI; polycystic liver disease: somatostatin analogues (1 source)
- [[nutrition-in-liver-disease]] — Malnutrition/nutrition in liver disease: cirrhosis assessment, hepatic encephalopathy (HE) protein strategy, branched-chain amino acids (BCAAs), late evening snacks, vitamin E (metabolic dysfunction–associated steatohepatitis (MASH)) (2 sources)
- [[chronic-hepatitis-b]] — Chronic hepatitis B (CHB): 5 phases (immune-tolerant/active/inactive/indeterminate/hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg)-loss); entecavir (ETV)/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (TDF)/tenofovir alafenamide (TAF); treat immune-tolerant >40 or ≥F2; treat indeterminate via shared decision-making (SDM); continue nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA) until HBsAg loss; expanded hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) surveillance for hepatitis D virus (HDV)/HIV co-infection and post-HBsAg loss (4 sources)
- [[hepatitis-d]] — Hepatitis D (Delta): defective RNA virus requiring hepatitis B virus (HBV); most aggressive viral hepatitis; universal anti-HDV screening of HBV patients → reflex HDV RNA; vibration-controlled transient elastography (VCTE)/Fibrosis-4 index (FIB-4) + HCC surveillance; pegylated interferon-α (peg-IFN-α) (only US therapy) and bulevirtide/pipeline agents (1 source)
- [[acute-liver-failure]] — Acute liver failure (ALF): ADDT — O'Grady classification, international normalized ratio (INR)/encephalopathy criteria, NAC for non-acetaminophen (APAP) early coma, continuous renal replacement therapy (CRRT) for ammonia, no routine coagulopathy correction, no prophylactic antibiotics, King's College Criteria (KCC)/MELD transplant criteria; ALL ALF → urgent LT center referral before formal criteria met per AASLD-AST 2025; **SURFASA now computable** — formula, the −0.9 cut-off and all four interpretation bands for calling corticosteroid failure in acute severe AIH at day 3 (5 sources)
- [[aki-in-cirrhosis]] — AKI in Cirrhosis/hepatorenal syndrome–AKI (HRS-AKI): ADDT — International Club of Ascites–AKI (ICA-AKI) staging, HRS-AKI vs. acute tubular necrosis (ATN) distinction, fractional excretion of urea (FEUrea) preferred over fractional excretion of sodium (FENa), terlipressin dosing/contraindications, renal replacement therapy (RRT) as bridge to transplant only, MELD paradox (4 sources)
- [[chronic-pancreatitis]] — Chronic pancreatitis: ADDT — Mechanistic vs. clinicopathologic definition; CT/MRI first-line then EUS/secretin-enhanced MRCP (sMRCP)/histology; TIGAR-O v2.0; M-ANNHEIM scoring; PRSS1/SPINK1/CFTR/CTRC genetics; surgery > endoscopy for obstructive CP (Cahen RCT); main pancreatic duct (MPD) stone/stricture endotherapy thresholds, fully covered self-expandable metal stent (FCSEMS) vs plastic; pancreatic enzyme replacement therapy (PERT) for exocrine pancreatic insufficiency (EPI) only (not pain); antioxidants/celiac plexus block; alcohol + smoking cessation (Strong); no routine cancer screening (5 sources)
- [[pancreas-divisum]] — Pancreas divisum: ADDT — most common congenital pancreatic anomaly (~6–10%); dorsal-duct drainage via minor papilla; minor papilla endotherapy only for recurrent acute pancreatitis (RAP) with objective outflow obstruction (dilated dorsal duct/santorinicele), no role for pain alone; post-ERCP pancreatitis (PEP) 10–15%, stenosis risk (AGA 2022) (1 source)
- [[exocrine-pancreatic-insufficiency]] — EPI: ADDT — suspect (CP/recurrent AP/PDAC/cystic fibrosis (CF)/pancreatic surgery) vs. consider (celiac/Crohn's/intestinal surgery/diabetes/Zollinger-Ellison syndrome (ZES)); fecal elastase-1 initial test (<100 µg/g = EPI, 100–200 indeterminate, valid on PERT); imaging cannot diagnose; PERT ≥40,000 USP lipase/meal with meals + H2/PPI for non-enteric-coated; fat-soluble vitamins + dual-energy x-ray absorptiometry (DXA) q1–2y (1 source)
- [[autoimmune-hepatitis]] — Autoimmune hepatitis (AIH): ADDT — simplified score (antinuclear antibody (ANA)/smooth muscle antibody (SMA)/anti–liver kidney microsomal type 1 (anti-LKM1)/immunoglobulin G (IgG)/histology), type 1 vs. 2, budesonide contraindicated in cirrhosis/acute severe AIH, mycophenolate mofetil (MMF) preferred second-line over tacrolimus (TAC), acute severe AIH/ALF → LT, withdrawal after ≥2y remission (50–87% relapse), AIH–primary biliary cholangitis (PBC)/PSC overlap (Paris criteria), anti–soluble liver antigen (anti-SLA)=never withdraw, post-LT recurrence 36–68% at 5y; acute severe AIH — day-3 SURFASA call beside AASLD's 1–2 week rule, transaminases do **not** measure response, autoantibodies absent in ~40–50% so histology anchors it (5 sources)
- [[hepatocellular-carcinoma]] — HCC: Liver Imaging Reporting and Data System (LI-RADS) non-invasive diagnosis (LR-1→LR-5/LR-M/LR-TIV), Barcelona Clinic Liver Cancer (BCLC) 2022 staging table, surveillance criteria (Table 1: cirrhosis/non-cirrhotic HBV/PAGE-B), recall algorithm, transarterial chemoembolization (TACE)/transarterial radioembolization (TARE) locoregional ladder with unsuitability/refractoriness criteria, systemic therapy dosing (atezo+bev, durvalumab+tremelimumab, sorafenib 400 mg BID, lenvatinib 12/8 mg once daily (QD), regorafenib, cabozantinib, ramucirumab), adjuvant atezo+bev contraindicated post-resection/ablation (AASLD 2025 GS32R, Strong Level 1; IMbrave050 second-interim HR=0.90 non-significant), transplant criteria (Milan/University of California, San Francisco (UCSF)/Up-to-seven/Extended Toronto/Kyoto/United Network for Organ Sharing downstaging (UNOS-DS); alpha-fetoprotein (AFP) thresholds), post-LT mammalian target of rapamycin (mTOR) IS (within-Milan), recurrence management; NCCN 2026 first-line tiering (ipilimumab+nivolumab elevated to Preferred category 1 per CheckMate-9DW; atezo+bev; durvalumab+tremelimumab), strengthened external beam radiation therapy (EBRT)/stereotactic body radiation therapy (SBRT) role; emerging-biomarker caveats (GALAD/multicancer detection (MCD) panels, PAGE-B/REAL-B) per AGA 2026; **RETREAT now computable** — full point table, the viable-tumour counting rule, and the derivation authors' post-LT surveillance schedule by score (9 sources)
- [[immune-checkpoint-inhibitor-hepatitis]] — ICI hepatitis: onset 4–12 weeks; incidence <5% monotherapy vs **~25% with ipilimumab+nivolumab**; **all four CTCAE grades now defined** (grade 4 = aspartate aminotransferase (AST)/alanine aminotransferase (ALT) >20× ULN, bili >10× ULN, or decompensation — gap closed 2026-09-04); grade-directed management with methylprednisolone named and dosed, 3–5 day non-response rule, 4–6 week taper; full alternative-etiology workup and the alkaline phosphatase (ALP)/bilirubin → biliary imaging rule; **infliximab cautioned here but first-line in [[immune-checkpoint-inhibitor-colitis|ICI colitis]]** (3 sources)
- [[spontaneous-bacterial-peritonitis]] — Spontaneous bacterial peritonitis (SBP): ADDT — polymorphonuclear leukocyte (PMN) ≥250/mm³ threshold, culture-negative SBP, bacterascites distinction, cefotaxime community vs. pip-tazo nosocomial, albumin 1.5+1.0 g/kg mandatory, ciprofloxacin prophylaxis (norfloxacin unavailable in US) (6 sources)
- [[hepatic-hydrothorax]] — Hepatic hydrothorax (HH): ADDT — transudative pleural effusion in [[portal-hypertension]] with no cardiopulmonary or malignant cause, from ascitic fluid crossing diaphragmatic defects; pleural fluid criteria separating it from empyema and malignancy, spontaneous bacterial empyema as its own entity with its own PMN threshold, and the therapeutic ladder ending at TIPS or transplant — with chest tubes as the thing not to do (5 sources)
- [[primary-sclerosing-cholangitis]] — PSC: ADDT — 3D MRI/MRCP dx (avoid ERCP for dx), IgG4 testing, liver stiffness (LS) by transient elastography (TE)/magnetic resonance elastography (MRE) for fibrosis staging, ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) 13–23 mg/kg/d conditional (never ≥28), annual MRI/MRCP ± carbohydrate antigen (CA) 19-9 cholangiocarcinoma (CCA) surveillance (not <18y/small-duct), FISH for relevant strictures, HD colonoscopy q1–2y from age 15 in PSC-IBD, LT for end-stage liver disease (ESLD)/recurrent cholangitis/early CCA, recurrent PSC (rPSC) 10–37% (4 sources)
- [[igg4-related-cholangitis]] — IgG4-related sclerosing cholangitis (IgG4-SC): the one sclerosing cholangitis that responds to corticosteroids, so it must be separated from PSC and from CCA — diagnostic triad (biliary stricture + IgG4-positive plasma cells in tissue + serum IgG4 elevation, with frequent pancreatic involvement), the three guideline triggers to send a serum IgG4 (possible PSC, established PSC at dx, cholestatic ALP elevation, biliary-cancer workup), stricture level/pancreatic/other-organ involvement as the features carrying management. **Clinical half completed 2026-10-12** — IgG4-SC vs PSC vs CCA comparison, the three serum-IgG4 thresholds each labelled with the question it answers (and the ACG-vs-AASLD disagreement on "marked elevation"), the ASGE indeterminate-stricture tissue-acquisition pathway, and the steroid regimen with its biliary branches (6 sources)
- [[pancreatic-cysts]] — Pancreatic cysts: ADDT — all 6 cyst types (pseudocyst/serous cystadenoma (SCA)/side-branch intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasm (SB-IPMN)/main-duct IPMN (MD-IPMN)/mucinous cystic neoplasm (MCN)/solid pseudopapillary neoplasm (SPN)), high-risk features, size-stratified surveillance table, cyst fluid analysis (carcinoembryonic antigen (CEA)/amylase/cytology), stop-surveillance thresholds (3 sources)
- [[hereditary-hemochromatosis]] — Hereditary hemochromatosis (HH): ADDT — HFE C282Y homozygote most common (Types 1–4), transferrin saturation (TS)+serum ferritin (SF) algorithm, HFE genotyping, liver biopsy if SF >1000 or LFT↑, phlebotomy to SF <50, HCC surveillance post-cirrhosis (3 sources)
- [[wilson-disease]] — Wilson disease: ADDT — ATP7B, Leipzig score (≥4 = Wilson disease (WD) highly likely), New Wilson Index (NWI) prognosis, Kayser-Fleischer (KF) rings, ceruloplasmin/24h urine copper/liver biopsy, chelation (D-penicillamine/trientine tetrahydrochloride), zinc, monitoring targets, decompensated cirrhosis combination therapy, ALF→immediate LT, HCC surveillance, pregnancy (reduce chelator dose; zinc safe) (3 sources)
- [[primary-biliary-cholangitis]] — PBC: ADDT — antimitochondrial antibody (AMA) 95% sensitivity; 2-of-3 diagnostic criteria; AMA-negative PBC (sp100/gp210); UDCA 13–15 mg/kg/day; biochemical response criteria (Paris I/II, Rotterdam, Toronto, Barcelona) at 12 months; obeticholic acid (OCA) 5→10 mg/day second-line (contraindicated in advanced cirrhosis per 2021 FDA warning); fibrates off-label second-line (bezafibrate/fenofibrate); pruritus ladder (resins→rifampicin→naltrexone→sertraline); Paris criteria for PBC-AIH overlap; GLOBE/UK-PBC prognostic scores; HCC surveillance; LT at MELD >14; recurrent PBC post-LT (20–50% at 10–20 years) (6 sources)
- [[nafld-masld]] — Nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD)/metabolic dysfunction–associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD): steatotic liver disease (SLD) nomenclature + 5-item cardiometabolic risk factor (CMRF) criteria + MASLD with increased alcohol intake (MetALD) ranges (Delphi 2023); 29 AASLD guidance statements; two-tier noninvasive test (NIT) pathway (FIB-4 rule-out <1.3 / rule-in >2.67, age caveats → VCTE/MRE/Enhanced Liver Fibrosis (ELF)) with diagnostic vs F2–F3 candidacy thresholds; blood-NIT confounders + "rule-out not rule-in"; statins safe (GS 1); weight-loss dose-response; bariatric; resmetirom FDA 2024 (thyroid hormone receptor (THR)-β, dosing/drug–drug interaction (DDI)/futility); semaglutide FDA Aug 2025 (ESSENCE); pioglitazone/vitamin E; post-LT recurrence (10 sources)
- [[lean-nafld]] — Lean NAFLD / Lean MASLD: ADDT — **lean NAFLD = NAFLD with body mass index (BMI) <25 kg/m² (non-Asian) or <23 kg/m² (Asian race)**, the race-based cutoff being the whole diagnostic hinge; **7%–20% of NAFLD is lean** (≈8–10 million US adults; ~6.8% of biopsy-confirmed nonalcoholic steatohepatitis (NASH) — older, more often Asian, fewer metabolic-syndrome components but **similar advanced-fibrosis risk**; National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) III 10.8%). **Lean is not a benign phenotype** — cross-sectionally less advanced fibrosis and [[cirrhosis]] than obese NAFLD, yet a **higher risk of liver-related death** at median 8.4 y. 15 numbered BPA from [[aga-2022-lean-nafld]], **ungraded**, with the lean-specific NIT workup, secondary-cause exclusion, and the lower **3%–5%** weight-loss target that separates it from [[nafld-masld]] (3 sources)
- [[liver-transplantation]] — Liver transplantation: candidate evaluation (MELD ≥12 referral, contraindications, cardiac/pulmonary/psychosocial workup, HCC/CCA criteria, ALD no fixed abstinence exclusion); graft complications (machine perfusion, vascular/biliary complications, T-cell–mediated rejection (TCMR)/PCR/AMR, IS trough targets 2025, no protocol biopsy Rec 34, mTOR for HCC/chronic kidney disease (CKD)); non-graft complications (CMV pre-emptive PCR, hydrophilic statins, glucagon-like peptide-1 receptor agonist (GLP-1RA) post-LT, cancer surveillance, bone/reproductive health); IS minimization (5 sources)
- [[hepatitis-c]] — Hepatitis C: ADDT — universal screening (adults ≥18/all pregnancies), simplified algorithm (G/P 8wk or sofosbuvir (SOF)/velpatasvir (VEL) 12wk pangenotypic), decompensated cirrhosis regimens (SOF/VEL ± ribavirin (RBV); NS3/4A contraindicated), retreatment (SOF/VEL/voxilaprevir (VOX)), incomplete adherence algorithm, post-transplant direct-acting antiviral (DAA), HIV/hepatitis C virus (HCV) coinfection, post-sustained virologic response (SVR) HCC surveillance (3 sources)
- [[hepatitis-c-in-pregnancy]] — HCV in pregnancy: vertical transmission, universal screening, case-by-case DAA consideration, breastfeeding (4 sources — see [[hepatitis-c]] for full treatment context)
- [[intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy]] — Intrahepatic cholestasis of pregnancy (ICP): ADDT — pruritus + serum bile acids >10 μmol/L (dx), UDCA 10–15 mg/kg/d, bile-acid-stratified delivery timing (≥100 → 35–36 wk); leading cause of pregnancy pruritus (2 sources)
- [[acute-fatty-liver-of-pregnancy]] — Acute fatty liver of pregnancy (AFLP): ADDT — rare 3rd-trimester microvesicular steatosis/liver failure; Swansea criteria; prompt delivery + transplant evaluation; differential diagnosis (DDx) vs hemolysis, elevated liver enzymes, low platelets (HELLP) (2 sources)
- [[cirrhosis]] — Cirrhosis: ADDT — F4 fibrosis; noninvasive liver disease assessment (NILDA)-first diagnosis (biopsy imperfect reference, area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) ceiling ~0.9); compensated vs decompensated; severity scores compared (Child-Turcotte-Pugh (CTP)/MELD-Na/Mayo/VOCAL-Penn variable table) plus a **MELD-variant comparison — original MELD vs MELD-Na vs MELD 3.0**, which decision uses which and which are computable from the corpus (the canonical formula here is **MELD 3.0**, previously mislabelled MELD-Na); low-risk band MELD 6–9 + CTP 5–6 without clinically significant portal hypertension (CSPH); acute-on-chronic liver failure (ACLF) scores over MELD in critical illness; complication map; treat the underlying disease; nutrition/frailty/sarcopenia; hemostasis + periprocedural; perioperative risk; palliative care; transplant referral (13 sources)
- [[portal-hypertension]] — Portal hypertension: ADDT — hepatic venous pressure gradient (HVPG) (gold standard; ≥16 non-hepatic surgery risk, >20 pre-emptive transjugular intrahepatic portosystemic shunt (TIPS)), compensated advanced chronic liver disease (cACLD)/CSPH noninvasive staging ("Rule of Five" figure + spleen-stiffness cutoffs), carvedilol preferred nonselective beta-blocker (NSBB) (6.25–12.5 mg/day), primary prophylaxis (NSBB or endoscopic variceal ligation (EVL)), acute variceal hemorrhage (AVH) management (preemptive TIPS for CTP B>7/C 10–13), recompensation criteria, secondary prophylaxis, gastric/ectopic varices (endoscopic cyanoacrylate injection (ECI)/TIPS/balloon-occluded retrograde transvenous obliteration (BRTO)), portal hypertensive gastropathy (PHG) management (8 sources)
- [[portal-vein-thrombosis]] — Portal vein thrombosis (PVT)/MVT: ADDT — noncirrhotic (thrombophilia workup: JAK2/factor V Leiden (FVL)/antiphospholipid syndrome (APLS)) vs. cirrhotic PVT, anticoagulate all acute symptomatic noncirrhotic + complete/extending cirrhotic PVT (Strong/Low), low-molecular-weight heparin (LMWH)/vitamin K antagonist (VKA)/direct oral anticoagulant (DOAC), NSBBs + anticoagulation (AC) for variceal prophylaxis, TIPS for recanalization; Yerdel grade IV PVT = relative (not absolute) LT contraindication per AASLD-AST 2025; AGA 2025 cirrhotic-PVT stratification (recent <6mo >50%/main-portal vein (PV)/mesenteric → AC; ischemia → urgent AC; chronic cavernoma → no AC) + CTP-class anticoagulant table + portal vein revascularization with TIPS (PVR-TIPS); Baveno VII standardized nomenclature + porto-sinusoidal vascular disorder (PSVD) coexistence (6 sources)
- [[acute-on-chronic-liver-failure]] — ACLF: ADDT — 3 definitions (North American Consortium for the Study of End-Stage Liver Disease (NACSELD)/European Association for the Study of the Liver–Chronic Liver Failure Consortium (EASL-CLIF)/Asian Pacific Association for the Study of the Liver (APASL)), ACLF-specific scores over MELD (GS 2), organ-specific management (brain/cardiovascular (CV)/respiratory/renal/infection/nutrition), terlipressin contraindicated in ACLF-3, protein restriction never indicated, LT candidacy criteria, futility threshold (CLIF-C>70/lactate>9), palliative care mandatory (51 guidance statements). Carries the **CLIF-SOFA organ-failure cut-offs** and the **numeric ACLF-1/-2/-3 definitions** from [[moreau-2013-canonic-aclf|CANONIC]], incl. the rule that a sub-threshold creatinine or grade I–II HE makes a single organ failure ACLF-1 (3 sources)
- [[budd-chiari-syndrome]] — Budd-Chiari: ADDT — hepatic venous outflow obstruction (primary vs secondary/extrinsic per Baveno VII), myeloproliferative neoplasm (MPN)/JAK2 most common etiology (40–50%), caudate hypertrophy, stepwise management (AC → angioplasty → TIPS → LT), ALF → urgent LT + emergency TIPS, avoid unfractionated heparin (UFH) (heparin-induced thrombocytopenia (HIT)), HCC surveillance q6mo with AFP (>15 ng/mL raises suspicion; LI-RADS not applicable), ≈2-weekly response assessment; **prognostic scores (incl. Budd-Chiari syndrome–TIPS (BCS-TIPS) prognostic index (PI)) demoted to research use per Baveno VIII** — both are now computable on the page, the Rotterdam score and the BCS-TIPS PI (age × 0.08 + bilirubin × 0.16 + INR × 0.63, cut-off >7) (6 sources)
- [[porto-sinusoidal-vascular-disorder]] — PSVD **or noncirrhotic portal fibrosis (NCPF)** (both names accepted, Baveno VIII): ADDT — noncirrhotic intrahepatic portal hypertension (PH) entity; adequate biopsy **≥15 mm with one fragment ≥10 mm**; **Baveno VIII point-scoring diagnosis** (specific PH signs +3 / non-specific +2; major histology +5 / minor +2; associated condition +1; concomitant chronic liver disease (CLD) −1 → **≥5 definite, 3–4 possible**) with major criteria nodular regenerative hyperplasia (NRH), muscularized portal venules, portal venule stenosis ≥50% of tracts; suspect when PH signs contrast with atypical-for-cirrhosis features (HVPG <10, liver stiffness measurement (LSM) <10); coexists with PVT; **LSM**-based varices criteria do not apply, but **endoscopy can be avoided if spleen stiffness measurement (SSM) <40 kPa and bilirubin <1 mg/dL**; surveillance 2 y no varices / 1 y small varices (2 sources)
- [[hereditary-hemorrhagic-telangiectasia]] — HHT (Osler-Weber-Rendu): ADDT — liver vascular malformations (LVMs) in 32–73%, no routine LVM screening, escalation ladder to bevacizumab at specialized centers, SMAD4→JPS/HHT overlap; **Curaçao criteria absent from the corpus — needs an HHT-specific source** (4 sources)
- [[hepatic-encephalopathy]] — HE: 4-axis classification (type A/B/C, West Haven Criteria (WHC) severity, time course, precipitants); overt hepatic encephalopathy (OHE) = diagnosis of exclusion; ammonia alone has NO diagnostic/staging/prognostic value (Rec 9); lactulose first-line (Rec 19); rifaximin add-on after 2nd episode (Rec 26); protein restriction never indicated (1.2–1.5 g/kg/day); late-night snack (Rec 32); 33 recommendations (AASLD/European Association for the Study of the Liver (EASL) 2014) (4 sources)
- [[acute-hepatic-porphyria]] — Acute hepatic porphyria (AHP): ADDT — inborn errors of heme synthesis (acute intermittent porphyria [AIP]/hereditary coproporphyria (HCP)/variegate porphyria (VP)/aminolevulinic acid dehydratase deficiency porphyria (ALAD); gene/inheritance table); acute neurovisceral attacks; dx by random urine aminolevulinic acid (ALA)+porphobilinogen (PBG) ≥5× ULN (creatinine-normalized) → genetic confirmation; hemin 3–4 mg/kg/day ×~4d + IV carbohydrate ~300 g/day, avoid porphyrinogenic drugs; givosiran subcutaneous (SC) monthly for ≥4 attacks/year; HCC surveillance from age 50 (US q6mo) + annual estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) (1 source)

### Other

- [[gastroenteropancreatic-neuroendocrine-tumors]] — Gastroenteropancreatic neuroendocrine tumors (GEP-NETs): ADDT — WHO grading (Ki-67/mitoses; NET G1/2/3 vs neuroendocrine carcinoma (NEC)), gastric types 1/2/3, **full AJCC v9 (2023) T/N/M staging site-by-site** (jejunum/ileum, duodenum/ampulla, appendix, colon/rectum, stomach, pancreas) — the strata its own therapy table conditions on, recovered from the ingested NCCN 2026 PDF after a stale flag wrongly declared them absent from the corpus; site/size/**depth**-based endoscopic vs surgical resection per NCCN's Principles of Endoscopic Treatment (EMR mucosal / ESD-eFTR submucosal / surgery at the muscle layer), the **three distinct Ki-67 cutoffs** (3%+20% grade · **>10% = "high G2"**, which drives the rectal pathway · 55% biology split), the gNET **secondary-vs-primary** subtyping with its three-test protocol and the **PPI-associated** branch, somatostatin receptor PET (SSTR-PET), somatostatin analogs, peptide receptor radionuclide therapy (PRRT) (Lu-177 dotatate), functioning-tumor syndromes; treatment-algorithm Mermaid (1 source)
- [[gastrointestinal-stromal-tumor]] — Gastrointestinal stromal tumor (GIST): ADDT — most common GI mesenchymal tumor; KIT(CD117)/DOG1+; KIT/PDGFRA/succinate dehydrogenase (SDH) mutational testing (essential, predictive); **NCCN 2026 "Risk Per CAP [College of American Pathologists]" bands (none/very low/low/moderate/high) by size × mitotic rate, gastric and non-gastric tables** — recovered from the ingested PDF after a stale flag claimed the moderate-vs-low stratum was uncorpusable, which mattered because adjuvant imatinib triggers at "moderate or high"; surgery + neoadjuvant/adjuvant imatinib (≥3y high-risk; **category 1** without prior imatinib, **2A** after); tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) sequence imatinib→sunitinib→regorafenib→ripretinib, avapritinib for PDGFRA D842V; treatment-algorithm Mermaid (3 sources)
- [[obesity]] — Obesity (BMI ≥30, or ≥27 + comorbidity): ranked pharmacotherapy (semaglutide 2.4mg > liraglutide > phentermine-topiramate > naltrexone-bupropion; against orlistat), intragastric balloon (responder rates, PPI prophylaxis, antiemetics, safety), lifestyle foundation, bariatric surgery (2 sources)
- [[post-transplant-lymphoproliferative-disorder]] — Post-transplant lymphoproliferative disorder (PTLD): ADDT — Epstein-Barr virus (EBV)-driven lymphoid proliferation post-LT (incidence 0.9–2.9%); risk factors (primary EBV, CMV mismatch, anti-lymphocyte antibodies); histopathology required (EBV viremia not diagnostic); treatment ladder — reduce immunosuppression first, then rituximab/surgery/radiotherapy (RT)/chemo if no response in 2–4 wk (2 sources)
- [[sclerosing-mesenteritis]] — Sclerosing mesenteritis: ADDT — idiopathic mesenteric fibroinflammation; CT misty mesentery/fat ring sign/pseudocapsule, usually dx without biopsy; only 1–6% need treatment; prednisone + tamoxifen 10 mg BID; surgery non-curative (1 source)
- [[hypermobile-ehlers-danlos-syndrome]] — Hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos syndrome (EDS) / hEDS: ADDT — heritable connective-tissue disorder with GI manifestations; DGBI overlap, postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome (POTS)/mast cell activation syndrome (MCAS) associations, Beighton/2017 criteria, motility & pelvic-floor testing, multidisciplinary symptom management (1 source)
- [[postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome]] — Postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome (POTS): ADDT — dysautonomia with orthostatic intolerance; diagnostic criteria (heart rate rise >=30 bpm within 10 min upright, >=40 bpm in adolescents 12-19 y, no orthostatic hypotension, >=6 mo, exclusions), hypovolemic/neuropathic/hyperadrenergic phenotypes, post-infectious and COVID-19 risk, GI motility findings, bedside vs autonomic testing, conservative targets (2 L/d fluid, 10 g/d salt) before volume expanders / rate control / vasoconstrictors (2 sources)
- [[mast-cell-activation-syndrome]] — Mast cell activation syndrome (MCAS): ADDT — episodic multisystem mast cell activation without proliferation; A+B+C consensus criteria (serum tryptase baseline + 20% + 2 ng/mL), primary (clonal, CD25/KIT D816V) vs secondary (IgE-mediated) vs idiopathic, tryptase at baseline and 1-4 h post-flare, CD-117 biopsy caveat, when NOT to test, H1+H2 first line then cromolyn/montelukast, trigger and low-histamine diet (1 source)
- [[alpha-gal-syndrome]] — Alpha-gal syndrome: ADDT — immunoglobulin E (IgE)-mediated mammalian-meat allergy (galactose-α-1,3-galactose) from tick-bite sensitization; GI phenotype of delayed (1–6 h) abdominal pain/diarrhea/nausea; 3-part dx (symptoms + ↑alpha-gal IgE + response to ≥1-mo avoidance; positive IgE alone insufficient); mammalian-product avoidance + tick precautions, epinephrine/antihistamine for acute exposure; cetuximab/gelatin/bioprosthetic-valve cross-reactivity (1 source)
- [[iron-deficiency-anemia]] — Iron-deficiency anemia: ADDT — ferritin <45 ng/mL dx (confirmatory transferrin saturation (TSAT)/sTfR/retic-Hgb when inflamed); bidirectional endoscopy + celiac biopsies + H. pylori test-and-treat, capsule for small bowel; oral iron (ferrous sulfate, once-daily/alternate-day, +vitamin C) vs IV iron (1–2 infusion formulations; pseudo-allergy safety); condition-specific repletion (post-bariatric, IBD Hgb<10→IV, PHG, gastric antral vascular ectasia (GAVE)→endoscopic band ligation (EBL)/APC, celiac→GFD, small-bowel angioectasias→enteroscopy+APC) (2 sources)
- [[narcotic-bowel-syndrome]] — Narcotic bowel syndrome (NBS): ADDT — paradoxic increase in abdominal pain despite continued or escalating opioid doses, ~6% of long-term opioid users seen for chronic GI conditions and widely under-recognized; the >100 mg morphine-equivalent/day and >3-month exposure markers, tramadol counted as an opioid, and the taper-plus-adjunct withdrawal protocol (3 sources)

---

- [[diverticulitis]] — Colonic diverticulitis: ADDT — modified Hinchey staging, CT-confirmed dx, **selective (not routine) antibiotics** for low-risk uncomplicated disease (with the full low-risk criteria + regimens/doses), post-recovery colonoscopy (strong after complicated, 6–8 wk delay), recurrence-prevention lifestyle bundle, elective surgery for quality of life (QoL) (episode count no longer an indication); against mesalamine/rifaximin/probiotics (1 source)
- [[segmental-colitis-associated-with-diverticulosis]] — Segmental colitis associated with diverticulosis (SCAD): chronic segmental colitis in diverticula-bearing colon, rectum spared; histology mimics UC/Crohn's; distinct-entity-vs-IBD unresolved (mgmt not in ingested source) (1 source)
- [[acute-cholecystitis]] — Acute cholecystitis: ADDT — diagnosis, the non-surgical candidate, and drainage route selection (EUS-guided vs percutaneous cholecystostomy vs transpapillary); TG18 diagnostic criteria (A + B suspected, + C definite) and Grade I/II/III severity table; imaging choice incl. gangrenous and emphysematous cholecystitis; antimicrobial therapy with the Grade I/II "stop within 24 h of surgery" rule; **antithrombotic rules before percutaneous drainage** (continue aspirin, stop clopidogrel 5 d, PT-INR <1.5 + heparin bridge, avoid with dual antiplatelet-plus-anticoagulant), percutaneous technique and its Grade III mortality signal, transpapillary tube-vs-stent choice (7 sources)
- [[acute-cholangitis]] — Acute cholangitis: ADDT — two decisions, **what grade** and **when to drain**. Tokyo Guidelines 2018 (TG18) 3-domain diagnostic criteria + combination rule; TG18 severity grading with the rule that decides it (**Grade III = any *one* organ dysfunction; Grade II = *two* of five** — age ≥75 or bili ≥5 alone is not moderate); ERCP over percutaneous transhepatic biliary drainage (PTBD), drainage + sphincterotomy/stone removal; **ASGE sets 48 h, not <24 h, and the 2026 RCT found urgent drainage in mild-moderate roughly doubles adverse events (17.1% vs 9.2%, mainly haemorrhage) with no mortality gain — resuscitate, then drain 24–48 h**; TG18's own <24 h moderate-cholangitis signal recorded but not followed; TG18 flowchart Mermaid. Full TG18 criteria and severity tables with units from Kiriyama 2018; Charcot's triad too insensitive to diagnose (6 sources)
- [[disconnected-pancreatic-duct-syndrome]] — Disconnected pancreatic duct (DPD) / DPD syndrome: ADDT — the duct is **completely** severed (vs a partial disruption/leak, which is why they are managed differently); classification, when to evaluate, MRCP/ERCP/secretin modalities and pitfalls; drainage route, transpapillary PD stents, **indefinite double-pigtail stents after lumen-apposing metal stent (LAMS) removal**, surgery. ⚠ U.S. Pancreatic Disease Study Group (USPG) 2025 is a consensus statement — **no GRADE ratings, no numbered recommendations** (3 sources)
- [[nonampullary-duodenal-adenoma]] — resection by size (cold-snare <20mm / hot-snare EMR >20mm), bleeding risk, surveillance, FAP/Spiegelman (3 sources)
- [[sphincter-of-oddi-dysfunction]] — SOD: sphincter-complex dysmotility (post-cholecystectomy pain / idiopathic recurrent acute pancreatitis (IRAP)); biliary (Rome III/Milwaukee) & pancreatic 3-type criteria; treat type I/1 (and manometry-confirmed II/2) with sphincterotomy, **never scope type III/3** (EPISOD); rectal indomethacin ± PD stent prophylaxis (3 sources)
