## Diagnostic Schemas

- [[dysphagia]] — Dysphagia: oropharyngeal vs. esophageal localization, solids-vs-liquids discriminator, EGD-first algorithm with mandatory EoE biopsies, HRM/functional lumen imaging probe (FLIP) when EGD normal, alarm features for malignancy; EoE biopsy rule with its qualifiers (≥6 biopsies, ≥2 levels, proximal/mid *and* distal). **Oropharyngeal arm, solids-vs-liquids discriminator and alarm-feature list remain unsourced — needs a dysphagia guideline** (6 sources)
- [[chronic-diarrhea]] — Chronic diarrhea (>4 wk): watery/inflammatory/fatty + secretory/osmotic classification, stool osmotic gap, calprotectin-guided algorithm, microscopic colitis/bile acid diarrhea/celiac/EPI workup, positive IBS-D diagnosis, role of endoscopy (ASGE 2010) (3 sources)
- [[nausea-and-vomiting]] — Nausea & vomiting: vs regurgitation/rumination, acute vs chronic, GI/central nervous system (CNS)/metabolic/functional DDx, mandatory pregnancy test, EGD + 4-hr gastric emptying for gastroparesis with its full protocol qualifiers (255-kcal 2% fat solid meal, imaging 0/1/2/4 h, ≥3 h required), CVS/CHS recognition. **Acute-vs-chronic framework, CNS/metabolic DDx and lab panel remain unsourced — needs an N&V guideline** (5 sources)
- [[abdominal-bloating-and-distention]] — Belching (gastric vs supragastric vs aerophagia), bloating vs distention, Rome IV criteria, symptom-driven workup (2-wk diet trial, celiac serology, anorectal testing), neuromodulator/behavioral therapy, probiotics not recommended, abdominophrenic dyssynergia (APD) (4 sources)
- [[jaundice]] — Jaundice: bilirubin fractionation (unconjugated/conjugated), hemolysis vs Gilbert, R-ratio hepatocellular vs cholestatic, right upper quadrant (RUQ) US → MRCP/EUS/ERCP for biliary dilation, cholangitis/painless-jaundice red flags (5 sources)
- [[dyspepsia]] — Dyspepsia: ACG/Canadian Association of Gastroenterology (CAG) 2017 — age ≥60 → EGD (raised from ≥55); age <60 → H. pylori test-and-treat (Strong/High) → PPI → TCA/prokinetic → psychotherapy; alarm features alone have low positive predictive value (PPV); PPI safety and the withdrawal-every-6-12-months rule. The post-endoscopy half now lives on [[functional-dyspepsia]] (3 sources)
- [[laryngopharyngeal-symptoms]] — Laryngopharyngeal symptoms (LPS) vs. laryngopharyngeal reflux disease (LPRD): San Diego Consensus algorithm — laryngoscopy → esophageal symptom bifurcation → EGD/pH-impedance/wireless pH; laryngeal hyperresponsiveness; laryngeal recalibration therapy (LRT); neuromodulators (2 sources)
- [[extraesophageal-reflux]] — Extraesophageal reflux (EER) umbrella (laryngitis, chronic cough, asthma, dental erosions): no single test proves gastroesophageal reflux (GER) causation, 50–60% aren't GERD; test-before-PPI without typical symptoms; PPI response ≠ diagnosis; one 12-wk trial → objective testing; on-therapy pH-impedance for refractory; surgery only with objective GERD (2 sources)
- [[variceal-upper-gi-bleeding]] — Variceal upper gastrointestinal bleeding (UGIB): resuscitation (Hgb 7 target; no fresh frozen plasma (FFP)/platelets based on INR/platelet count), immediate vasoactive + antibiotics (ceftriaxone 1g × 5d), EVL vs. glue for gastric varices, preemptive TIPS (CTP B>7 active bleeding or CTP C 10–13, within 72h ideally 24h — **no MELD threshold is an indication in either source**), NSBB at vasoactive discontinuation, no routine PPI after AVH confirmed, secondary prophylaxis (NSBB + EVL); 6-week mortality **10–15%**; HVPG ≥10 = CSPH (<12 is only a post-TIPS target); Prosty 2025's prophylaxis-vs-none / short-vs-long split (7 sources)
- [[seronegative-villous-atrophy]] — Villous atrophy with negative celiac serology: the four-way definition table (seronegative enteropathy vs seronegative celiac disease (CeD) vs CeD with selective IgA deficiency vs idiopathic villous atrophy), why raised intraepithelial lymphocytes (IELs) with normal villi do **not** qualify, the non-celiac causes (immune-mediated, infectious, iatrogenic/drug, inflammatory), and the stepwise workup including HLA-DQ2/DQ8 and response to a gluten-free diet (3 sources)
- [[fever-returning-traveler]] — Fever in a returning traveler: GI-focused differential (enteric fever, amoebiasis, hepatitis A/E, schistosomiasis), diagnostic algorithm, key tests, red flags. Rebuilt on three guidelines; the non-GI differential is explicitly out of their scope and the page says so (3 sources)
- [[biliary-stricture]] — Biliary stricture: benign vs. malignant differential, EUS-guided fine-needle biopsy (EUS-FNB) vs. ERCP sampling, perihilar vs. distal algorithm, cholangioscopy + FISH, drainage goals, photodynamic therapy (PDT)/RFA for unresectable perihilar CCA (3 sources)
- [[recurrent-acute-pancreatitis]] — Recurrent acute pancreatitis: ≥2 AP episodes with complete resolution ≥3 mo apart; EUS-preferred evaluation of unexplained RAP (MRI/MRCP complementary), ERCP role controversial, occult-malignancy red flags; pancreas-divisum/SOD framework (AGA 2022) (1 source)
- [[subepithelial-lesion]] — GI subepithelial lesion: EUS layer-based differential (GIST/leiomyoma/NET/lipoma), tissue acquisition (FNA/FNB/single-incision needle-knife (SINK)/unroofing), size thresholds for resection, **type-1 gastric NET — EMR has the higher R0 (95% vs 83% for ESD) and less vertical-margin involvement (3% vs 17%)**, full rectal-NET size ladder, malignant EUS features (2 sources)
- [[acute-lower-gi-bleeding]] — Acute lower gastrointestinal bleeding (LGIB): Oakland score, CTA-first for active bleeding, urgent colonoscopy no longer standard (two RCTs show no benefit), EBL vs. clipping for diverticular bleed, tranexamic acid contraindicated, resume anticoagulation within 7 days (3 sources)
- [[ascites]] — Ascites: serum–ascites albumin gradient (SAAG)-based differential, grade 1–3 classification, diagnostic paracentesis algorithm, spironolactone/furosemide dosing, large-volume paracentesis (LVP) + albumin 6–8 g/L, TIPS criteria, hepatic hydrothorax + inpatient volume-overload/anasarca + hyponatremia management (5 sources)
- [[upper-gi-bleeding]] — Upper GI Bleeding: Glasgow-Blatchford score (GBS) scoring (0–1 = safe discharge), restrictive transfusion at Hgb 7, erythromycin pre-endoscopy, 24h endoscopy standard, ACG's actual stigmata data (oozing 39% pooled; number needed to treat (NNT) 2 active bleeding, NNT 5 nonbleeding visible vessel (NBVV); adherent-clot RRs), epinephrine never monotherapy, high-dose PPI post-hemostasis, transcatheter arterial embolization (TAE) over surgery for failure. **Forrest class definitions + class-specific rebleeding rates tabled from ACG/ASGE 2025** (ACG 2021 never defines them); Rockall and AIMS65 components from AGA 2020, with the page stating plainly that Rockall's point-assignment cutpoints are not in any guideline it cites and that AIMS65's published albumin unit is a printing error (7 sources)
- [[abnormal-liver-chemistries]] — Abnormal liver chemistries: true healthy ALT ULN (29–33 M / 19–25 F), R-ratio hepatocellular vs. cholestatic, tiered AST/ALT algorithm (5 tiers), ALP + gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) evaluation, bilirubin fractionation, liver biopsy indications (2 sources)
- [[acute-diarrhea]] — Acute diarrheal infections: severity classification, viral/bacterial/parasitic/non-infectious differential, ACG 2016 Figure 1 stepwise algorithm, ORS/loperamide/antibiotic table, parasite-specific treatment, Infectious Diseases Society of America (IDSA) 2017 additions (enteric fever, Shiga toxin–producing Escherichia coli (STEC)/hemolytic uremic syndrome (HUS) monitoring, empiric-antibiotic exceptions, pediatric ancillary care, vaccines), postinfectious IBS (4 sources)
- [[hiv-aids-related-diarrhea]] — HIV/AIDS-related diarrhea: endoscopic evaluation strategy (sig vs colonoscopy, CD4<100 yield, CMV biopsy) + Cryptosporidium/microsporidia treatment (3 sources — incomplete pending a primary opportunistic infection (OI) guideline)
- [[small-bowel-bleeding]] — Small bowel bleeding: SBB/potential-SBB/obscure gastrointestinal bleeding (OGIB) terminology, age-stratified DDx, 3 diagnostic algorithms (occult, brisk, sub-acute), video capsule endoscopy (VCE)/device-assisted enteroscopy (DAE)/computed tomography enterography (CTE)/angiography roles, angioectasia treatment (3 sources)
- [[liver-disease-in-pregnancy]] — Liver disease in pregnancy: physiologic lab changes table, trimester-stratified DDx, Swansea criteria for AFLP, HG/intrahepatic cholestasis of pregnancy (IHCP)/preeclampsia/HELLP/AFLP, viral hepatitis, chronic liver disease management in pregnancy (3 sources)
- [[chronic-constipation]] — Chronic constipation: what counts as abnormal; the **three physiologic subtypes** (normal-transit, slow-transit, defecatory disorder) and which test separates them; secondary causes to exclude before labelling it primary; stepwise algorithm; **test conditions that change the answer**; red flags (6 sources)
- [[colorectal-polyposis]] — Colorectal polyposis: the schema the 9 polyposis disease scripts point at — DDx by **phenotype** (adenomatous / hamartomatous / serrated / *not* a polyposis), diagnostic algorithm, **polyp-count and age thresholds that trigger genetic evaluation**, serrated polyposis syndrome WHO clinical criteria, which genes go on the panel, red flags (5 sources)
- [[ingested-foreign-body]] — Ingested foreign body & food bolus impaction: who ingests and **where objects get stuck**; underlying esophageal pathology behind a food bolus (EoE first); 7-step algorithm — airway/surgical screen → localize → **timing triage (emergent/urgent/elective)** → size and length thresholds → object-class management (button battery, magnets, sharps, narcotic packets) → endoscopic technique → post-retrieval obligations; red flags (1 source)
- [[gastric-outlet-obstruction]] — Gastric outlet obstruction: the **Gastric Outlet Obstruction Scoring System (GOOSS) 0–4 score** in full (this page is its home); malignant vs benign DDx; EGD-first algorithm (ASGE Rec 11, High) with mandatory biopsy; **the 2-month prognosis threshold that selects palliation** — enteral stent if expected survival <2 mo, surgical gastrojejunostomy if ≥2 mo — with the SUSTENT outcome table and its three load-bearing qualifiers (Bonferroni caveat, the definitional major-complication difference, the mostly-*open* surgical arm); transpapillary stents can foreclose ERCP access; EUS-guided gastroenterostomy (EUS-GE) technique only — **the corpus contains no outcome data on EUS-GE vs surgery** (4 sources)
- [[focal-liver-lesions]] — Focal liver lesions **in the non-cirrhotic/not-at-risk liver** (ACG 2024 explicitly excludes the at-risk liver — that is [[li-rads]] territory): Rec 1 multiphasic protocol, benign/malignant DDx, gadoxetate/OATP1B3 hepatobiliary-phase discriminator and its pitfall, US-only hemangioma exit (Key Concept (KC) 24a), biopsy do/don't lists, cystic-lesion workup (Recs 12/13), at-risk-liver hemangioma exception (q3–6 mo × ≥1 y), routing table to disease scripts (1 source)

## General GI Procedures

- [[bowel-preparation]] — Bowel preparation — **coverage-gap queue item 28, cleared 2026-10-15**; now the single home for the prep content moved off [[colonoscopy]]. Split-dose for everyone (second portion **4-6 hr before**, finished **>=2 hr before** start); **no purgative is superior**, 2 L suggested over 4 L on tolerability — **except after a prior inadequate prep, where 4 L wins** (81% vs 67%, OR 2.07); the 10-regimen table with volumes, split schedules and which three are **not FDA-approved**; NaP and magnesium contraindication thresholds (creatinine clearance <60, NYHA III-IV, EF <50%) and the NaP/picosulfate lesions that **mimic early IBD**; simethicone now **suggested** (>=320 mg), a reversal of the 2015 position; **BBPS/Ottawa/Aronchick per-segment criteria** with the operative rule (**segment score 2 or 3 in every segment = adequate**) and the warning that the **2014 total->=5 rule and the 2025 per-segment rule disagree**; confirm inadequacy by scoping to the sigmoid rather than cancelling on patient self-report; salvage Mermaid; repeat within **12 mo** (3 mo after an abnormal stool test); 19-row risk-factor OR table, additive to a 98% plateau at 7 factors, with the explicit warning **not to use a risk score** (PPV 29%-41%) (3 sources)
- [[colonoscopy]] — CRC screening ages, post-polypectomy surveillance tables, bowel prep standards (incl. glucagon-like peptide-1 (GLP-1) agonist guidance), polypectomy quick reference, IBD surveillance technique (SCENIC); **complication rates corrected — perforation 0.08% overall (1.5% for ≥20 mm lesions), bleeding 9.8/1000**; Boston Bowel Preparation Scale (BBPS) adequacy cutoff, salvage maneuvers, capsule colonoscopy. Quality targets rebuilt on **ASGE/ACG 2024** (ADR ≥35%, adenomas per colonoscopy ≥0.6, sessile serrated lesion detection rate ≥6%, withdrawal **≥8 min**, cecal intubation ≥95%, cold snare 4–9 mm ≥90%), each shown beside the superseded AGA 2021 figure, with the widened ADR denominator flagged; the full 15-indicator set lives on [[colonoscopy-quality-indicators]] (16 sources)
- [[polypectomy]] — Colorectal polypectomy: lesion assessment (Paris/laterally spreading tumor (LST)/narrow-band imaging (NBI) International Colorectal Endoscopic (NICE)/Workgroup Serrated Polyps and Polyposis (WASP), non-lifting sign), technique by size and morphology incl. AGA 2024 tailored-polypectomy BPAs, tattoo, malignant polyp recognition and management (Haggitt/Kikuchi, favorable vs unfavorable histology, specimen handling, CAP reporting), quality standards and competency tools, electrosurgical current choice and mid-stalk snare placement (6 sources)
- [[upper-endoscopy]] — EGD quality standards: UGIB timing, BE surveillance, GPMC biopsy, celiac biopsy, peptic ulcer hemostasis (5 sources)
- [[argon-plasma-coagulation]] — APC: non-contact thermal hemostasis/ablation via ionized argon; GAVE, angioectasias, radiation proctopathy, GI bleeding; power-vs-argon-flow physics, 0.8–1.0 L/min, explosion mechanism and oral-prep rule (17 sources)
- [[enteral-access]] — Choosing and managing enteral tubes: gastric vs small-bowel, nasoenteric vs percutaneous endoscopic gastrostomy (PEG)/percutaneous endoscopic gastrojejunostomy (PEGJ)/direct percutaneous endoscopic jejunostomy (DPEJ) route selection, periprocedural antibiotics/antithrombotics, technique, and complications (2 sources)
- [[esophageal-dilation]] — Bougie and through-the-scope balloon dilation of esophageal strictures: indications, dilator choice, "start low and go slow" sizing and stop rules, the ≥16 mm target, and adverse-event rates with denominators (9 sources)
- [[endoscopic-management-of-perforation]] — Endoscopic closure of GI perforation — **created 2026-09-08**, the wiki's first coverage of this at all (grepping the three endoscopy pages for perforation/over-the-scope clip (OTSC)/through-the-scope clip (TTSC)/Stapfer had returned three lines total, all rate figures from other sources). The two **absolute surgical indications** (hemodynamically unstable; delayed with peritoneal signs); the CO₂-low-flow / keep-the-defect-non-dependent first response; and **the closure device chosen by defect size, organ by organ** — TTSC <1–2 cm, OTSC 1–3 cm, suturing or endoloop+TTSC >2–3 cm, fcSEMS as rescue. Now the **one home for the full Stapfer classification** (types I–IV with the mechanism and location defining each, added 2026-10-20): type I scope-induced duodenal wall → surgery, type II peri-Vaterian and type III distal bile duct seal spontaneously, type IV retroperitoneal air alone is **not a true perforation**. Carries the **five surgical indications**, the **1-minute contrast rule** that separates a large from a minimal leak, and the 8 h / 48 h CT surveillance schedule — plus the explicit negatives: fever and leukocytosis alone are not an indication, and a benign abdomen does not exclude surgery. Sphincterotomy-technique prevention rules and the caveat that 14% of retroperitoneal air is asymptomatic; colon site distribution (sigmoid 63.8%) and the **right-colon TTSC-only constraint**; the five-step EMR avoidance sequence (2 sources)
- [[endoscopic-hemostasis]] — The hemostatic devices themselves (site-specific algorithms stay on the bleeding pages): epinephrine 1:10,000 as temporizing-only, ethanol volume cap, heater probe 25–30 J ×4–5 (total 100–150 J), bipolar 15–20 W, ≥8 s forceful contact; monopolar hemostatic forceps soft coag 80 W effect 4 applied **closed** (98% vs 80% initial hemostasis vs clips) and its failure modes; OTSC for recurrent bleeding (**no head-to-head data between any two devices**); TC-325 rescue-only because **the powder is gone in <24 h**, with the full dry-catheter technique; Doppler probe after hemostasis; bleeding caused by EMR/ESD/sphincterotomy and the prophylactic PD-stent threshold; the failed-hemostasis checklist and TAE-vs-surgery (4 sources)
- [[paracentesis]] — Paracentesis — **coverage-gap queue item 34, cleared 2026-10-17**; the procedure home for the mechanics moved off [[ascites]]. Who gets a diagnostic tap (all new-onset ascites, every emergent cirrhotic admission even without infection signs, AKI, decompensation/ACLF workup, hyponatremia workup) and **within 12–24 h of admission — the timing is associated with lower hospital mortality**; what to send, with **bedside inoculation of ≥10 mL into aerobic and anaerobic blood culture bottles before antibiotics raising culture sensitivity >90%**; the **2-day repeat tap with <25% PMN fall = antibiotic failure**; bleeding — **no prophylactic transfusion**, 1% minimal cutaneous bleeding at INR >1.5 with platelets <50 ×10⁹/L, safe without FFP to **INR 8.7**, with the DIC/uremia and creatinine >2.5 mg/dL carve-outs; **LVP >5 L → albumin 6–8 g per litre removed**, the sub-5 L triggers (systolic BP <90, Na <130, AKI), the <8 L/session PPCD ceiling, and the **AASLD/AGA per-litre vs AGA 2022 weight-based 1 g/kg conflict** surfaced; serial-tap alternatives (TIPS, tunnelled catheter/alfapump per Baveno VIII 6.11) and the separate pediatric rules. ⚠ No ingested guideline prescribes puncture site, needle or ultrasound technique — deliberately absent (8 sources)
- [[endoscopic-band-ligation]] — Endoscopic band ligation (EBL) as a technique across all five of its indications: esophageal varices and GOV1 (session and surveillance schedule, post-EVL ulcer bleeding with MELD >18 or emergency EVL as the risk set), where banding stops for cardiofundal gastric varices, GAVE, colonic diverticular hemorrhage (clip-mark → withdraw → reload → reinsert, device-diameter feasibility criterion, 0.31% perforation), internal hemorrhoids, and the four *when not to band* situations (6 sources)

## Advanced GI Procedures

### Foregut and Motility Procedures

- [[antireflux-surgery]] — Antireflux surgery: candidate-selection table; [[hiatal-hernia|hernia]]-size × Hill-grade decision table (**≤2 cm + Hill I/II → TIF 2.0; >2 cm + Hill III/IV → cTIF**); Society of American Gastrointestinal and Endoscopic Surgeons (SAGES) technique trade-offs (robotic vs laparoscopic, complete vs partial wrap, short-gastric division); surgery vs medical management; Stretta and the investigational endoscopic techniques; post-surgical GE valve assessment; pre-op HRM via [[hrm-antireflux-surgery]]. **TIF-specific selection, technique and aftercare moved to [[transoral-incisionless-fundoplication]] 2026-10-05** (one home per fact) (6 sources)
- [[transoral-incisionless-fundoplication]] — TIF 2.0 / cTIF: what the EsophyX valve is and how it differs from a surgical wrap; ASGE 2025 eligibility (confirmed GERD + hernia ≤2 cm + Hill I/II + ≥1 of 5 qualifying criteria); hernia-size × Hill-grade routing; EsophyX relative-contraindication table; AFS 5-item pre-TIF workup (**HRM only if dysphagia**, FLIP acceptable alternative); technique rules (CO₂ 15 mmHg, pre-dilate 57–60 Fr, advance 0.5–1.0 cm below EGJ before firing, 11 o'clock start, 20-fastener minimum, never re-fire, 270°–320° valve); cTIF intra-abdominal-length hard stop (2–3 cm); post-TIF protocol (antiemetics, staged 6-wk diet, PPI 4–6 wk, ≤15 lb × 8 wk); outcome and adverse-event tables (2 sources)
- [[intragastric-balloon]] — Endoscopic restrictive balloon for weight loss; **BMI 30–40 kg/m² is the only selection criterion AGA 2021 gives** and it prescribes **no dwell or removal time**; graded recommendations cover endoscopically placed balloons only (not swallowable Elipse); periprocedural orders table (PPI prophylaxis *Strong* with no named agent/dose, scheduled antiemetics × 2 wk, 1–2 adult multivitamins); adverse-event grid (serious AE 5.6% vs 1.1%, RR 3.07 [1.16–8.11]; perforation 0.3%; early removal 9.4%; 0 deaths) (2 sources)
- [[endoscopic-bariatric-therapies]] — Endoscopic bariatric therapies (EBTs) as a class: the gastric vs small-bowel classification and mechanisms, AGA 2017's **7 ungraded Best Practice Advice** (lifestyle failure indication, bridge to surgery or to a weight-limited operation, structured program, pre-enrollment screening, prospective follow-up of poor responders, credentialing); non-balloon device data — endoscopic sleeve gastroplasty (ESG), primary obesity surgery endolumenal (POSE), aspiration therapy, TransPyloric Shuttle, Full Sense; small-bowel EndoBarrier duodenojejunal bypass sleeve (DJBS) with the **3.5% hepatic-abscess trial termination**. AGA 2017 gives **no BMI cut-point, contraindication list, or age criterion**; balloon-specific data and the graded recommendations live on [[intragastric-balloon]] (3 sources)
- [[endoscopic-eradication-therapy]] — EMR/ablation eradication of dysplastic Barrett's (resect-then-ablate, CE-IM endpoint) (5 sources)
- [[radiofrequency-ablation]] — RFA: preferred ablative modality within EET for flat dysplastic Barrett's (balloon/focal catheters, ~3 sessions to CE-IM); also bleeding radiation proctopathy and intraductal RFA + plastic stent for unresectable perihilar cholangiocarcinoma; pooled adverse event (AE) 8.8% (stricture 5.6%) (6 sources)
- [[bariatric-surgery]] — Surgical weight-loss procedures (sleeve, Roux-en-Y gastric bypass (RYGB), banding) (6 sources)

- [[high-resolution-manometry]] — HRM: Chicago v4.0 protocol, manufacturer-specific IRP thresholds, key metrics (IRP/DCI/peristaltic integrity), pre-ARS mandatory (5 sources)
- [[poem]] — POEM: preprocedure workup (endoscopy/timed barium esophagram (TBE)/HRM/FLIP), achalasia type-driven myotomy (Type III preferred, tailored long myotomy), EGJOO/spastic disorders case-by-case only, single-dose antibiotics, anterior=posterior tunnel, post-POEM reflux (acid exposure 41–56%, ¼ asymptomatic) + surveillance, same-day discharge, failed-myotomy POEM 62% vs PD 27% (**the only RCT**; uncontrolled single-arm series report 94–95.7%, and the design difference is now stated wherever the number appears); G-POEM for gastroparesis (AGA 2024 CPU) (4 sources)
- [[heller-myotomy]] — Laparoscopic Heller myotomy + partial fundoplication for achalasia; surgical alternative to POEM/PD; built-in anti-reflux wrap → less post-op GERD than POEM (5 sources)
- [[pneumatic-dilation]] — PD for achalasia: graded Rigiflex 3.0→3.5→4.0 cm protocol with 4–6-week reassessment, inflation parameters (10–15 psi, 15–60 s), the surgical-candidacy prerequisite, favorable-response predictors, cumulative efficacy by balloon size (74/86/90%), perforation 1.9% vs ASGE 2.8% and its size-dependent management, no routine post-dilation esophagram, GERD 15–35%, PD-after-failed-LHM 89% (4 sources)
- [[endoscopic-submucosal-dissection]] — ESD (esophagogastric): en-bloc knife resection of early GI neoplasia; ESD vs EMR vs surgery by lesion size (esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) >15 mm, EAC >20 mm, gastric adenocarcinoma (GAC) 20–30 mm), curative R0 criteria, M1–SM3 depth, gastric absolute/expanded criteria, ESD-vs-EMR outcomes and perforation risk (5 sources)
- [[flip-panometry]] — FLIP Panometry v2.0: 16-cm EndoFLIP protocol, EGJ Opening (reduced [REO]/normal [NEO]) × Contractile Response → 6-class motility matrix; Normal = high NPV for achalasia; Non-spastic Obstruction = EGJOO equivalent; AGA 2025 indications/use (EGD first, not for GERD, intraprocedural myotomy) (2 sources)
- [[anorectal-manometry]] — High-resolution anorectal manometry (HR-ARM) + BET: the **IAPWG standardized protocol** (stabilization 3 min → rest 60 s → 3 × 5-s squeeze → 30-s long squeeze → 2 coughs → 3 × 15-s push → rectal sensory test → RAIR, with every recovery interval), equipment minimums (≥6 cm recording length, balloon 3–5 cm above the anal canal, ≥400 mL RST balloon, 16 Fr BET catheter), the reported-measurement set, technique-specific reference values (values are not transferable between systems), BET mechanics with balloon-type qualifiers (sens/spec ~88%, PPV 64%, **NPV 97%**), rectoanal-gradient pitfalls, anal electromyography (EMG) cutoff, pattern assignment via [[london-classification]] (6 sources)
- [[capsule-endoscopy]] — Capsule endoscopy: small-bowel visualization, SBB first-line investigation (**PPV 94–97%, NPV 83–100%** — not sensitivity/specificity), graded ACG/ASGE recommendations, Crohn's and polyposis indications, yield predictors by timing/indication, retention risk + patency-capsule table, repeat-VCE role (5 sources)
- [[device-assisted-enteroscopy]] — DAE (single/double-balloon, spiral): therapeutic enteroscopy after positive VCE, DAE-first in altered anatomy/angiectasia suspicion, SBB hemostasis (4 sources)

### Colorectal Procedures

- [[sacral-nerve-stimulation]] — SNS: **Strong/Low for moderate–severe fecal incontinence; explicitly NOT recommended for constipation**; two-stage trial, outcomes, adverse-event and alternatives tables (2 sources)
- [[biofeedback-therapy]] — Biofeedback: indication/strength table; 5-step protocol; balloon-expulsion gating role; digital rectal exam performance (75% sensitivity / 87% specificity for dyssynergia); diagnosis via [[anorectal-manometry]]; manometric pattern names from [[london-classification]] (3 sources)

- [[colonic-stenting]] — Colonic SEMS for malignant obstruction: bridge-to-surgery vs palliation; **uncovered** stents in the colon (unlike the covered esophageal choice); technical/clinical success 91.9%/71.7%, perforation 1.7%, migration 3.9%; **right-sided (proximal to the splenic flexure) is an accepted bridge to surgery (BTS) indication**; the bevacizumab-within-6-weeks contraindication and the **odds ratio (OR) 46 seeding risk after stent perforation** (2 sources)
- [[colorectal-esd]] — ESD (colorectal): limited indication set (≥20 mm superficial submucosal invasion (SMI) not en-bloc resectable by EMR, fibrosis, residual/recurrent early carcinoma, colitis-associated dysplasia), hybrid ESD, eFTR; early-T1 CRC en-bloc imperative, unified USMSTF/ESGE/Japan Gastroenterological Endoscopy Society (JGES) curative criteria, solitary deep-SMI reappraisal (~2.6% lymph node metastasis (LNM)), post-resection surveillance; **eFTR size ceilings reconciled by lesion type 2026-09-08** — mucosal <30 mm outer bound / ≤20 mm working ceiling (USMSTF's own R0 breakpoint: 81.2% vs 58.1%), T1 CRC <2 cm, subepithelial <15 mm — replacing the page's former self-contradiction and a **fabricated "Rec 26 (Weak; low-quality)" grade that does not exist in the source** (4 sources)
- [[endoscopic-mucosal-resection]] — Colorectal EMR: inject-and-cut technique + electrosurgery settings, submucosal lifting agents (AGA 2025), post-resection margin ablation/clipping and bleeding benchmarks, underwater EMR, cold snare EMR, hot avulsion (cutting current, AGA 2026), post-EMR (piecemeal ≥20mm) surveillance (5 sources)

### Hepatobiliary Procedures

- [[ercp]] — ERCP: choledocholithiasis/cholangitis indications, difficult-stone techniques (endoscopic papillary large-balloon dilation (EPLBD), cholangioscopy lithotripsy), biliary stricture sampling/drainage (FCSEMS), bile leaks, SOD typing, cholangitis drainage timing (<48 h; <24 h "may be considered" only in fluid-refractory septic shock), **PEP prevention corrected — incidence 8% average-risk / 15% high-risk; rectal NSAID >30 min before or during (not "immediately"); NSAID+PD-stent trials underpowered**; wire-guided cannulation, hydration regimen, quality metrics, altered-anatomy access (13 sources)
- [[endoscopic-ultrasound]] — EUS quality standards: indications, full quality-indicator set with ACG 2025's own **>** thresholds, FNA/FNB yield table, interventional EUS technical success, AE benchmarks; cyst-fluid molecular markers (combined **KRAS and/or GNAS** — no separate GNAS figure exists); mediastinal adenopathy / lung-cancer staging (EUS vs endobronchial ultrasound (EBUS) stations, combined-modality NPV, lymphoma sampling) (7 sources)
- [[cholangioscopy]] — Direct endoscopic biliary visualization with targeted biopsy during ERCP (4 sources)
- [[intraductal-ultrasound]] — IDUS: the 6 malignant criteria (incl. wall thickness >9 mm), its role as a *targeting* tool that cannot sample, accuracy figures for indeterminate strictures / hilar CCA staging / ampullary tumour staging, and the ASGE-vs-ACG disagreement on whether it is still available (4 sources)
- [[tips]] — TIPS: physiological effects; indications (variceal bleeding incl. preemptive/rescue, refractory ascites, hepatic hydrothorax, vascular); absolute contraindications (congestive heart failure (CHF) stage C/D or ejection fraction (EF) <50%, mPAP >45 mm Hg, uncontrolled HE, sepsis) — no MELD threshold contraindicates TIPS; portosystemic pressure gradient (PSPG) targets by indication (<12 mm Hg or ≥50% reduction for acute variceal bleed); polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE) stent technique; complications; shunt surveillance; TIPS vs retrograde transvenous obliteration (RTO)/BRTO (8 sources)

- [[endoscopic-full-thickness-resection]] — Endoscopic full-thickness resection (EFTR) for GI subepithelial lesions: exposed vs nonexposed (submucosal tunneling endoscopic resection (STER), device-assisted full-thickness resection device (FTRD)) techniques, size limits, R0/en-bloc rates, adverse events (3 sources)
- [[g-poem]] — G-POEM: endoscopic pyloromyotomy for refractory gastroparesis — selection (GES retention >20% at 4h; GCSI >2, N/V-predominant), success predictors (GCSI >2.6), durable success 50–60% (sham RCT 71% vs 22%), AEs 6–14% (2 sources)
- [[eus-guided-gallbladder-drainage]] — EUS-guided gallbladder drainage (LAMS): indications in the non-surgical candidate, technique, outcomes vs percutaneous and transpapillary drainage, adverse events (4 sources)
- [[pancreatic-fluid-collection-drainage]] — Pseudocyst and walled-off necrosis (WON) drainage: revised-Atlanta collection definitions with CT findings, drainage indications (incl. the >8-week sterile-necrosis rule and "size alone is not an indication"), timing (2-week floor, 4-week target), pre-procedure checklist, step-up algorithm, transmural vs transpapillary vs percutaneous route selection, stent choice and dwell time (plastic vs SEMS vs LAMS), direct endoscopic necrosectomy, after-care, adverse events, outcomes, and the 12 graded ASGE 2016 recommendations (4 sources)
- [[eus-guided-biliary-drainage]] — EUS-BD after failed or infeasible ERCP: the four techniques plus EDGE, indications/contraindications, route selection by aetiology and papillary access (rendezvous in benign disease; EUS-CDS vs EUS-HGS criteria; EUS-HGS for hilar obstruction), surgically altered anatomy (EDGE in RYGB, E-ERCP first in non-bypass), when PTBD is preferred, the 6 conditional ASGE 2024 recommendations, outcomes vs PTBD, adverse events, and ACG/ASGE 2025 quality targets (4 sources)
- [[interventional-eus-vascular]] — Interventional EUS for vascular investigation and therapy: EUS-guided portal pressure gradient, coil/glue therapy for gastric varices, portal vein sampling; EUS-guided portal pressure gradient (EUS-PPG) best-practice consensus (Wang 2026 Delphi) (2 sources)
- [[celiac-plexus-block]] — Celiac plexus block (CPB) vs neurolysis (CPN) — the **injectate**, not the route, is the distinction (bupivacaine + triamcinolone vs alcohol/phenol); indications in chronic pancreatitis (nonobstructive, medically refractory; set expectations at consent — **<60% respond, benefit <6 mo**) and in unresectable pancreatic cancer (adjunct to analgesia, not a substitute; not for resectable/borderline disease); CPN technique (≥22-gauge needle not the EUS-TA needle, 10–20 mL 99% alcohol, 1 L IV saline, ~2 h monitoring); efficacy tables for both populations; repeat-injection interval (≥3 mo); adverse-event rates by procedure incl. **spinal cord infarction 0.2%**; the ACG-2020-vs-ASGE-2024 route disagreement surfaced (6 sources)

## Meds

- [[lubiprostone]] — Lubiprostone: ClC-2 chloride-channel activator; **CIC 24 mcg BID (Conditional/Low, OTC-failure qualifier)**; place vs other secretagogues; cost; ACG 2020 vs AGA 2022 contradiction on IBS-C surfaced (IBS-C dose not in any source) (3 sources)

- [[antibiotic-prophylaxis-cirrhosis]] — SBP primary prophylaxis in cirrhotic upper gastrointestinal bleeding (UGB): IV ceftriaxone during acute GI hemorrhage (AASLD 2021); norfloxacin withdrawn from US 2014 → ciprofloxacin; duration controversy (5–7d vs. shorter, 2025 systematic review) (5 sources)
- [[vonoprazan]] — Potassium-competitive acid blocker (PCAB); H. pylori dual/triple therapy (FDA-approved 2022); potent acid suppression; no CYP2C19 dependency; AGA 2024 P-CAB CPU positioning (severe erosive esophagitis (EE), H. pylori (HP), PUD) (2 sources)
- [[potassium-competitive-acid-blockers]] — P-CAB drug class (vonoprazan et al.); AGA 2024 Best Practice Advice — not first-line where superiority unproven (cost/safety); **use for severe EE Los Angeles (LA) C/D & in place of PPIs for H. pylori**; not first-line NERD/mild EE/PUD (2 sources)
- [[proton-pump-inhibitors]] — PPI drug class; covalent H+/K+-ATPase inhibition; mainstay for GERD/PUD/H. pylori; contrast with P-CABs; CDI risk (continue if indicated, never dose-reduce); **full de-prescribing framework (AGA 2022)** — the definite/conditional/not-indicated grid split by >8 wk vs ≤8 wk, the UGIB high-risk criteria that forbid stopping, BID→daily step-down, rebound acid hypersecretion, and BPA 10 (an adverse-event signal is never itself a reason to stop) (6 sources)
- [[eoe-therapies]] — Drug and diet therapy for eosinophilic esophagitis — **coverage-gap queue #14, cleared 2026-10-03.** The prescribing decision in one place: PPI at **double the approved reflux dose** (divided twice daily beats once daily, 53–54% vs 10–12%), swallowed topical steroids with their **formulations, vehicle and compounding** (budesonide oral suspension 2 mg BID, budesonide orodispersible 1 mg BID, fluticasone swallowed-not-inhaled from a metered-dose inhaler with the 100- vs 250-µg disk caveat), and **dupilumab by weight band** (≥40 kg = 300 mg **weekly**, not every other week). Carries the **8–12 wk induction** interval, the **<15 eos/hpf + EREFS ≤2** response targets, maintenance doses with their 48-wk remission rates, the 78% relapse-by-1-year-off-therapy figure, and candidiasis/adrenal-insufficiency safety. ⚠ Surfaces 3 **ACG 2025 vs AGA 2020 contradictions** (allergy-test-directed diet, anti-IL-4Rα, step-up vs 6FED) — ACG wins as the newer tier-1 source (2 sources)
- [[hbv-nucleoside-analogs]] — HBV nucleos(t)ide analogs — **coverage-gap queue #12, cleared 2026-10-03.** The three preferred agents (entecavir, TDF, TAF) with the **avoid rules that pick between them** — pregnancy and prior lamivudine exposure disqualify ETV, renal/bone disease disqualifies TDF, TAF is out at CrCl <15 mL/min not yet on dialysis — plus administration (tenofovir with food, ETV on an empty stomach), the switch-vs-add-on table for suboptimal virologic response, the four treatment-target endpoints, pregnancy/breastfeeding rules, the do-not-stop-until-HBsAg-loss default with its finite-prophylaxis exceptions, and monitoring intervals. **AASLD/IDSA 2025 prints no adult milligram doses or renal cut-points**, so the entecavir label was ingested 2026-10-20 to close it: ETV **0.5 mg daily**, **1 mg** for lamivudine/telbivudine resistance *or* decompensated disease, the **four-band CrCl renal table on both dose tracks** (adjust below 50 mL/min; dose after dialysis), no hepatic adjustment, and the three-part boxed warning. **Completed 2026-10-24** with the Viread and Vemlidy labels: TDF **300 mg daily** with interval extension below CrCl 50 mL/min (and no recommendation below 10 off dialysis), TAF **25 mg daily with food** with **no renal adjustment** above 15 mL/min or on dialysis, plus TDF's renal/bone monitoring and TAF's Child-Pugh B/C restriction (10 sources)
- [[givosiran]] — ALAS1-targeting small interfering RNA (siRNA) for recurrent acute hepatic porphyria (≥4 attacks/year); SC monthly; biochem+genetic confirmation, avoid in pregnancy; monitor ALT/eGFR (1 source)
- [[hemin]] — IV heme therapy for acute AHP attacks & prophylaxis; 3–4 mg/kg/day ×~4 days via high-flow central vein (1 source)
- [[linaclotide]] — Guanylate cyclase-C (GC-C) agonist; IBS-C 290 µg, CIC 145 µg; diarrhea-limiting (4 sources)
- [[plecanatide]] — GC-C agonist (uroguanylin analog); IBS-C 3/6 mg, CIC 3 mg (4 sources)
- [[prucalopride]] — Selective serotonin type 4 receptor (5-HT4) agonist; CIC 2 mg daily; no CV signal (3 sources)
- [[pamoras]] — PAMORA drug class (naldemedine, naloxegol, methylnaltrexone): peripheral μ-opioid receptor blockade that restores enteric function without crossing into the CNS, so analgesia is preserved; the class gate is **laxative-refractory** OIC; avoid where the blood–brain barrier is compromised (risk of withdrawal or reversal of anesthesia); per-agent GRADE and efficacy live on [[opioid-induced-constipation]] (1 source)
- [[tenapanor]] — First-in-class sodium/hydrogen exchanger 3 (NHE3) inhibitor (apical Na⁺/H⁺ exchanger; locally acting, minimally absorbed); IBS-C **50 mg by mouth (PO) b.i.d.** (Conditional/moderate, AGA 2022 Rec 1); 3 RCTs, pooled n=1,372 — FDA responder 34.1% vs 21.7%; efficacy maintained to 26 wk; diarrhea-limiting (3 sources)
- [[alosetron]] — Serotonin type 3 receptor (5-HT3) antagonist; severe IBS-D in women, Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS) (ischemic colitis risk) (2 sources)
- [[eluxadoline]] — μ-/κ-opioid agonist + δ-antagonist for IBS-D; 100 mg BID (75 mg if hepatic impairment/OATP1B1 inhibitor/intolerance); contraindicated with no gallbladder, >3 alcohol/day, pancreatitis or SOD history; not in Child-Pugh C (2 sources)
- [[tegaserod]] — 5-HT4 agonist; IBS-C in women <65 with ≤1 CV risk factor (2 sources)
- [[fmt]] — Fecal microbiota-based therapies for recurrent C. difficile (conventional FMT + FDA products); immunocompromise gate; earlier-use criteria, 1–4 d washout, multi-dose lower-endoscopy delivery and mortality RR 0.37/NNT 4 in severe-fulminant CDI; against routine IBD/IBS use (2 sources)
- [[loperamide]] — Loperamide: peripheral μ-opioid antimotility; **4 mg → 2 mg per loose stool; ceiling 8 mg/day self-treatment vs 16 mg/24 h with antibiotics for traveler's diarrhea** (the qualifier is the decision); indications table; avoid <18 y and in febrile/inflammatory diarrhea; CDI antimotility contraindications (6 sources)
- [[nitazoxanide]] — Nitazoxanide: antiprotozoal; role-by-pathogen table (Cryptosporidium first choice — in HIV only with effective combination antiretroviral therapy (cART); Giardia first choice with tinidazole; Cyclospora/Cystoisospora alternatives); persistent/nonresponder diarrhea. **Dose unsourced corpus-wide and deliberately not stated — needs a Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC)/IDSA parasitic treatment table** (2 sources)
- [[botulinum-toxin]] — Botulinum Toxin (GI indications) — **coverage-gap queue #35, cleared 2026-10-19.** One agent, a different stance at every site: achalasia **100 U above the squamocolumnar junction in 0.5–1 mL aliquots**, reserved for patients unfit for definitive therapy, with the full relief-decay table (78.7% at 30 d → **40.6% at 12 mo**), 46.6% reinjection and 30% progression to definitive therapy; **intrapyloric injection recommended against for gastroparesis (Strong, Moderate — the firmest grading it carries anywhere)**; anal fissure second-line after topical CCB (healing 60–80%, recurrence up to 42%, temporary incontinence ~18%); one of the less-invasive options to exhaust before myotomy in nonachalasia spastic disorders; and the sites where evidence does **not** support it (defecation disorders, chronic proctalgia). Carries the FLIP contractile-response predictor (odds ratio 22.5) (8 sources)
- [[lactulose]] — Lactulose: mechanism; indications (overt HE treatment vs secondary prophylaxis vs primary prophylaxis, and where a source recommends against); oral and **enema** dosing with titration to the bowel-movement-per-day endpoint; nonresponse; the over-titration failure mode (dehydration/hyponatremia/hypokalemia, which itself precipitates HE); combination with [[rifaximin]]; alternatives, stopping rules, special situations; and its diagnostic use as a breath-testing substrate (11 sources)
- [[rifaximin]] — Rifaximin: non-absorbable antibiotic; **SIBO dose differs by society — AGA 800–1200 mg/d in divided doses vs ACG 550 mg three times daily (TID) (1,650 mg/d), a ~40% gap** — course **7–10 d (AGA)**, which closes a duration the page had explicitly flagged as unsourced (the former "× 14 d" here was never in ACG); IMO + neomycin combination; IBS-D 550 mg TID × 14d; HE add-on to lactulose (never monotherapy); **ACG 2026 reverses the 2014 rule — start 14 days before elective TIPS and continue ≥6 months** (contradiction surfaced on page) (7 sources)
- [[glp-1-receptor-agonists]] — GLP-1 receptor agonist (RA) **drug class**: incretin mimetics with two GI-relevant consequences — AGA-endorsed anti-obesity medications, and they slow gastric emptying (drives GI side effects + the periprocedural aspiration question). AGA 2022 obesity Recs 1–3 with grades, semaglutide 2.4 mg weekly / liraglutide 3.0 mg daily dosing + titration, magnitude of weight loss, pre-endoscopy guidance. Agent-level detail on [[semaglutide]] (2 sources)
- [[semaglutide]] — GLP-1 RA (Wegovy/Ozempic/Rybelsus); FDA-approved Aug 2025 for MASH; ESSENCE trial efficacy; NIT-based candidacy (VCTE/MRE/ELF cutoffs); full safety table (10 domains); cardiometabolic benefit (SELECT/FLOW/STEP trials); concomitant resmetirom use; investigational/off-label for alcohol use disorder (phase 2 RCT) (4 sources)
- [[resmetirom]] — THR-β agonist (Rezdiffra); first FDA-approved MASH F2–F3 drug (Mar 2024); MAESTRO-NASH; dosing 100/80 mg + CYP2C8/statin DDI; thyroid/gallstone pretreatment; 12-mo futility (VCTE ≥25%/MRE ≥20%/MRI proton density fat fraction (MRI-PDFF) <30%) (2 sources)
- [[tacrolimus]] — Tacrolimus (FK506): calcineurin inhibitor (CNI) backbone post-LT; 2025 trough targets (mo1: 7–10, mo2–12: 4–8, mo12+: 4 ng/mL); extended-release (XR) equivalence (Rec 31); mTOR for HCC/CKD; Paxlovid DDI; nephrotoxicity/post-transplant diabetes mellitus (PTDM)/neurotoxicity; special populations (2 sources)
- [[calcineurin-inhibitors]] — CNI drug class: TAC vs. cyclosporine (CSA) comparison; basiliximab induction (Rec 25, Strong); antithymocyte globulin (ATG) contraindicated (Rec 26, Strong); steroid minimization; IS minimization ladder (dose↓→MMF→mTOR→angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitor (ACEi)→kidney Tx); CSA DDIs (sofosbuvir/glecaprevir/voxilaprevir) (3 sources)


- [[probiotics]] — Probiotics: strain/dose-specific; AGA 2020 FOR (pouchitis, NEC), AGAINST (peds gastroenteritis), trial-only elsewhere; **C. difficile prevention reversed — not advised (AGA 2026)** (5 sources)
- [[bismuth-quadruple-therapy]] — ACG 2024 preferred first-line H. pylori eradication: optimized components/doses (metronidazole 1,500–2,000 mg/day), indications, efficacy (2 sources)
- [[direct-acting-antivirals]] — DAA drug classes (NS3/4A + NS5A + NS5B); pangenotypic regimens with doses (G/P 300/120 mg with food, SOF/VEL 400/100 mg); decompensated defined as CTP ≥7 current or prior; eGFR <30 exclusion; HBsAg check before therapy; SVR at 12 weeks (SVR12) timing and post-SVR HCC surveillance; DDI table; see [[hepatitis-c]] for treatment tables (2 sources)
- [[somatostatin-analogs]] — Octreotide/lanreotide: SSTR2/5 agonism; acute variceal bleeding (octreotide 50 mcg bolus → 25–50 mcg/hr × 2–5 d); GEP-NET symptom + antiproliferative control and carcinoid crisis; refractory small-bowel vascular bleeding (octreotide long-acting release (LAR) 90 mg monthly × 12 mo); biliary/glycemic adverse effects (4 sources)
- [[glp-2-agonists]] — Glucagon-like peptide-2 (GLP-2) agonists (teduglutide): trophic to intestinal mucosa; enhance adaptation + facilitate PN weaning in short bowel syndrome; see [[nutrition-in-ibd]] (3 sources)
- [[albumin]] — IV albumin in cirrhosis: indications and dosing for large-volume paracentesis, SBP, and AKI/hepatorenal syndrome (HRS) (2 source)
- [[ursodeoxycholic-acid]] — Ursodeoxycholic acid — **coverage-gap queue #11, cleared 2026-09-24.** First-line bile acid for [[primary-biliary-cholangitis|PBC]], dosed **by indication**: PBC and PBC–AIH overlap **13–15 mg/kg/d** (superior to both 5–7 and 23–25 mg/kg/d), PSC 13–23 mg/kg/d *may be considered*, ICP 10–15 mg/kg/d divided, recurrent PBC post-transplant 10–15 mg/kg/d. Carries the **hard high-dose harm signal in PSC — do not use ≥28 mg/kg/d** (trial terminated early for futility; excess transplant/varices/death and increased colorectal neoplasia in PSC–UC). Now the **one home for the PBC biochemical-response criteria** (Paris I/II, Toronto, Barcelona, Rotterdam, Rochester I/II, Global) with their real cut-points, all assessed at **12 months**; second-line escalation with obeticholic acid **5 → 10 mg/d** and its advanced-cirrhosis contraindication, plus off-label bezafibrate 400 mg/d and fenofibrate 160 mg/d; and a table of indications where UDCA is **not** recommended (11 sources)
- [[terlipressin]] — Terlipressin — **coverage-gap queue #9, cleared 2026-09-22.** V1 agonist for **HRS-AKI**, gated by four things that must all be true before the first dose: HRS-AKI criteria met, the **2-day albumin 1 g/kg challenge** failed, precipitants addressed, and **ICA-AKI Stage 2+** (no recommendation exists for Stage 1). Carries all four dosing regimens side by side — AGA 2022 bolus (1 mg q4–6h → 2 mg if <25% serum creatinine (SCr) fall by day 3, max 12 mg/d), US FDA/CONFIRM (1 mg q6h → 2 mg day 4 if <30% fall), AASLD 2024 GS 25 (0.5–2.0 mg q6h), and continuous infusion from **2 mg/24h** (Baveno's preferred route) — with the **14-day cap**, albumin co-dosing (1 g/kg day 1 → 20–40 g/day), response/futility/stop rules, and the contraindication set (**ACLF-3, SpO₂ <90%, active ischemia, SCr ≥5 mg/dL, pregnancy**). The **respiratory-failure signal** is tabled with all three circulating CONFIRM rates rather than one. ⚠ AASLD 2024 GS 25 prints the infusion dose as "2 **g**/24h" — a units error, corrected on-page and on [[acute-on-chronic-liver-failure]] (6 sources)
- [[nonselective-beta-blockers]] — Nonselective beta-blockers (NSBBs): conventional (propranolol, nadolol) vs carvedilol, and why the modern indication is **CSPH rather than varices** — the goal is preventing decompensation, not only preventing a bleed. Full AASLD dose/titration table with its qualifiers (heart rate 55–60 bpm for conventional agents only; carvedilol titrated to blood pressure — SBP ≥90 mm Hg and, per ASGE 2026, MAP >65 mm Hg — cap 12.5 mg/day; maximum halves with ascites for conventional agents only), the two differing contraindication lists (AASLD bradycardia <50 bpm vs ASGE 2026 <55 bpm), stop/hold/restart rules, PREDESCI, and the Baveno VII→VIII changes (4 sources)
- [[anti-tnf-agents]] — Anti-TNF class (infliximab, adalimumab, certolizumab pegol, golimumab) — **coverage-gap queue #1, cleared 2026-09-04.** The longest-established advanced therapy in IBD and the drug class behind most of the wiki's combination-therapy, immunogenicity and pre-biologic-screening rules. Induction/maintenance dosing, positioning in CD and UC, ASUC rescue parameters, combination therapy with [[thiopurines]], pre-treatment tuberculosis (TB) and HBV screening, therapeutic drug monitoring for loss of response, biosimilars — plus the **non-IBD indication**: infliximab 5 mg/kg at weeks 0/2/6 for glucocorticoid-refractory [[immune-checkpoint-inhibitor-colitis|ICI colitis]], avoided in hematologic malignancy and cautioned in [[immune-checkpoint-inhibitor-hepatitis|ICI hepatitis]] (6 sources)
- [[il-23-and-il-12-23-inhibitors]] — IL-23 and IL-12/23 inhibitor class (ustekinumab = anti-p40; risankizumab, mirikizumab, guselkumab = anti-p19) — **coverage-gap queue #3, cleared 2026-09-06.** All four are Strong/Moderate for induction *and* maintenance in both UC and CD (ACG 2025). **Dosing differs by indication, not just by drug** — risankizumab 1200 mg IV in UC vs 600 mg IV in CD; mirikizumab IV in UC, SC in CD; guselkumab has two separate CD regimens. The one evidence-based intra-class split is **risankizumab over ustekinumab after anti-TNF exposure in CD** (SEQUENCE: endoscopic remission 32% vs 16% at wk 48; ACG Rec 19, Conditional/Low). SEAVUE (ustekinumab = adalimumab in bio-naïve CD) is why ustekinumab sits alongside anti-TNF, not behind it. AGA 2024 UC tiers mirikizumab a rung below ustekinumab while ACG 2025 grades them equally — surfaced. No malignancy signal; vaccine-response data absent for the p19 agents (6 sources)
- [[vedolizumab]] — Gut-selective α4β7 anti-integrin for UC, Crohn’s, chronic pouchitis and ICI colitis — **coverage-gap queue #2, cleared 2026-09-04.** Best safety profile of the advanced therapies (no systemic immunosuppression, no progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML)), slower onset, and an efficacy tier that **inverts with prior anti-TNF exposure** (higher-efficacy in the naïve patient, lower after anti-TNF). Carries the guidelines’ verbatim recommendations and GRADE ratings, the ACG-2025-vs-AGA-2025 Crohn’s disagreement (Strong/permits-first-line vs Conditional/lower-efficacy — both 2025, neither supersedes), and the **corrected IV induction schedule: 300 mg at weeks 0 and 2 — no ingested source states a week-6 dose for IBD** (12 sources)
- [[mesalamine-5-asa]] — 5-ASA class (mesalamine, sulfasalazine, olsalazine) — **coverage-gap queue #5, cleared 2026-09-08.** UC dosing is selected by **disease extent**, and induction ≠ maintenance: proctitis → rectal 1 g/d; left-sided → rectal ≥1 g/d **plus** oral ≥2.0 g/d (the oral+rectal combination is left-sided only); extensive → oral ≥2.0 g/d; maintenance oral **≥1.5 g/d**. In mildly active disease **2.0–2.4 g is preferred over 4.8 g** (Rec 15, scoped to *mild* only). **The formulation trap:** Apriso is US-approved for maintenance at 1.5 g/d but **not for induction**. On failure, do not switch formulation — reclassify as moderate-severe. **Crohn's: recommended against, Strong/Moderate** (Rec 4), sulfasalazine surviving only for symptomatic mild *colonic* CD at 3–6 g/d. Version deltas that change practice: the UC oral maintenance floor **dropped from ≥2 g/d (2019) to ≥1.5 g/d (2025)**, and ACG 2025 **withdrew** 2018's postoperative-CD mesalamine option. ⚠ No renal/complete blood count (CBC) monitoring interval and no mention of interstitial nephritis exists anywhere in the corpus — flagged on-page, not supplied (11 sources)
- [[epinephrine]] — Epinephrine in GI practice — **coverage-gap queue #36, cleared 2026-10-22.** Owns the drug's numbers across three distinct uses: hemostatic injection **1:10,000 (= 100 µg/mL)** upper GI and **1:10,000 or 1:20,000 in 1-mL aliquots up to 2 mL** for diverticular bleeding (ASGE 2014 Rec 9, high quality), lifting-solution adjunct **1:200,000–1:500,000** for cold EMR (table range 1:50,000–1:500,000), and self-injectable epinephrine in alpha-gal syndrome. Carries the **never-as-monotherapy** rule with its relative risks (RR 2.20 alone vs standard monotherapies; RR 0.34 for adding a second modality) **and the counterweight** — AGA 2020 finds dual therapy no better than properly applied thermal or mechanical monotherapy — plus prophylaxis for pedunculated heads ≥20 mm / stalks ≥5 mm, the uncertain role in cold EMR, and the post-injection abdominal pain that mimics perforation (8 sources)
- [[cdiff-antibiotics]] — Antibiotics for *Clostridioides difficile* infection — **coverage-gap queue #13, cleared 2026-10-03.** The agent-level page behind [[clostridioides-difficile]]: fidaxomicin vs vancomycin vs metronidazole with the regimens and durations for first episode, severe and fulminant disease (including triple therapy, de-escalation timing and total duration), recurrence regimens (vancomycin taper-pulse, fidaxomicin EXTEND), antibiotic dosing around [[fmt|fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT)]], suppressive and prophylactic vancomycin, special populations (pregnancy and lactation, immunocompromise, IBD, renal disease), absorption/adverse effects/resistance, and what not to use (3 sources)
- [[corticosteroids-ibd]] — Corticosteroids in IBD (systemic, budesonide controlled ileal release (CIR), budesonide multimatrix (MMX)) — **coverage-gap queue #6, cleared 2026-09-20.** **Induction agents only** — every ingested guideline recommends *against* them for maintenance, systemic/budesonide/topical alike ([[acg-2025-uc]] Rec 20 Strong/Moderate; [[acg-2025-crohns]] Rec 6 Strong/Low, and budesonide **not beyond 4 months**). The decision is *which formulation*, set by disease, extent and severity, because **the two budesonides are not interchangeable**: CIR releases in the terminal ileum (ileocecal CD, **9 mg PO daily × 8–12 wk**, bioavailability ≈10%–20%, **ineffective for colonic CD**) while MMX releases across the colon (UC, **9 mg PO daily**). Prednisone **40–60 mg/d then taper 5 mg/wk**; ASUC **IV methylprednisolone 60 mg/d total or hydrocortisone 100 mg TID/four times daily (QID)** (Rec 50). Carries the quantified 5-ASA trial that must fail first (**oral ≥2 g/d and rectal ≥1 g/d** — [[acg-2019-uc]] Rec 9, which [[acg-2025-uc]] restates without the numbers), the severity gate (budesonide MMX is *mild–moderate* only), steroid dependence defined by **behaviour on taper, not a day-count or milligram threshold**, fracture risk from **prednisone 2.5 mg/d** upward, the preoperative **≤20 mg/d** target, and the inverted autoimmune-hepatitis rule set (6 sources)
- [[methotrexate]] — Methotrexate — **coverage-gap queue #7, cleared 2026-09-21.** **The indication is disease-specific and asymmetric:** suggested for **CD maintenance after corticosteroid induction** (up to **25 mg once weekly intramuscularly or SC** — [[acg-2025-crohns]] Rec 11, Conditional/Moderate; dose may drop to **15 mg/wk** after 4 steroid-free months; oral 15–25 mg/wk only with normal small-bowel absorption), and recommended **against in UC** — both induction monotherapy and maintenance, the negative **MERIT-UC** trial showing relapse **29/44 (66%)** on methotrexate vs **25/40 (63%)** on placebo. Third role: **adjunctive at 12.5–15 mg PO weekly to suppress [[anti-tnf-agents|anti-TNF]] immunogenicity** — not a disease-controlling dose, and off-label. **Contraindicated in pregnancy.** ⚠ Neither ACG guideline states a folic acid dose or schedule; hepatotoxicity thresholds live on [[drug-induced-liver-injury]] (4 sources)
- [[jak-inhibitors]] — Oral JAK–signal transducer and activator of transcription (STAT) small molecules in IBD (tofacitinib = pan-JAK, UC only; upadacitinib = JAK-1 selective, UC **and** CD; filgotinib = JAK-1 selective, UC, **Europe only — not available in the US**) — **coverage-gap queue #4, created by the 2026-09-07 cron, which then left it out of this index and as a whole-wiki orphan with 0 inbound links until 2026-09-08.** Upadacitinib carries the **highest evidence grade of any UC induction therapy except infliximab** (Strong/High), yet the class is the only one in IBD that is *regulatorily gated*: the FDA label reserves every JAK inhibitor for patients who have failed or cannot tolerate a TNF antagonist. That gate traces to **ORAL Surveillance — a rheumatoid-arthritis trial, not an IBD one** — and the criteria defining who it actually targets are the operative content of the page, alongside per-drug/per-indication induction and maintenance dosing, extended induction, the boxed-warning risk factors, and pre-treatment screening/monitoring (7 sources)
- [[thiopurines]] — Thiopurine **immunomodulator class** (azathioprine, 6-mercaptopurine (6-MP)) in IBD — closes a long-standing corpus gap. **Two roles only:** maintenance of corticosteroid-induced remission, and combination with infliximab to suppress immunogenicity — **never induction** (onset 8–12 wk; ACG 2025 CD Rec 8 and UC Rec 23 both recommend *against* induction monotherapy). Carries the ACG grade table by setting, dosing, pre-treatment thiopurine methyltransferase (TPMT)/NUDT15 pharmacogenomic testing, and the malignancy/monitoring profile (4 sources)
