## Concepts

- [[brain-gut-behavioral-therapy]] — Psychogastroenterology's treatment arm: cognitive-behavior therapy vs gut-directed hypnotherapy (protocols, number needed to treat, session counts), the assessment instruments, the two lay-language patient scripts, provider taxonomy and referral pathway, the comorbid-psychopathology sequencing rule, and cost/coverage (1 source)

- [[esophageal-dysfunction-systemic-disease]] — Esophageal dysfunction in systemic immune/inflammatory disease: hypereosinophilic syndrome (HES)/eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis (EGPA) (absolute eosinophil count (AEC) >1500), connective-tissue disease (systemic sclerosis (SSc)/mixed connective tissue disease (MCTD)/systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE)/Sjögren/myositis), esophageal Crohn's, dermatologic (esophageal lichen planus (ELP)/pemphigus, direct immunofluorescence (DIF)), secondary achalasia (COVID/Chagas/eosinophilic); high index of suspicion for nonspecific endoscopy (AGA 2024) (1 source)
- [[hepatic-venous-pressure-gradient]] — HVPG: gold-standard sinusoidal portal-pressure measurement (wedged hepatic venous pressure (WHVP)−free hepatic venous pressure (FHVP)); balloon-occlusion technique, interpretation ladder (normal 1–5; >5 sinusoidal PH; **≥10 CSPH; ≥16 non-hepatic surgery mortality; ≥20 at bleeding → pre-emptive TIPS; <10 off NSBB confirms CSPH resolution after recompensation**), NSBB response = fall ≥10% or to ≤12 mmHg, Baveno VIII's NIT-first demotion, limitations (underestimates presinusoidal PH and MASLD gradients → EUS-PPG) (4 sources)
- [[li-rads]] — LI-RADS: applicability (cirrhosis / at-risk HBV only), major features (arterial phase hyperenhancement (APHE), nonperipheral washout, capsule, threshold growth), LR-5 size criteria, category-by-category management (LR-3 → 3–6 mo imaging; LR-4 → multidisciplinary discussion (MDD) ± biopsy; LR-5 = HCC, no biopsy; LR-M → biopsy mandatory), MRI vs CT performance, plus the **lexicon definitions that decide a category** (size = outer-edge-to-outer-edge; the three limbs of subthreshold growth incl. a new mass of any size; washout vs fade; washout assessed before the transitional phase with a hepatobiliary agent) (6 sources)
- [[colonoscopy-surveillance]] — Colonoscopy surveillance intervals (USMSTF 2020): adenoma + serrated-polyp + serial-surveillance interval tables; high-quality-exam prerequisite; 1-2 small adenomas extended to 7-10y; definitions (advanced/high-risk adenoma) (2 source)
- [[artificial-intelligence-endoscopy]] — Artificial intelligence (AI) in endoscopy: computer-aided detection (CADe; ADR RR 1.43 in RCTs, inconsistent real-world; AGA 2025 no recommendation), computer-aided diagnosis (CADx; optical diagnosis; ASGE Preservation and Incorporation of Valuable Endoscopic Innovations (PIVI) ≥90% NPV / >90% surveillance-agreement thresholds for resect-and-discard/diagnose-and-leave), computer-aided quality assessment; adoption barriers (2 sources)
- [[hcc-surveillance]] — HCC Surveillance: at-risk populations (Child-Pugh A/B cirrhosis + select non-cirrhotic HBV), semiannual US+AFP (sens 63% vs 51%), CT/MRI not routine, harms, GALAD/biomarkers not yet ready + no MCD panels, PAGE-B/REAL-B (point values plus the **5-year HCC incidence each band implies — 0% / 3% / 17%** — and why the trigger is ≥10), the LI-RADS US category and VIS-A/B/C tables with the recall algorithm, prevent cirrhosis (6 sources)
- [[pancreatic-cancer-screening]] — High-risk pancreatic cancer surveillance: eligibility criteria and family-history rules by gene, risk magnitudes, condition-specific start ages, EUS/MRI choice, annual interval, what an abnormal finding triggers, yield/harms; ACG 2015 vs ASGE 2022 comparison; who NOT to screen (3 sources)
- [[mri-mrcp]] — MRI/MRCP modality page: technique standards (1.5-T min, 3-T for small pancreatic lesions, T2w 3D 1-mm slices); limitations (normal MRCP misses 30% of high-pretest PSC; EUS better for small solid pancreatic lesions; go straight to ERCP if cholangitis/high pretest stricture); routing table for where MRCP carries the decision (choledocholithiasis, PSC dx + CCA surveillance, pancreatic cysts, post-LT strictures, high-risk pancreatic screening) (7 sources)
- [[treat-to-target-ibd]] — Treat-to-target in IBD (Selecting Therapeutic Targets in Inflammatory Bowel Disease II [STRIDE-II]): the immediate / intermediate / long-term target framework, what counts as a target vs a monitoring measure, the formal targets for CD and UC (clinical response → clinical remission + normalized C-reactive protein (CRP)/calprotectin → endoscopic healing, with normalized QoL and absent disability), why histologic healing and transmural healing are **not** formal targets, and the pediatric additions (growth, development) (1 source)
- [[low-fodmap-diet]] — Low-FODMAP diet in IBS: the three phases (restriction → structured reintroduction → personalization) with the restriction-phase duration, who is a poor candidate, why an registered dietitian nutritionist (RDN) should deliver it, the predetermined trial duration and stop rule, and the nutritional-adequacy risks of staying in restriction (2 sources)
- [[brca-pathogenic-variants]] — BRCA1/2 germline variants and pancreatic cancer risk (2 sources)
- [[brush-cytology]] — ERCP-based exfoliative cytology for biliary strictures (2 sources)
- [[fish]] — FISH cytogenetic adjunct to biliary brush cytology — carries the two probe sets (UroVysion vs pancreatobiliary 1q21/7p12/8q24/9p21), the **cytogenetic definition of polysomy** (≥3 copies of ≥2 probes; **≥4 cells on the whole slide**, an absolute count not a percentage), the equivocal categories with their 100-cell cut-offs, and the head-to-head sensitivity (PB FISH 64.7% vs UroVysion 45.9% vs cytology 18.8%) (3 sources)
- [[confocal-laser-endomicroscopy]] — Probe-based CLE for benign vs. malignant biliary strictures (2 sources)
- [[liver-biopsy]] — Hepatic tissue sampling: indications, percutaneous/transjugular/EUS-guided approaches, coagulation assessment, complications, specimen adequacy (4 sources)
- [[liver-stiffness-measurement]] — LSM/elastography (VCTE, point shear wave elastography (pSWE), 2-dimensional shear wave elastography (2D-SWE), MRE): the noninvasive backbone of cACLD/CSPH staging; TE cut-off anchors (<10 exclude cACLD, ≤15+plt≥150 exclude CSPH, <20+plt≥150 spares EGD, ≥25 rule-in CSPH; **SSM >55 rule-in / <40 spares EGD / <25 with LSM <15 after recompensation**), 12-mo monitoring, "most recent value governs", relative-change thresholds, confounders; full algorithm on portal-hypertension (4 sources)
- [[endoscopy-ergonomics]] — Endoscopy-related injury (ERI): 57.7% pooled rate; female sex/volume risk; neutral posture, ergonomics education, micro/macrobreaks, monitor/bed height, antifatigue mats (1 source)
- [[electrosurgery]] — Electrosurgery in therapeutic endoscopy: cut/coag physics (duty cycle, crest factor, >100°C vs <100°C), current density and the non-generator factors that move it, monopolar vs bipolar, analog vs microprocessor electrosurgical units (ESUs), ESU settings by indication × manufacturer (Olympus/ConMed/ERBE), dispersive pad + cardiac-device safety, gas-vs-fluid environment, soft-coagulation deep injury, application-specific current choice (polypectomy, avulsion, sphincterotomy, third-space) (1 source)

- [[bristol-stool-form-scale]] — Bristol Stool Form Scale: the **7 type descriptions verbatim** (1 separate hard lumps → 7 watery, no solid pieces) and the transit direction they encode, the evidence that **stool form predicts whole-gut transit better than defecation frequency** (r = −0.54 vs 0.35; change r = −0.65 vs 0.41) and is the least variable bowel measure (coefficient of variation 13.6%), the **types 2-vs-5 confusion** the authors themselves flag, and a pointer table to every page where a Bristol threshold gates a decision. Single home for the scale — IBS subtyping, lactulose titration, constipation and diarrhea definitions all link here instead of reproducing it (1 source + 4 that use it)
- [[chicago-classification-v4]] — Chicago Classification v4.0: HRM protocol, manufacturer-specific IRP thresholds (Medtronic/Laborie), achalasia I/II/III, EGJOO always inconclusive (requires TBE/FLIP + symptoms), IEM revised criteria, fragmented peristalsis eliminated (2 sources)
- [[london-classification]] — London Classification (IAPWG 2020) of anorectal function: four parts (rectoanal inhibitory reflex, anal tone/contractility, rectoanal coordination, rectal sensation) as decision trees, major/minor/inconclusive tiering after the Chicago model, all 13 named diagnoses with their triggers, the fixed terminology (hypotension/hypocontractility/propulsion/dyssynergia/hyposensitivity); **publishes no reference values by design** — every cut-point is the lab's own ULN/LLN; cough and long squeeze excluded from the classification (1 source)
- [[colorectal-cancer-screening]] — CRC screening: age 45–75 (conditional 45–49), colonoscopy + annual FIT preferred, blood-based screening (Shield/Freenome — Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) criteria, positioning vs FIT/colonoscopy), CIR ≥95%, ADR ≥25%, family history stratification, aspirin 50–69 conditional (5 sources)
- [[anal-cancer-screening]] — Anal cancer screening: target is anal high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesion (HSIL) not cancer; International Anal Neoplasia Society (IANS) Risk Category A (≥17/100,000 p-y — screen, per-group start ages) vs Category B (<10 — shared decision-making); cytology / high-risk human papillomavirus (hrHPV) / co-testing strategies and their high-resolution anoscopy (HRA)-referral rules; digital anal rectal examination (DARE) at every visit; the National Institutes of Health (NIH)/CDC/IDSA pathway for people with HIV and its disagreement with IANS on primary hrHPV; what to do where HRA is unavailable (1 source)
- [[ibd-preventive-care]] — IBD health maintenance: vaccination schedule by agent/age incl. hepatitis B (HepB) seroprotection algorithm, cervical/skin/anal cancer screening, osteoporosis dual-energy x-ray absorptiometry (DEXA), depression/anxiety screening (3 sources)
- [[endoscopic-oncology]] — Endoscopic oncology subspecialty: scope (ESD/EMR/EUS staging/biliary palliation), relationship to surgical and medical oncology, training pathway (3 sources)
- [[disorders-of-gut-brain-interaction]] — DGBI framework: Rome V definition, biopsychosocial model, 34 adult + 22 pediatric classifications (A–H), Rome Clinical Criteria (8-wk duration), IBS severity table, key Rome IV→V changes (1 source)
- [[hrm-antireflux-surgery]] — HRM before/after antireflux surgery: pre-ARS motor disorder screening, Padova Classification algorithm, post-fundoplication outflow obstruction (PFOO) definition, pseudoachalasia, post-ARS IRP norms (1 source)
- [[ambulatory-reflux-monitoring]] — Lyon Consensus 2.0 diagnostic framework: acid exposure time (AET) thresholds (>6% conclusive, <4% excludes, 4–6% inconclusive), reflux episode counts, mean nocturnal baseline impedance (MNBI), symptom association probability (SAP)/symptom index (SI), wireless vs. pH-impedance test selection (2 sources)
- [[reflux-testing]] — GERD diagnostic testing: pH-metry, multichannel intraluminal impedance (MII)-pH impedance, Bravo wireless, endoscopic gastroesophageal junction (GEJ) assessment (Hill grade, AFS, LA/Prague), HRM role, CYP2C19 genotyping, diagnostic algorithm (3 sources)
- [[anticoagulation-gi-bleeding]] — Anticoagulation in GI bleeding and periendoscopic period: 4-factor prothrombin complex concentrate (4F-PCC) over FFP for warfarin; no bridging (BRIDGE trial — doubles bleeding, non-inferior for thromboembolism, except mechanical valves); platelet transfusion increases mortality (OR 5.57); resume aspirin day of hemostasis; DOAC interruption 1–2 days pre-procedure; drug-specific hold-interval/reversal reference table (2 sources)
- [[test-and-treat]] — Test-and-treat strategy for H. pylori in uninvestigated dyspepsia (3 sources)
- [[gastric-polyps]] — Gastric Polyps: subtype table (fundic gland polyp (FGP)/gastric hyperplastic polyp (GHP)/gastric adenoma (GA)/pyloric & oxyntic gland adenoma/gastric neuroendocrine tumor (G-NET)) with background mucosa & malignant potential; evaluate polyp + field; test/treat H. pylori; resect largest; surveillance keyed to histology + GIM/atrophic gastritis (3 sources)
- [[inflammatory-bowel-disease]] — IBD overview: Crohn's vs. UC comparison table, shared biologic classes, DILI in IBD, inpatient-management framework (admission, venous thromboembolism (VTE) prophylaxis, discharge), links to disease scripts (1 source)
- [[nutrition-in-ibd]] — Diet & nutritional therapy in IBD: diet-by-clinical-state table, Mediterranean baseline, exclusive enteral nutrition (EEN)/Crohn's disease exclusion diet (CDED) induction (CD), PN indications/thresholds + SBS/GLP-2, malnutrition screening (drop serum proteins), vitamin D/iron/B12 monitoring, registered dietitian (RD) co-management (1 source)
- [[iron-overload-and-iron-metabolism]] — Iron metabolism: hepcidin-ferroportin axis, intestinal absorption physiology, primary vs. secondary iron overload pathophysiology, key lab interpretation (1 source)
- [[intestinal-ultrasound]] — Intestinal ultrasound (IUS) for IBD monitoring: bowel wall thickness (BWT) + Doppler hyperemia metrics, comparable to magnetic resonance enterography (MRE)/CTE, treat-to-target thresholds (CD ≥25% BWT reduction; UC BWT <2.8mm, ASUC ≥20% at 48h), pregnancy/comorbid/obese populations, no dysplasia surveillance (1 source)
- [[small-bowel-motility]] — Small bowel motility: migrating motor complex (MMC) phase III as protective against SIBO, dysmotility causes and SIBO risk (1 source)
- [[operational-tolerance]] — Operational tolerance: IS-free allograft acceptance; ~7%/year increase in meta-analysis (n=620); biopsy pre-withdrawal; AASLD 2025 Rec 36 against routine withdrawal (Strong, Level 4) (2 sources)
- [[nutrition-in-hospitalized-patients]] — Nutrition in hospitalized patients: enteral nutrition (EN) preferred over PN when gut functional, Nutritional Risk Screening 2002 (NRS-2002)/Nutrition Risk in the Critically Ill (NUTRIC) risk scoring, avoid albumin/prealbumin as nutrition markers, EN within 24–48h for high-risk, permissive underfeeding in early acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS)/obesity, no gastric residual volume (GRV) monitoring, PN deferred 7–10d in low-risk if EN not feasible, end-of-life (EOL) autonomy governs nutrition; endoscopic enteral access (30-day nasoenteric-vs-percutaneous rule, PEGJ/DPEJ indications, PEG complications) (3 sources)
- [[cirrhosis-hemostasis]] — Hemostasis in cirrhosis: rebalanced hemostasis concept, INR/platelet count poor predictors of bleeding, no prophylactic FFP/platelets before routine procedures, thromboelastography (TEG)/rotational thromboelastometry (ROTEM) preferred for transfusion guidance, PVT paradox; full AGA 2021 population, intervention, comparison, outcome (PICO) 1A–6 table with the operative qualifiers (INR >1.8/plt <50k study definition, 1.5% low-vs-high bleeding-risk split, thrombopoietin receptor agonist (TPO-RA) ~1% 30-day thrombosis, VTE 0.5–1.9%) and the guideline's own admission that no validated cutoff exists (2 sources)
- [[palliative-care-in-cirrhosis]] — Palliative care in cirrhosis: the three misconceptions the AGA update names and rebuts (palliative care is not hospice; not mutually exclusive with transplant or other life-prolonging therapy; not only for decompensated disease — Child-Pugh class A patients already have measurable distress and below-norm quality-of-life scores), plus advance care planning, symptom-based management, and caregiver-fatigue screening (2 sources)
- [[noninvasive-liver-disease-assessment]] — NILDA: blood (FIB-4/NAFLD Fibrosis Score (NFS)/ELF) + imaging (VCTE/MRE/MRI-PDFF) tools for fibrosis/steatosis; two-tier FIB-4→VCTE/MRE/ELF pathway; rule-out > rule-in; ELF ≥11.3 prognostic; against blood steatosis indices & serial monitoring; advanced fibrosis (AF) rule-in cutoff/AUROC table incl. aspartate aminotransferase to platelet ratio index (APRI) >0.84 and shear wave elastography (SWE) >8.0 kPa; NPV is prevalence-dependent (96% at 10% → 73% at 50%); sequential FIB-4+VCTE rule-out/rule-in pair cuts biopsy 33%→19%; MRE + FIB-4 (MEFIB) > FibroScan-AST (FAST); biopsy triggers (indeterminate/discordant/conflicting/alternative etiology); hub for MASLD/portal-hypertension (HTN)/cholestatic staging (8 sources)
- [[endoscopy-sedation]] — Procedural sedation continuum for GI endoscopy: American Society of Anesthesiologists (ASA) class, nothing by mouth (NPO)/aspiration risk, GLP-1 RA before endoscopy (AGA individualized vs ASA hold), moderate sedation (benzodiazepine+opioid) vs propofol/monitored anesthesia care (MAC), capnography, reversal agents (2 sources)
- [[antibiotic-prophylaxis-endoscopy]] — When periprocedural antibiotics are/aren't indicated for endoscopy: not for infective endocarditis (IE) prevention; yes for incomplete biliary drainage, PEG/percutaneous endoscopic jejunostomy (PEJ), EUS-FNA of cysts, cirrhosis + GI bleed (2 sources)
- [[preprocedure-testing]] — Selective, risk-based laboratory testing before endoscopy: against routine coag/chest x-ray (CXR)/electrocardiogram (ECG)/type-screen/CBC/urinalysis (UA)/chem in healthy patients; test-by-test indications; pregnancy testing before fluoroscopy; abnormal partial thromboplastin time (PTT) does not predict bleeding (1 source)

---

- [[gastric-cancer-screening]] — GC screening/surveillance framework: who to screen, EGD+Sydney biopsy, intervals by atrophic gastritis (AG)/GIM/dysplasia (1 source)
- [[ibd-endoscopic-scoring]] — Endoscopic scoring indices for IBD: Mayo Endoscopic Score (MES), Ulcerative Colitis Endoscopic Index of Severity (UCEIS), Crohn's Disease Endoscopic Index of Severity (CDEIS), Simple Endoscopic Score for Crohn's Disease (SES-CD), modified multiplier SES-CD (MM-SES-CD), and modified Rutgeerts — components, point bands, and remission/response cutoffs (1 source)
- [[ibd-pain-management]] — Managing chronic abdominal pain in IBD: central vs peripheral drivers, neuromodulator dosing, opioid avoidance, and brain-gut behavior therapies (1 source)
- [[ibd-in-older-adults]] — IBD at age ≥60: mimics to exclude after 60 (CRC, ischemic colitis, SCAD, NSAID, radiation, microscopic colitis), elderly-onset phenotype (colonic CD 44%, left-sided UC 40%), drug-by-drug age-specific safety table (thiopurine nonmelanoma skin cancer (NMSC) 4.04 vs 0.66/1000 and lymphoproliferative 5.41 vs 0.37/1000 py; anti-TNF infection OR 2.28; tofacitinib zoster and VTE rates), frailty-vs-phenotype split on combination therapy, National Surgical Quality Improvement Program (NSQIP) surgical risk (34.5% vs 21.3%), IPAA-vs-end-ostomy on sphincter tone, and the inputs for stopping dysplasia surveillance (1 source)
- [[ibd-in-malignancy]] — Cancer risk from IBD inflammation vs from IBD drugs, AGA 2024 Table 1 (what to do with each drug class when a lymphoma/melanoma/NMSC/solid/heme cancer develops), reassuring prior-cancer registry data, cancer-treatment effects on IBD (chemo, hormone therapy, ICI colitis) (2 source)
- [[ostomy-management]] — Ostomy types, output expectations, high-output ostomy (>1.5 L/d) definitions and treatment ladder, and peristomal/hernia/prolapse complications (1 source)
- [[therapeutic-drug-monitoring-ibd]] — Therapeutic drug monitoring in IBD: reactive vs proactive therapeutic drug monitoring (TDM), the three mechanisms of anti-TNF failure with their observed frequencies, and the trough × antidrug-antibody grid that decides **optimize vs switch within class vs switch class** — maintenance targets infliximab (IFX) ≥5, adalimumab (ADA) ≥7.5, certolizumab pegol (CZP) ≥20 µg/mL; antibody-titer interpretation; pre-treatment TPMT testing with its dose-reduction table; 6-thioguanine nucleotide (6-TGN) 230–450 pmol/8×10⁸ red blood cells (RBCs) (monotherapy only). Vedolizumab and ustekinumab explicitly excluded by the source (1 source)
- [[endoscope-reprocessing]] — Endoscope reprocessing and duodenoscope-transmitted infection: why high-level disinfection has almost no safety margin (10⁹–10¹² organisms at procedure end; precleaning −10³, manual washing −10⁶, leaving 10⁶–10⁹ to the germicide), why the duodenoscope elevator is the worst case, outbreak epidemiology, the reprocessing sequence, supplemental measures beyond standard reprocessing, monitoring/tracking, and sterilization vs high-level disinfection (2 sources)
- [[telemedicine-in-gastroenterology]] — Virtual visits in outpatient GI: the two CPU tables (appropriate uses of each format; best practices across seven categories), who is an excellent vs poor candidate, video-vs-telephone access disparities, the chronic-stable vs new-acute outcome split, and the licensure/documentation/billing constraints (place-of-service and modifier codes, the 2021 change dropping the physical-exam documentation requirement) (1 source)
- [[colonoscopy-quality-indicators]] — The governing **ASGE/ACG 2024** set: all **15 numbered indicators** with the task force's own grade, measure type and target, the **5 priority indicators**, and the exact numerator/denominator each uses — including the widened ADR denominator (screening + surveillance + diagnostic, age ≥45, four exclusions). Covers ADR ≥35%, adenomas per colonoscopy ≥0.6, sessile serrated lesion detection rate ≥6%, withdrawal ≥8 min, cecal intubation ≥95%, prep adequacy ≥90%, cold snare 4–9 mm ≥90%, lesion documentation and specimen-bottling rules, adverse-event review, interval adherence and the three IBD indicators; plus the remediation ladder, tiers of quality, interventions that raise ADR, and a **four-document comparison table** showing where the older AGA 2021 / ACG 2021 / USMSTF numbers and denominators differ (6 sources)
- [[ercp-quality-indicators]] — The governing **ACG/ASGE 2026** set: all **13 numbered indicators** with the task force's own grade, measure type and target, and the **5 priority indicators** (accepted indication >98%, deep cannulation of a native papilla >90%, extrahepatic stone extraction >90%, rectal NSAID with an intact papilla >90%, unplanned hospital visit <15%). Owns the denominators that decide whether a hard case counts against the operator — EUS-BD after failed cannulation and duodenal obstruction are failures, modified upper GI anatomy is excluded, and the stone denominator is no longer capped at 1 cm — plus the adverse-event benchmark table, the consensus post-ERCP pancreatitis definition and why PEP rates are not comparable between endoscopists, the rectal-NSAID and prophylactic-pancreatic-stent indicators, and the ALARA radiation measures. Grades are the task force's own 8-level scheme, **not GRADE** (3 = expert opinion) (3 sources)
- [[endoscopy-quality-indicators]] — The **base layer** of the ASGE/ACG quality series (**2024**): the **19 indicators that apply to every GI endoscopic procedure**, with grade, measure type and target, and the 4 priority indicators (appropriate indication, prophylactic antibiotics, antithrombotic plan, adverse-event documentation). Explains why almost the whole set is graded 3 at >98% — the task force treats failure to perform them as a **never event**, so variation was deliberately not the basis for inclusion. Owns the sedation plan/monitoring/fasting rules (ASA intervals incl. the fried-or-fatty-meal 8 h; residual gastric fluid <25 mL after bowel prep), the antithrombotic rules (aspirin 81–325 mg continued, cold snare on clopidogrel, the high-thromboembolic-risk list), the full antibiotic **not recommended** vs **recommended** table, the adverse-event definition/certainty/severity grading with the **perforation and bleeding rates by procedure**, the 20 minimal elements of an endoscopy report, photodocumentation landmarks, specimen verification, discharge criteria and credentialing (1 source)

## Sources

- [[aga-2018-psychogastroenterology]] — AGA 2018 Clinical Practice Update: incorporating psychogastroenterology into management of digestive disorders — 5 ungraded Best Practice Advice
- [[aga-2017-surgical-device-fecal-incontinence]] — AGA 2017 Clinical Practice Update: surgical and device-aided therapy for fecal incontinence and defecatory disorders — 15 ungraded Best Practice Advice
- [[aga-2018-esd-united-states]] — AGA 2018 Clinical Practice Update: endoscopic submucosal dissection in the United States (indexed 2026-09-30; the ingesting pass never added this row)
- [[aga-2019-esd-t1b-esophageal-cancer]] — AGA 2019 Clinical Practice Update (**Expert Review**): Utility of ESD in T1b Esophageal Cancer — Othman, Lee, Wang. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2019;17:2161–2166 — ⚠ **no numbered statements and no grading** (guidance is one closing prose section; the 6-item list on the source page is the wiki's numbering). Defines **SM1/SM2/SM3 by histology — 200 µm squamous vs 500 µm adenocarcinoma**; **lymph node metastasis (LNM) by depth and cell type** (SM1 27% squamous vs 6% adeno, SM2 36%/23%, SM3 55%/58%); Table 1 LNM predictors; endoscopic predictors of deep invasion (Paris 0-I 79% / 0-III 84% — the **opposite of the colon**, type B3 intrapapillary capillary loop, Lugol voiding lesion); **EUS overstages T1b** (pooled false-positive rate 35.85%) and **PET/CT is not needed in early-stage disease**; ideal endoscopic candidate = well-differentiated, <2 cm, SM1, no lymphovascular invasion; **duplicated muscularis mucosae invasion = T1a, not T1b**; AJCC 8th-ed grade modifier (T1a G2–G3 → stage IB)

### AASLD

- [[aasld-2025-hcc-critical-update]] — AASLD 2025 Critical Update: Adjuvant/Neoadjuvant Therapy After HCC Resection/Ablation (Taddei et al. Hepatology 2025) — Guidance Statement 32 Revised (Strong, Level 1): atezo+bev adjuvant contraindicated; IMbrave050 second interim negative; active surveillance standard
- [[aasld-2025-semaglutide-mash]] — AASLD 2025 Practice Guidance: Semaglutide for MASH — 20 guidance statements; FDA-approved Aug 2025; ESSENCE trial (first 800 participants); NIT candidacy framework (VCTE/MRE/ELF cutoffs) + cirrhosis-exclusion thresholds; safety table (10 domains); lean-mass loss stated with **both** denominators (~13% of baseline lean mass; ~39% of total weight lost)
- [[aasld-ast-2025-liver-transplant-candidate-evaluation]] — AASLD/AST 2025 Practice Guideline: Liver Transplantation Candidate Evaluation — 59 recommendations; referral criteria (MELD ≥12, decompensation, ACLF, ALF, HCC, CCA); multidisciplinary workup; ALD fixed abstinence interval NOT a listing exclusion; HPS/POPH candidacy thresholds
- [[aasld-ast-2025-liver-transplant-graft-complications]] — AASLD/AST 2025 Practice Guideline: Liver Transplant Graft Complications — 57 recommendations; machine perfusion; vascular/biliary complications; TCMR/PCR/AMR; 2025 IS trough targets; no protocol biopsy (Rec 34); ATG contraindicated (Rec 26); mTOR for HCC within Milan
- [[aasld-ast-2025-liver-transplant-non-graft-complications]] — AASLD/AST 2025 Consensus: LT Non-Graft Complications — 70 recommendations; wellness/nutrition; bone (DEXA at 6mo); vaccination (no live vaccines); CMV (pre-emptive PCR preferred; maribavir for resistant); cancer surveillance (2.45× standardized incidence ratio (SIR); non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL) SIR ~10); PTLD EBV PCR not recommended; hydrophilic statins (pravastatin/rosuvastatin); GLP-1RA for obesity/atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease–type 2 diabetes mellitus (ASCVD-T2DM); bariatric surgery post-LT
- [[aasld-idsa-2025-chb-treatment]] — AASLD/IDSA 2025: Treatment of Chronic Hepatitis B — Ghany, Terrault et al.
- [[aasld-2024-aclf]] — AASLD 2024 Practice Guidance: Acute-on-Chronic Liver Failure — Karvellas, Bajaj et al. - [[aasld-2024-nilda-blood]] — AASLD 2024 Practice Guideline: Blood-Based NILDA of Hepatic Fibrosis and Steatosis — Sterling, Patel, Duarte-Rojo et al. Hepatology 2024 — 6 PICOs / 10 statements (GRADE); FIB-4 rule-out <1.3 (alt ≥1.45) / rule-in >2.67 (alt ≥3.25); NFS −1.455/0.676; ELF highest diagnostic odds ratio (DOR) (~21.5), ≥11.3 prognostic; FIB-4→ELF cuts referrals 80%; against blood steatosis indices; against blood NILDA for serial monitoring; confounders (splenectomy/inflammation/CKD)
- [[aasld-2024-resmetirom]] — Resmetirom Therapy for MASLD: Oct 2024 AASLD Guidance Update — Chen, Morgan, Rotman et al. Hepatology 2025;81:312–320 — THR-β agonist; first FDA-approved MASH F2–F3 drug (Mar 2024); MAESTRO-NASH 26–30% MASH resolution; candidacy VCTE 8–15/MRE 3.1–4.4 kPa; dosing 100/80 mg + CYP2C8/statin DDI; thyroid/gallstone pretreatment; 12-mo efficacy/futility (VCTE ≥25%/MRE ≥20%; MRI-PDFF <30% = futility)
- [[aasld-2024-nilda-imaging]] — AASLD 2024 Practice Guideline: Imaging-Based Noninvasive Liver Disease Assessment (fibrosis & steatosis) — VCTE/MRE/SWE and controlled attenuation parameter (CAP)/MRI-PDFF cutoffs for staging and steatosis quantification
- [[aasld-2024-nilda-portal-htn]] — AASLD 2024 Practice Guideline: Noninvasive Assessment of Portal Hypertension — liver/spleen stiffness, platelet–LSM (Baveno/Rule of Five), CSPH and high-risk varices prediction
- [[aasld-2024-tips]] — AASLD 2024 Practice Guidance: TIPS, Variceal Embolization, and Retrograde Transvenous Obliteration (BRTO) — indications, candidacy, and complication management for portal-hypertensive bleeding and refractory ascites
- [[aasld-2023-hcc]] — AASLD 2023 Practice Guidance: Prevention, Diagnosis, and Treatment of HCC — Singal AG, Llovet JM et al. Hepatology 2023;78:1922–1965 — 50 guidance statements; surveillance populations (Table 1: cirrhosis ≥1%/yr, non-cirrhotic HBV ≥0.2%/yr, PAGE-B ≥10); US+AFP q6mo (GS 10, Level 2 Strong); LI-RADS CT/MRI framework (GS 16–22); BCLC 2022 staging (Figure 9); TACE (GS 41, Level 1 Strong) and TARE (GS 42); first-line atezo+bev (IMbrave150: overall survival (OS) 19.2 mo, HR 0.66) and durvalumab+tremelimumab (HIMALAYA: OS 16.4 mo, HR 0.78); sorafenib 400 mg BID; lenvatinib 12 mg (≥60 kg)/8 mg (<60 kg) QD; second-line regorafenib/cabozantinib/ramucirumab; Milan/UCSF/UNOS-DS transplant criteria; adjuvant GS 32 (original, superseded by 2025 update — contraindicated)
- [[aasld-2023-masld-nomenclature]] — MASLD: Update and Impact of New Nomenclature on AASLD NAFLD Guidance — Kanwal, Neuschwander-Tetri, Loomba, Rinella. Hepatology 2024;79:1212–1219 — SLD/MASLD/metabolic dysfunction–associated steatotic liver (MASL)/MASH/MetALD schema; 5-item CMRF criteria with thresholds; MetALD alcohol ranges (20–50♀/30–60♂ g/d); >99% NAFLD↔MASLD overlap → all NAFLD guidance applies; dual pathology; cryptogenic SLD
- [[aasld-2023-nafld]] — AASLD 2023 Practice Guidance: Clinical Assessment and Management of NAFLD — Rinella, Neuschwander-Tetri, Siddiqui et al. Hepatology 2023;77:1797–1835 — 29 guidance statements; at-risk NASH = ≥F2; FIB-4 two-tier pathway (Table 5 cut-points); statins safe (GS 1–2); alcohol ≥F2 abstinence (GS 7); weight-loss dose-response (3–5/7–10/>10%); Mediterranean diet, coffee ≥3 cups; bariatric (GS 22); pioglitazone/vitamin E/semaglutide positioning (GS 23–28, no antifibrotic benefit); ALT/MRI-PDFF surrogates (GS 29)
- [[aasld-2023-portal-hypertension]] — AASLD 2023 Practice Guidance: Portal Hypertension and Varices in Cirrhosis — Kaplan et al. Hepatology 2024;79:1180–1211 — 58 guidance statements; cACLD/CSPH noninvasive staging (Rule of Five); carvedilol preferred NSBB; preemptive TIPS criteria (CTP B>7 active bleeding or CTP C 10–13); no FFP/platelets based on INR/platelet; secondary prophylaxis NSBB + EVL; gastric/ectopic varices
- [[aasld-idsa-2023-hcv]] — AASLD-IDSA 2023 Hepatitis C Guidance Update — Bhattacharya et al. Clin Infect Dis 2023; doi:10.1093/cid/ciad319 — G/P 8 wk for compensated cirrhosis (EXPEDITION-8); simplified algorithm includes HIV coinfected; incomplete adherence algorithm; retreatment (MAGELLAN-3 G/P+SOF+RBV 96%); viremic donor organs; pediatric ≥3 years treatment
- [[aasld-2022-psc]] — AASLD 2022 Practice Guidance: PSC and Cholangiocarcinoma — Bowlus et al. Hepatology 2023;77:659–702 — 53 guidance statements; 3D MRI/MRCP preferred (avoid ERCP dx); LS by TE/MRE for fibrosis staging; UDCA 13–23 mg/kg/d conditional; annual MRI/MRCP ± CA 19-9 CCA surveillance; FISH for relevant strictures; HD colonoscopy q1–2y from age 15; LT for ESLD/recurrent cholangitis/CCA; rPSC 10–37%; comprehensive iCCA/pCCA/dCCA management with LT neoadjuvant protocol for pCCA ≤3 cm
- [[aasld-2022-wilson-disease]] — AASLD 2022 Practice Guidance: Wilson Disease — Schilsky, Roberts et al. Hepatology 2025;82:E41–E90 — 38 guidance statements; Leipzig score; NWI; treatment targets table; trientine tetrahydrochloride (FDA 2022); combination therapy for decompensated cirrhosis; ALF management; HCC surveillance; pregnancy guidance
- [[aasld-2022-dili]] — AASLD 2022 Practice Guidance: Drug, Herbal, and Dietary Supplement-Induced Liver Injury — causality assessment (RUCAM), hepatocellular/cholestatic/mixed patterns, NAC, drug-specific management
- [[aasld-2022-palliative-cirrhosis]] — AASLD 2022 Practice Guidance: Palliative Care and Symptom-Based Management for Decompensated Cirrhosis — symptom control, goals-of-care, concurrent care with LT evaluation
- [[aasld-2021-ascites-sbp-hrs]] — AASLD 2021: Diagnosis, Evaluation, and Management of Ascites, SBP, and HRS — PMN ≥250 threshold; albumin mandatory in SBP; LVP + albumin 6–8 g/L; ciprofloxacin prophylaxis; TIPS criteria; hyponatremia grading
- [[aasld-2021-pbc]] — AASLD 2021 Practice Guidance Update: Primary Biliary Cholangitis — Lindor et al. Hepatology 2022;75:1012–1013 — Focused 2-statement update: OCA contraindicated in advanced cirrhosis (prior/current decompensation or portal hypertension per May 2021 FDA warning); fibrates discouraged in decompensated liver disease; monitor all cirrhotics on OCA
- [[aasld-2020-ald]] — AASLD 2019/2020 Practice Guidance: Diagnosis and Treatment of Alcohol-Associated Liver Diseases — Crabb, Im, Szabo, Mellinger, Lucey. Hepatology 2020;71:306–333 — 22 guidance statements; definite/probable/possible AH consensus criteria; MDF ≥32 primary steroid threshold; MELD ≥20 prompts consideration; Lille score at day 7; prednisolone 40 mg × 28d; IV NAC (potential benefit, 30-day improvement); pentoxifylline explicitly abandoned (GS-19); AKI >2.5 mg/dL relative contraindication; gastrointestinal bleeding (GIB) NOT absolute contraindication; early liver transplantation (ELT) for severe AH (GS-22); no fixed abstinence interval (GS-21); baclofen/acamprosate for AUD in ALD; PEth preferred biomarker; PNPLA3/TM6SF2/HSD17B13 genetics
- [[aasld-2020-autoimmune-hepatitis]] — AASLD 2020 Practice Guideline: Autoimmune Hepatitis — Mack et al. Hepatology 2020 — 5 GRADE recommendations + extensive guidance; budesonide+azathioprine (AZA) vs. prednisone+AZA first-line; MMF preferred second-line; ≥2y remission before withdrawal; 50–87% relapse; overlap syndromes (Paris criteria AIH-PBC); anti-SLA=never withdraw; TPMT before AZA; HCC surveillance in cirrhosis
- [[aasld-2018-pbc]] — AASLD 2018 Practice Guidance: Primary Biliary Cholangitis — Lindor, Bowlus, Boyer, Levy, Mayo. Hepatology 2019;69:394–419 — 21 guidance statements; 2-of-3 diagnostic criteria; UDCA 13–15 mg/kg/day; 8 biochemical response criteria (Paris I/II/Rotterdam/Toronto/Barcelona); OCA 5→10 mg/day (POISE trial); fibrates off-label; pruritus ladder; bone disease; HCC surveillance; LT at MELD >14; recurrent PBC post-LT; PBC-AIH overlap (Paris criteria)
- [[aasld-2021-malnutrition-cirrhosis]] — AASLD 2021 Practice Guidance: Malnutrition, Frailty, and Sarcopenia in Cirrhosis — Liver Frailty Index, muscle-mass assessment, protein 1.2–1.5 g/kg/day, late-evening snack
- [[aasld-2021-reproductive-liver]] — AASLD 2021 Practice Guidance: Reproductive Health and Liver Disease — contraception, fertility, pregnancy management in cirrhosis/portal hypertension and specific liver diseases
- [[aasld-2021-vascular-pvt]] — AASLD 2021 Practice Guidance: Vascular Liver Disorders, Portal Vein Thrombosis, and Procedural Bleeding in Cirrhosis — Budd-Chiari, PVT anticoagulation, rebalanced hemostasis, periprocedural management
- [[aasld-easl-2014-hepatic-encephalopathy]] — AASLD/EASL 2014 Practice Guideline: Hepatic Encephalopathy in Chronic Liver Disease — Vilstrup et al. Hepatology 2014;60:715–735 — 33 recommendations; 4-axis classification; WHC/Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) grading; ammonia no diagnostic value (Rec 9); lactulose first-line (Rec 19); rifaximin add-on after 2nd episode (Rec 26); protein restriction opposed (1.2–1.5 g/kg/day); late-night snack (Rec 32); post-TIPS HE: no prophylaxis benefit; recurrent intractable OHE = LT indication
- [[aasld-2012-liver-transplant-long-term]] — AASLD 2012: Long-Term Management of the Successful Adult Liver Transplant Recipient — Lucey et al. Liver Transpl 2013;19:3–26 — 93 recommendations; IS monitoring/minimization; metabolic complications; oncologic surveillance; recurrent disease
- [[aasld-2011-alf]] — AASLD Position Paper: Management of Acute Liver Failure (Update 2011) — Lee, Larson, Stravitz (historical; 48 graded recs; superseded by acg-2023-alf)
- [[aasld-2011-hemochromatosis]] — AASLD 2011 Practice Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Hemochromatosis — Bacon et al. Hepatology 2011;54:328–343 (historical; 16 GRADE recs; TS+ferritin→HFE testing, biopsy if ferritin >1000 µg/L, phlebotomy to ferritin 50–100 µg/L, HCC surveillance in HH cirrhosis (RR~20), no population screening; complements acg-2019-hereditary-hemochromatosis)
- [[aasld-idsa-2011-hcv-genotype1]] — AASLD 2011: Treatment of Genotype 1 Chronic HCV — Ghany et al. Hepatology 2011;54:1433–1444 (historical/obsolete; boceprevir/telaprevir + peginterferon/ribavirin triple therapy, response-guided therapy, IL28B; entirely superseded by interferon-free DAA era, aasld-idsa-2023-hcv)
- [[aasld-2009-liver-biopsy]] — AASLD 2009 Position Paper: Liver Biopsy — Rockey et al. Hepatology 2009;49:1017–1044 — 34 graded recommendations (ACC/AHA Class I–III, Level A–C); no validated INR/platelet cutoff predicts bleeding; ideal specimen ~3 cm/16-gauge/>11 portal tracts; ultrasound marking preferred over CT (13% site change; 0.5% vs 2.2% major complications); transjugular if coagulopathy/ascites (minor 6.5%, major 0.6%, mortality 0.09% in 7,649 biopsies); >25% error rate when read by a nonspecialist pathologist; severe bleeding at 2–4 h, delayed up to a week; Tables 3–7 and 10 recreated natively

### ACG

- [[acg-2026-hepatic-encephalopathy]] — ACG 2026: Hepatic Encephalopathy — diagnosis/grading (West Haven), ammonia's limited role, lactulose first-line, rifaximin add-on, nutrition, secondary prophylaxis, post-TIPS HE
- [[acg-2025-crohns]] — ACG 2025: Management of Crohn's Disease in Adults — Lichtenstein et al.
- [[acg-2025-egd-quality]] — ACG/ASGE 2025: Quality Indicators for Upper GI Endoscopy — Yadlapati et al. — 29 indicators with Guyatt grades (1A–3), strict ">" targets, 9 priority indicators; LA and Forrest criteria; acid-suppressant dose table
- [[acg-2025-eoe]] — ACG 2025: Diagnosis and Management of Eosinophilic Esophagitis — Dellon et al.
- [[acg-2025-eus-quality]] — ACG/ASGE 2025: Quality Indicators for EUS — Mishra et al. — 20 indicators with Guyatt grades (1A–3) and performance targets; 5 priority indicators; antibiotic need by indication
- [[acg-2025-gastric-premalignant]] — ACG 2025: Diagnosis and Management of Gastric Premalignant Conditions — Morgan et al.
- [[acg-2025-ibd-preventive-care]] — ACG 2025: Preventive Care in IBD — Farraye et al. (vaccinations, cancer screening, osteoporosis, smoking, depression/anxiety; 12 GRADE recs + 11 key concepts)
- [[acg-2025-liver-nutrition]] — ACG 2025: Malnutrition and Nutritional Recommendations in Liver Disease — Singal, Wong et al.
- [[acg-2025-uc]] — ACG 2025: Ulcerative Colitis in Adults (Guideline Update) — Rubin et al.
- [[acg-2025-perioperative-cirrhosis]] — ACG 2025: Perioperative Risk Assessment and Management in Cirrhosis — VOCAL-Penn/Mayo surgical risk scores, frailty, decompensation risk, timing and optimization for elective surgery
- [[acg-2024-acute-pancreatitis]] — ACG 2024: Management of Acute Pancreatitis — Tenner, Vege et al.
- [[acg-2024-ald]] — ACG 2024: Alcohol-Associated Liver Disease — Jophlin, Singal et al.
- [[acg-2024-focal-liver-lesions]] — ACG 2024: Focal Liver Lesions — Frenette et al.
- [[acg-2024-hp-treatment]] — ACG 2024: Treatment of Helicobacter pylori Infection — Chey, Howden et al.
- [[acg-2023-alf]] — ACG 2023: Acute Liver Failure — O'Grady classification, NAC for non-APAP, CRRT for ammonia, no prophylactic antibiotics, no routine coagulopathy correction, KCC/MELD transplant criteria
- [[acg-2023-biliary-strictures]] — ACG 2023: Diagnosis and Management of Biliary Strictures — EUS-FNB vs. ERCP sampling, perihilar vs. distal algorithm, cholangioscopy + FISH, confirm malignancy before uncovered SEMS (uSEMS), PDT/RFA for unresectable perihilar CCA
- [[acg-2023-lgib]] — ACG 2023: Management of Acute Lower GI Bleeding — Oakland score, CTA-first, urgent colonoscopy no longer standard, EBL vs. clipping, tranexamic acid contraindicated, resume anticoagulation within 7 days
- [[acg-2023-subepithelial-lesions]] — ACG 2023: Diagnosis and Management of GI Subepithelial Lesions — EUS layer-based differential, GIST size thresholds, gastric NET types 1/2/3, rectal NET band ligation EMR, malignant EUS features
- [[acg-2022-barretts]] — ACG 2022: Diagnosis and Management of Barrett's Esophagus — 21 GRADE recs; Prague C&M; dysplasia grading; NDBE surveillance by M-length; EET for LGD+; EMR-first for visible lesions; post-CEIM surveillance
- [[acg-2022-celiac]] — ACG 2022: Diagnosis and Management of Celiac Disease — ≥6 biopsies protocol; Marsh grading; IgA deficiency; nonbiopsy dx in children; refractory celiac disease types 1/2; EATL risk
- [[acg-2022-gastroparesis]] — ACG 2022: Gastroparesis — 4-hour scintigraphy protocol; metoclopramide/domperidone/erythromycin dosing; G-POEM preferred; no botox/GES/nortriptyline; EndoFLIP for pyloric DI
- [[acg-cag-2022-anticoag-gi-bleeding]] — ACG/CAG 2022: Anticoagulants and Antiplatelets in Acute GI Bleeding and Periendoscopic Period — 18 conditional statements; no bridging (BRIDGE trial); 4F-PCC over FFP; platelet transfusion mortality signal; DOAC reversal only for life-threatening hemorrhage
- [[acg-2021-anorectal-disorders]] — ACG 2021: Management of Benign Anorectal Disorders — Wald, Bharucha, Limketkai et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2021;116:1987–2008
- [[acg-2021-cdiff]] — ACG 2021: Prevention, Diagnosis, and Treatment of CDI — two-step testing; severity thresholds; vancomycin/fidaxomicin; fulminant dosing; FMT for recurrent; bezlotoxumab; no probiotics
- [[acg-2021-crc-screening]] — ACG 2021: Colorectal Cancer Screening — Shaukat, Kahi, Burke et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2021;116:458–479
- [[acg-2021-dili]] — ACG 2021: Diagnosis and Management of Idiosyncratic DILI — Chalasani, Maddur, Russo et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2021;116:878–898
- [[acg-2021-gerd]] — ACG 2021: Diagnosis and Management of GERD — Katz, Dunbar, Schnoll-Sussman et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2022;117:27–56
- [[acg-2021-ugib]] — ACG 2021: Upper GI and Ulcer Bleeding — 16 statements; GBS 0–1 safe discharge; erythromycin pre-endoscopy; 24h endoscopy; Forrest classification; high-dose PPI post-hemostasis; TAE over surgery for failure
- [[acg-2020-achalasia]] — ACG 2020: Diagnosis and Management of Achalasia — Vaezi, Pandolfino et al.
- [[acg-2020-chronic-pancreatitis]] — ACG 2020: Chronic Pancreatitis — Gardner et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2020;115:322–339 — Mechanistic Definition; TIGAR-O v2.0; CT/MRI first-line; PRSS1/SPINK1/CFTR/CTRC genetics; surgery > endoscopy for obstructive CP; PERT for EPI only
- [[acg-2020-esophageal-physiologic-testing]] — ACG 2020: Clinical Use of Esophageal Physiologic Testing — Yadlapati et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2020;115:1412–1428 — 16 GRADE recommendations; symptom-based routing (obstructive/reflux/extraesophageal); empiric PPI test performance; FLIP; rumination and supragastric belching
- [[acg-2020-hepatic-mesenteric-circulation]] — ACG 2020: Disorders of the Hepatic and Mesenteric Circulation — Simonetto et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2020;115:18–40 — rebalanced hemostasis in cirrhosis; no prophylactic FFP/platelets; anticoagulate noncirrhotic + complete cirrhotic PVT; Budd-Chiari syndrome (BCS) stepwise management (AC→angioplasty→TIPS→LT); mesenteric artery aneurysms (high-risk sites treated at any size; >2 cm all); HHT liver vascular malformations; all 23 recs + 28 key concepts, Tables 3–6
- [[acg-2020-ibs]] — ACG 2020: Management of Irritable Bowel Syndrome — Lacy, Pimentel, Brenner, Chey et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2021;116:17–44 — 25 GRADE statements; positive diagnostic strategy; calprotectin + celiac serology; low-FODMAP, fiber, TCAs; secretagogues for IBS-C; rifaximin/alosetron/eluxadoline for IBS-D
- [[acg-2020-sibo]] — ACG 2020: Small Intestinal Bacterial Overgrowth — Pimentel et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2020;115:165–178 — 6 GRADE recommendations + 15 key concepts; IMO nomenclature; breath test protocols; antibiotic table
- [[acg-2019-hereditary-hemochromatosis]] — ACG 2019: Hereditary Hemochromatosis — Crownover, Covey. Am J Gastroenterol 2019;114:1202–1218 — 10 GRADE recommendations; TS+SF algorithm; HFE genotyping; phlebotomy protocol; HCC surveillance; chelation agents
- [[acg-2019-uc]] — ACG 2019: Ulcerative Colitis in Adults — Rubin et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2019;114:384–413 (historical; GRADE, 54 recs; superseded by acg-2025-uc — predates ustekinumab/ozanimod/upadacitinib in UC)
- [[acg-2018-pancreatic-cysts]] — ACG 2018: Diagnosis and Management of Pancreatic Cysts — Elta et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2018;113:464–479 — 20 recommendations (all low/very-low quality); cyst-type classification; size-stratified surveillance; high-risk features
- [[acg-2018-crohns]] — ACG 2018: Management of Crohn's Disease in Adults — Lichtenstein et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2018;113:481–517 (historical; 60 recs; superseded by acg-2025-crohns — drug positioning predates IL-23/JAK agents)
- [[acg-2017-hp-guidelines]] — ACG Clinical Guideline: Treatment of Helicobacter pylori Infection (2017) — Chey et al.
- [[acg-2017-liver-chemistries]] — ACG 2017: Evaluation of Abnormal Liver Chemistries — Kwo, Cohen, Lim. Am J Gastroenterol 2017;112:18–35 — true healthy ALT ULN, R-ratio, 5-tier AST/ALT algorithm, ALP + bilirubin fractionation algorithms, liver biopsy indications
- [[acg-2017-ibd-preventive-care]] — ACG 2017: Preventive Care in Inflammatory Bowel Disease — Farraye et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2017;112:241–258 (historical; 14 GRADE recs; vaccinations/cervical/skin-cancer/osteoporosis/depression/smoking; superseded by acg-2025-ibd-preventive-care)
- [[acg-cag-2017-dyspepsia]] — ACG/CAG 2017: Management of Dyspepsia — Moayyedi et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2017;112:988–1013 — EGD threshold raised to ≥60; HP test-and-treat (Strong/High) first; PPI → TCA → prokinetic → psychotherapy; SSRIs ineffective
- [[acg-2016-acute-diarrhea]] — ACG 2016: Diagnosis, Treatment, and Prevention of Acute Diarrheal Infections in Adults — Riddle et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2016;111:602–622 — 18 recommendations; pathogen-specific antibiotic table; multiplex PCR panel guidance; travelers' diarrhea (TD) prevention
- [[acg-2016-liver-disease-pregnancy]] — ACG 2016: Liver Disease and Pregnancy — Tran, Lavezo, Higa et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2016;111:176–194 — 36 recommendations; HG/IHCP/HELLP/AFLP/preeclampsia; viral hepatitis in pregnancy; chronic liver disease management
- [[acg-2016-nutrition-hospitalized]] — ACG 2016: Nutrition Therapy in the Adult Hospitalized Patient — McClave et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2016;111:315–334 — EN preferred over PN; NRS-2002/NUTRIC scoring; avoid albumin as nutrition marker; 38-item recommendations; permissive underfeeding in ARDS/obesity; no routine GRV monitoring; PN deferral strategy
- [[acg-2015-colon-ischemia]] — ACG 2015: Colon Ischemia — Brandt et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2015;110:18–44 — rebuilt from the PDF: **Table 6 severity criteria in full** (ten poor-outcome factors with their numeric cutoffs, plus the ≤3 / >3 count rule and the four hard severe findings) and the **Ischemic Colitis Mortality Risk (ICMR) score** (5 factors, mortality 10.5%→100% by points); verbatim recommendations with grades — note **multiphasic CTA is required, not withheld,** when IRCI is suspected or AMI can't be excluded; colonoscopy within 48 h with minimal insufflation, halted at the distalmost extent; IRCI 30-d mortality 20.3% vs 9.0%, pan-colonic 21.7%
- [[acg-2015-hereditary-gi-cancer]] — ACG 2015: Genetic Testing and Management of Hereditary GI Cancer Syndromes — Syngal et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2015;110:223–262 — 25 recommendations; Lynch, FAP/MAP, Peutz-Jeghers, Juvenile Polyposis, Cowden, SPS, HDGC, hereditary pancreatic cancer
- [[acg-2015-psc]] — ACG 2015: Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis — Lindor, Kowdley, Harrison. Am J Gastroenterol 2015;110:646–659 — MRCP-preferred dx, IgG4 testing, UDCA >28 mg/kg/d harmful, dominant stricture ERCP + FISH, annual chromoendoscopy in PSC-IBD, LT at MELD >14
- [[acg-2015-small-bowel-bleeding]] — ACG 2015: Diagnosis and Management of Small Bowel Bleeding — Gerson et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2015;110:1265–1287 — 31 recommendations; SBB terminology; VCE/DAE algorithms; angioectasia treatment
- [[acg-2013-eoe]] — ACG 2013: Esophageal Eosinophilia and Eosinophilic Esophagitis — Dellon et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2013;108:679–692 (historical; PPI-responsive esophageal eosinophilia (PPI-REE) construct + mandatory PPI trial; 21 recs; superseded by acg-2025-eoe)
- [[usmstf-2014-lynch-syndrome]] — US Multi-Society Task Force 2014: Genetic Evaluation and Management of Lynch Syndrome — Giardiello et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2014;109:1159–1179 — universal tumor testing; Amsterdam II/Bethesda criteria; Tables 10 and 12 surveillance/management; aspirin CAPP2; genetic counseling 14-component framework
- [[acg-2014-bowel-cleansing]] — USMSTF 2014: Optimizing Adequacy of Bowel Cleansing for Colonoscopy — Johnson, Barkun, Cohen et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2014;109:1528–1545 (foundational; 23 GRADE recs; split-dose superiority, 4–6 h/2 h timing, ≥85% adequate-prep per-physician benchmark, avoid Mg/sodium phosphate (NaP) in CKD/IBD/elderly; superseded for products by usmstf-2025-bowel-prep)
- [[acg-2026-diverticulitis]] — ACG 2026: Colonic Diverticulitis — Peery, Strate, Stollman et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121:1549–1561 — 14 GRADE recs; selective antibiotics, modified Hinchey, post-recovery colonoscopy, recurrence prevention, elective-surgery reversal; against mesalamine/rifaximin/probiotics

### AGA

- [[aga-2022-personalized-gerd]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Personalized Approach to the Evaluation and Management of GERD — Yadlapati, Gyawali, Pandolfino (CGIT GERD Consensus Conference). Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2022;20:984–994 — ungraded BPA; up to **half of suspected-GERD patients get no benefit from acid suppression**; phenotype grid from EGD **off PPI** + prolonged wireless pH **off PPI** → No GERD / borderline GERD / GERD, with a **severe GERD** modifier, each with its own treatment ladder; no-GERD patients stop the PPI and are worked up as a functional esophageal disorder
- [[aga-2020-functional-heartburn]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Functional Heartburn — Fass, Zerbib, Gyawali. Gastroenterology 2020;158:2286–2293 — ungraded BPA; retrosternal burning on **double-dose PPI ≥3 months** with **normal acid exposure, no major motor disorder, no mucosal pathology, and a negative reflux–symptom association**; the three-way separation from **NERD** (abnormal acid exposure) and **reflux hypersensitivity** (physiologic acid exposure, *positive* symptom association); ~a quarter of PPI-refractory heartburn; escalating acid suppression or operating is ineffective and potentially harmful
- [[aga-2018-extraesophageal-gerd]] — AGA 2018 CPU: Extraesophageal Symptoms Attributed to GERD — "Where is the Pendulum Swinging Now?" Vaezi, Katzka, Zerbib. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2018;16:1018–1029 — reflux causation **overestimated**; attribution strongest for asthma, cough, hoarseness, weaker (association only) for sore throat, sinusitis, ear pain, pulmonary fibrosis; ear, nose, and throat (ENT)/pulmonary/allergy evaluation often **initial**; direct-contact vs neurogenic mechanisms. ⚠ The **older** counterpart to [[aga-2023-extraesophageal-gerd]] — ingested to record what changed
- [[aga-2021-post-esd-surveillance]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Commentary): Surveillance After Pathologically Curative ESD of Early GI Neoplasia in the US — Wang, Hwang, Bhatt, Draganov. Gastroenterology 2021;161:2030–2040 — **no numbered BPA and no GRADE**; the five criteria for a pathologically curative ESD and the **organ-specific depth thresholds** (<500 µm esophageal/gastric adenocarcinoma, <1000 µm colorectal, **no curative depth once the muscularis mucosae is reached in esophageal SCC**); R0 redefined inside a premalignant field (highest grade absent from margins), R1 horizontal → re-resect vs R1 vertical → surgery; **five surveillance tables** (esophageal SCC, Barrett's/EAC, gastric, colonic, rectal) with lymph node (LN)-metastasis risk per stratum; post-ESD ablation at 2–3 mo on high-dose PPI to CE-IM; stricture prophylaxis above **75% circumference**; intervals explicitly do **not** apply to hereditary syndromes
- [[aga-2022-subepithelial-lesions]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Subepithelial Lesions Encountered During Routine Endoscopy — Sharzehi, Sethi, Savides. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2022;20:2435–2443 — **10 numbered BPA, ungraded**; subepithelial lesions (SELs) in **1 of every 300 endoscopies**, two-thirds gastric; the full **Table 1** differential by EUS layer × echogenicity × histology/immunohistochemistry (IHC) (CD117/DOG1 vs desmin/SMA vs S-100) and **Table 2** resection-technique table (endoscopic submucosal resection (ESMR)/ESD/STER/full-thickness resection (FTR) with size limits and adverse-event rates); GIST metastasis risk by size×site×mitotic index (gastric ≤2 cm **0%**, small bowel <2 cm high-mitotic up to **50%**); gastric type 1/2/3 carcinoid management; EUS alone is **64% sensitive / 80% specific** for malignant potential
- [[aga-2022-diet-ibs]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Role of Diet in IBS — Chey, Hashash, Manning, Chang. Gastroenterology 2022;162:1737–1745 — ungraded BPA; diet as a **primary** treatment (drugs help <½ of patients, therapeutic gain **7–15%** over placebo; **>80%** link symptoms to a meal); **RDN** should deliver restrictive diets; every diet trial gets a **predetermined duration**; poor candidates named (few culprit foods, malnutrition risk, food insecurity, eating disorder/uncontrolled psychiatric disorder); disordered eating common and overlooked
- [[aga-2021-chronic-gi-pain-dgbi]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Chronic Gastrointestinal Pain in Disorders of Gut–Brain Interaction — the central-sensitization framing of chronic DGBI pain, the communication approach that keeps the patient in the consultation rather than in repeat testing, neuromodulators and brain-gut behavioral therapies as the active treatments, and the opioid trap that becomes [[narcotic-bowel-syndrome]]
- [[aga-2017-opioids-in-gastroenterology]] — AGA 2017 CPU (**Narrative Expert Review**): Opioids in Gastroenterology — Treating Adverse Effects and Creating Therapeutic Benefits. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2017 — ⚠ **no numbered Best Practice Advice and no evidence grades**; never cite it as graded. The defining source for [[narcotic-bowel-syndrome]] (>100 mg morphine equivalent/day, >3 months, pain rising as the dose rises) and the source supplying the [[pamoras]] doses AGA 2019 omits
- [[aga-2018-rumination-syndrome]] — AGA 2018 CPU (Expert Review): Diagnosis and Treatment of Rumination Syndrome — Halland, Pandolfino, Barba. *Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol* 2018;16:1549–1555 — **7 numbered Best Practice Advice statements, ungraded**, and the **only document in the corpus that treats rumination**; [[rumination-syndrome]] had an **empty Therapeutics section** saying so. Supplies the first-line therapy **with the technique, not just the label** — diaphragmatic breathing ± biofeedback in **all** cases (BPA 4): hand on chest and hand on abdomen, **only the abdomen moves**, inhale through the nose / exhale through the mouth at **6–8 respirations per minute, 15 min after every meal**, plus practice away from meals; **20%–66% improve**, it both **lowers intragastric pressure and raises EGJ pressure**, and ⚠ **episodes restart promptly when the breathing stops**. ⚠ **BPA 5 says any familiar practitioner can teach it** — no specialist referral needed. **Baclofen 10 mg three times daily is the named refractory step** (BPA 7; symptom improvement **63% vs 26% placebo**); ⚠ **levosulpiride worsened 14%**, **buspirone has no rumination trials**, and **surgery is to be avoided** (fundoplication rests on 5 cases, and earlier cohorts included patients still ruminating *after* fundoplication). Diagnosis: consider it in **any** consistent postprandial regurgitation — these patients get labelled refractory reflux (BPA 1) — and ⚠ **nocturnal regurgitation, dysphagia, nausea or non-meal symptoms do not exclude it, only make it less likely** (BPA 2). Figure 4's approach recreated as a flowchart on the disease page. ⚠ Prints **Rome IV** criteria; [[rome-v-2026-dgbi]] is newer but does not print rumination criteria, so there is nothing to conflict with
- [[aga-2019-celiac-monitoring]] — AGA 2019 CPU (Expert Review): Diagnosis and Monitoring of Celiac Disease — Changing Utility of Serology and Histologic Measures — Husby, Murray, Katzka. Gastroenterology 2019;156:885–889 — 12 BPA; **tissue transglutaminase 2 IgA (TG2-IgA) + total IgA** anchor, endomysial antibody (EMA) second-line confirmatory, deamidated gliadin peptide IgG (DGP-IgG)/TG2-IgG for IgA deficiency; **TG2-IgA >10× ULN confirmed by EMA on a second sample → PPV "virtually 100%"**, licensing the biopsy-avoiding pathway in selected children; Marsh classification with IEL counting and morphometry; serology is **weak for monitoring** and detects persistent injury poorly
- [[aga-2021-seronegative-enteropathies]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Evaluation and Management of Seronegative Enteropathies — Leonard, Lebwohl, Rubio-Tapia, Biagi. Gastroenterology 2021;160:437–444 — separates seronegative **CeD** (1.7–5% of CeD), **CeD with selective IgA deficiency** (falsely negative IgA tests — *not* seronegative disease), and **non-celiac villous atrophy**; **villous atrophy must be present** — raised IELs with normal villi do not qualify; immune/infectious/iatrogenic/inflammatory causes
- [[aga-2017-severe-alcoholic-hepatitis]] — AGA 2017 CPU (Expert Review): Medical Management of Severe Alcoholic Hepatitis — Mitchell, Friedman, McClain. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2017;15:5–12 — **BPA 1–12, ungraded**; National Institute on Alcohol Abuse and Alcoholism (NIAAA) consensus definition (jaundice within **60 d** of >**50 g/d** for ≥**6 months**, bilirubin >**3 mg/dL**, AST **50–400 U/L**, **AST:ALT >1.5**, no other cause) and the definite/probable/possible classification; systemic inflammatory response syndrome (SIRS) on admission predicts multi-organ failure; histologic features
- [[aga-2019-psc-cancer-surveillance]] — AGA 2019 CPU (Expert Review): Surveillance for Hepatobiliary Cancers in PSC — Bowlus, Lim, Lindor. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2019;17:2416–2422 — 8 BPA; **cross-sectional imaging (US/CT/MRI) ± CA 19-9 every 6–12 months in every adult with large-duct PSC**, independent of stage, MRI "preferred by many experts"; CCA annual risk **0.5–1.0%**, 30-y cumulative **20%**, **400×** general population, and **27–37% of incident CCAs appear within 1 year of the PSC diagnosis**; no level-1 evidence and no validated risk-stratification tool exist
- [[aga-2020-hcc-screening-nafld]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Screening and Surveillance for HCC in NAFLD — Loomba, Lim, Patton, El-Serag. Gastroenterology 2020;158:1822–1830 — **8 BPA, ungraded**; screen NAFLD **cirrhosis** (risk threshold ≈**≥1.5%/y**; benefit restricted to compensated cirrhosis or decompensated *listed for transplant*), screen when noninvasive markers show advanced fibrosis/cirrhosis, and **do not routinely screen NAFLD without advanced fibrosis**; the no-biopsy rule — **≥2 noninvasive tests from 2 different test groups** (point-of-care scores / blood panels / elastography), **high cut-points chosen for specificity**, screen only if concordant; the **LI-RADS ultrasound visualization score A/B/C** with definitions, and inadequate ultrasound → CT or MRI ± AFP **every 6 months**; alcohol/tobacco, diabetes/dyslipidemia and obesity as modifiable risk. ⚠ Uses "screening" for both index and periodic assessment, and explicitly **declines** to use age-, sex- or ethnicity-specific rates in the screening decision
- [[aga-2021-lifestyle-modification-nafld]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Lifestyle Modification Using Diet and Exercise to Achieve Weight Loss in NAFLD — Younossi, Corey, Lim. Gastroenterology 2021;160:912–918 — **10 BPA, ungraded**; the weight-loss dose–response ladder (**≥5% total body weight (TBW)** → less steatosis, **≥7%** → NASH resolution, **≥10%** → fibrosis regression/stability); hypocaloric target **1200–1500 kcal/d or −500–1000 kcal/d** from baseline; **Mediterranean diet**, minimize saturated fat, red/processed meat and commercial fructose; **150–300 min moderate or 75–150 min vigorous** aerobic exercise weekly, resistance training complementary **not** a replacement; normal-weight NAFLD (BMI ≤25, **≤23 in Asian patients**) gets a lower **3%–5%** target; alcohol restricted; **sarcopenia in NASH cirrhosis inverts the advice — avoid a caloric deficit**. ⚠ The abstract box prints BPA 5's BMI cut-offs as 26/24 and the body text as 25/23 — the page carries the body-text values and flags the discrepancy
- [[aga-2022-lean-nafld]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Diagnosis and Management of NAFLD in Lean Individuals — Long, Noureddin, Lim. Gastroenterology 2022;163:764–774 — **15 numbered BPA, ungraded**; **lean NAFLD = NAFLD + BMI <25 kg/m² (non-Asian) or <23 kg/m² (Asian race)**; **7%–20% of NAFLD is lean** (~6.8% of biopsy-confirmed NASH — older, more often Asian, fewer metabolic-syndrome components but similar advanced-fibrosis risk), ≈**8–10 million US adults**; lower cross-sectional prevalence of advanced fibrosis and cirrhosis than overweight/obese NAFLD
- [[aga-2019-daa-hcc]] — AGA 2019 CPU (Expert Review): Interaction Between Oral DAAs for Chronic HCV and HCC — Singal, Lim, Kanwal. Gastroenterology 2019;156(8):2149–2157 — ungraded; **no evidence DAAs promote or accelerate HCC** — incidence *falls* with time after SVR (overall **1.14% → 0.84%/y**; with cirrhosis **2.27% → 1.73%/y**), and early post-DAA tumours are **missed prevalent disease** (mean **9.8 months**, range 2–22, from DAA exposure to diagnosis, independent of SVR); **non-characterised nodules at DAA initiation RR 2.83**; semiannual surveillance to 15 y cost-effective at $100,000/quality-adjusted life-year (QALY); treat HCV **before** HCC develops
- [[aga-2017-care-after-svr-hcv]] — AGA 2017 CPU (Expert Review): Care of Patients Who Have Achieved SVR After Antiviral Therapy for Chronic HCV — Jacobson, Lim, Fried. Gastroenterology 2017;152:1578–1587 — ungraded; **SVR12** = undetectable HCV RNA 12 wk post-treatment on an assay with **lower limit of detection (LLOD) <12 IU/mL**; late relapse **0.2%–0.5%**; HCC **1%–4%/y** in HCV cirrhosis → **ultrasound ± AFP every 6 months**, and the decision is made on the **pretreatment** fibrosis stage (**F3/F4 surveil, F0–F2 do not**); cross-sectional imaging when US is limited (obesity, indeterminate lesions, poor parenchymal visualisation). Covers post-SVR follow-up that [[aasld-idsa-2023-hcv]] does not
- [[aga-2018-surgical-risk-perioperative-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2018 CPU (Expert Review): Surgical Risk Assessment and Perioperative Management in Cirrhosis — Northup, Friedman, Kamath. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2019;17(4):595–606 — ungraded; **severity of liver disease is the single most important determinant** of postoperative mortality; **CTP A** may generally proceed *absent thrombocytopenia or CSPH*, **selected CTP B** are elective candidates, **CTP C are not**; MELD has largely supplanted CTP (mortality rises **linearly**; **MELD <16** lower risk) but **underperforms with severe portal hypertension and preserved synthetic function**; **emergency surgery ≥2-fold RR** not captured by MELD models; futility — **ASA V** median survival **2 days**, 90% mortality at 14 d, 100% at 90 d. Distinct from [[acg-2025-perioperative-cirrhosis]]
- [[aga-2020-palliative-care-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Palliative Care Management in Cirrhosis — Tandon, Walling, Patton, Taddei. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19(4):646–656 — ungraded; median survival **2 y decompensated vs 12 y compensated**, quality of life **parallels advanced cancer**; symptom-prevalence table for end-stage liver disease (fatigue, depression, sleep disturbance, pain, cognitive impairment); **most physical symptoms are best treated by optimally managing the cirrhosis complication itself**, pruritus being an exception. AGA counterpart to [[aasld-2022-palliative-cirrhosis]]
- [[aga-2020-bariatric-surgery-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Bariatric Surgery in Cirrhosis — Patton, Heimbach, McCullough. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19(3):436–445 — **10 numbered Best Practice Advice, ungraded**; obesity in ~**30%** of cirrhosis and it drives decompensation (**14% normal weight → 31% overweight → 43% obese**); obesity is **no longer an absolute contraindication to liver transplant** but raises waitlist mortality and postoperative complications; **sarcopenia in 40%–70%** of cirrhotics, sarcopenic obesity worse still, CT psoas measurement; feeds [[bariatric-surgery]] in [[cirrhosis]]
- [[aga-2016-low-grade-dysplasia-barretts]] — AGA 2016 CPU (Expert Review): Diagnosis and Management of Low-Grade Dysplasia in Barrett's Esophagus — Wani, Rubenstein, Vieth, Bergman. Gastroenterology 2016;151:822–835 — **16 numbered Practice Advice, ungraded**; the organizing claim is that the LGD *diagnosis itself* is the problem — interobserver agreement is fair-to-slight (**κ = 0.32** LGD, **κ = 0.15** indefinite-for-dysplasia, **κ = 0.14** between two expert central GI pathologists), **73–75% of community LGD is downgraded** to NDBE or indefinite on expert review, and reported progression spans **0.4%–13.4%/y** largely because of overcall rather than biology; hence expert-pathologist confirmation before any management decision
- [[aga-2018-barretts-screening-techniques]] — AGA 2018 CPU (Expert Review): New Screening Techniques in Barrett's Esophagus — Great Ideas or Great Practice? — the non-endoscopic and unsedated alternatives to screening upper endoscopy (swallowable cell-collection devices with molecular markers, transnasal endoscopy, capsule-based imaging), what each would have to demonstrate before displacing sedated endoscopy, and why population screening has not followed from the technology
- [[aga-2020-endoscopic-treatment-barretts-dysplasia]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Treatment of Barrett's Esophagus With Dysplasia and/or Early Cancer — Sharma, Shaheen, Katzka, Bergman. Gastroenterology 2020;158:760–769 — **16 numbered BPA, ungraded**; the procedural playbook for Barrett's endoscopic therapy, deliberately excluding nondysplastic BE. Histologic gradient: **HGD and T1a EAC → BET preferred over both surveillance and esophagectomy**; **confirmed *and persistent* LGD → BET and surveillance are both reasonable** (a deliberate non-verdict); **T1b EAC → BET a reasonable alternative to esophagectomy only when all three low-risk features hold — sm1 <500 μm, good-to-moderate differentiation, no lymphatic invasion** — and especially in poor surgical candidates
- [[aga-2020-post-endoscopy-esophageal-adenocarcinoma]] — AGA 2020 CPU (**Commentary**): Reducing Rates of Post-Endoscopy Esophageal Adenocarcinoma — Wani, Gyawali, Katzka. Gastroenterology 2020;159:1533–1537 — ⚠ **carries no Best Practice Advice, no numbered recommendations and no grading** (narrative prose only — never cite it as a numbered or graded AGA recommendation). Imports the post-colonoscopy CRC framework into the esophagus and names the esophageal equivalent **post-endoscopy esophageal cancer (PEEC)** — EAC and/or BE-related HGD found within a finite window, typically **1 year**, after an index endoscopy; argues this neoplasia is mostly **prevalent disease the endoscopy failed to find or fully remove**, making it a measure of *examination quality*, not Barrett's natural history; four named solutions plus proposed quality indicators
- [[aga-2022-barretts-new-technology]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): New Technology and Innovation for Surveillance and Screening in Barrett's Esophagus — the successor to the 2018 screening-techniques update; wide-area transepithelial sampling, tissue-systems pathology and risk-prediction assays, advanced imaging, and where each currently sits relative to the Seattle protocol
- [[aga-2021-refractory-h-pylori]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Refractory *H. pylori* Infection — Shah, Iyer, Moss. Gastroenterology 2021;160:1831–1841 — ungraded; **refractory = persistently positive *non-serologic* test ≥4 weeks after ≥1 completed guideline-recommended course**, and must be distinguished from **recurrent** (initially negative, later positive); US resistance data sparse and dated (clarithromycin 13–15%, metronidazole 17–25%, levofloxacin 29%); **immigrants may carry their country of origin's resistance pattern**; selecting salvage therapy by **prior antibiotic exposure is not inferior to susceptibility testing** (only 38% of US providers ask); resistance mechanisms by drug
- [[aga-2021-ibd-colorectal-dysplasia]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Surveillance and Management of Colorectal Dysplasia in IBD — Murthy, Feuerstein, Nguyen, Velayos. Gastroenterology 2021;161:1043–1051 — ungraded; adopts a **modified Paris Classification** and **abandons dysplasia-associated lesion or mass (DALM), adenoma-like mass (ALM) and "flat dysplasia"**; every visible lesion described by size, morphology, border clarity, ulceration, location, whether within past/current colitis, completeness of resection and the imaging modality used; start surveillance **8–10 years after diagnosis of colonic IBD**, and **immediately at diagnosis of [[primary-sclerosing-cholangitis|PSC]]**; **staging biopsies from multiple segments** at that first exam set the future interval
- [[aga-2021-colonic-diverticulitis]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Medical Management of Colonic Diverticulitis — **14 verbatim Best Practice Advice, ungraded**; supplies the BPA 7 antibiotic-selection cut-points (C-reactive protein >140 mg/L, white blood cell count >15 × 10⁹/L, 86 mm vs 65 mm CT segment length) and the BPA 13 removal of episode count as an indication for elective resection
- [[aga-2020-pancreas-cancer-screening]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Pancreas Cancer Screening in High-Risk Individuals — Aslanian, Lee, Canto. Gastroenterology 2020;159(1):358–362 — ungraded; **>5% lifetime risk** defines a high-risk individual (general population ~**1.3%**); **familial pancreas cancer = kindred with pancreas cancer in ≥2 first-degree relatives** not meeting another hereditary cancer syndrome's criteria; 85–90% sporadic, 5–10% familial, 3–5% inherited syndromes; 5-y survival by stage (localized **37.4%**, regional 12.4%, metastatic **2.9%**)
- [[aga-2022-short-bowel-syndrome]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Short Bowel Syndrome — 12 Best Practice Advice; SBS definition by residual length + the measurement convention, 3-group anatomic classification and prognosis, nutrition/micronutrient monitoring table, anatomy-specific diet and ORS rules, PN weaning probability by time since resection, teduglutide with its malignancy gate, intestinal-transplant referral triggers
- [[aga-2019-autoimmune-pancreatitis]] — AGA 2019 "Here and Now" expert column: Management of Autoimmune Pancreatitis — ⚠ **narrative column, NOT a CPU: zero numbered BPAs, no GRADE ratings**; AIP type 1 vs IDCP type 2 phenotype table, ICDC cardinal features (named, components not printed), serum IgG4 ≥2× ULN (sens 53%/spec 99%), FNB-over-FNA rationale, steroids-not-stents for mild jaundice. **Its Figure 1 treatment algorithm is an unextractable raster image — every steroid dose/taper/interval is therefore missing**
- [[aga-2023-ai-colon-polyp]] — AGA 2023 CPU (Commentary): Role of Artificial Intelligence in Colon Polyp Diagnosis and Management — CADe (ADR RR 1.43, inconsistent real-world), CADx optical diagnosis + ASGE PIVI thresholds (≥90% NPV / >90% surveillance agreement), computer-aided quality assessment; adoption barriers
- [[aga-2024-tailored-polypectomy]] — AGA 2024 CPU (Expert Review): Appropriate and Tailored Polypectomy — 12 BPA; cold snare polypectomy (CSP) <10 mm, no hot forceps, cold-vs-hot decision for intermediate 10–19 mm, serrated→cold, hot snare polypectomy (HSP) for pedunculated >10 mm, clips only ≥20 mm proximal, referral ≥20 mm/difficult/recurrent; optical-diagnosis table (Paris/NICE/WASP/Kudo/Japan NBI Expert Team (JNET))
- [[aga-2024-esophageal-immunity-infection]] — AGA 2024 CPU (Expert Review): Esophageal Dysfunction Due to Disordered Immunity and Infection — 10 BPA; infectious esophagitis (Candida/HSV/CMV dx + doses), lymphocytic esophagitis, EoE→motility, HES/EGPA (AEC >1500), connective-tissue/dermatologic esophageal disease, secondary achalasia (COVID/Chagas)
- [[aga-2024-poem-advances]] — AGA 2024 CPU (Expert Review): Advances in POEM — 12 BPA; preprocedure workup (endoscopy/TBE/HRM/FLIP), achalasia subtype selection (Type III preferred), EGJOO/spastic case-by-case, antibiotic/tunnel/myotomy-length questions, post-POEM reflux + surveillance, same-day discharge, failed-myotomy POEM>PD (62% vs 27%)
- [[aga-2025-endoscopic-resection-crc]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Endoscopic Resection for Early Colorectal Cancer — en-bloc imperative; ESD-preferred / eFTR <2 cm; unified curative-resection criteria; deep submucosal invasion reappraised (~2.6% LNM, not independent) → individualized organ preservation; low- vs high-risk T1 surveillance
- [[aga-2025-crc-blood-tests]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Blood Tests for CRC Screening — cell-free DNA (cfDNA) blood tests (Shield/Freenome) performance + CMS criteria (3-yr, ≥74% sens/≥90% spec); inferior to colonoscopy/FIT/stool-DNA by modeling; best for patients declining other screening; programmatic follow-up colonoscopy after positive; not multicancer panels
- [[aga-2025-noncolorectal-cancer-ibd]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update: Noncolorectal Cancer Screening and Vaccinations in IBD — 13 BPA; skin (melanoma/NMSC), cervical, and anal cancer screening; human papillomavirus (HPV)/HepB/pneumococcal/respiratory syncytial virus (RSV)/zoster vaccination by agent and age
- [[aga-2025-heds-gi]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update: GI Manifestations and Autonomic/Immune Dysfunction in Hypermobile EDS — 16 BPA; DGBI overlap, dysmotility, POTS/MCAS associations, diagnostic criteria, multidisciplinary management
- [[aga-2026-therapeutic-endoscopy-ibd]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Role of Therapeutic Endoscopy in IBD — EBD first-line for short (≤4–5 cm) simple strictures (target 18–20 mm, wire-guided controlled radial expansion (CRE), no intralesional steroids, ≤5 cm predicts surgery-free survival); endoscopic stricturotomy/FCSEMS alternatives; hold antithrombotics/steroids/GLP-1 RA, no pre-EBD antibiotics; fistula endotherapy (drainage > closure, short/simple tracts); EMR/ESD for visible dysplasia
- [[aga-2026-cdiff-adults]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Management of C. difficile Infection in Adults — Fischer, Vaughn, Peery, Kelly. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2026;24:2359–2369 — 14 BPA, ungraded; multistep testing (GDH/PCR → toxin EIA) but treat PCR+/toxin− if suspicion high; severe = WBC >15k and/or Cr >1.5× baseline, fulminant adds shock/ileus/megacolon (1–3%, 30–40% mortality); fidaxomicin 200 BID ×10 d preferred for all nonfulminant CDI, vancomycin 125 QID acceptable, metronidazole fulminant-only; ≥50% stool reduction by day 3–5; no test of cure; first recurrence = switch class (vancomycin taper-pulse or fidaxomicin EXTEND); microbiota therapy after 2nd recurrence or earlier if >65/immunocompromised/skilled nursing facility (SNF); fulminant vancomycin 500 q6h + IV metronidazole + rectal if ileus, FMT or colectomy at 48 h; suppressive vancomycin 125 daily; **bezlotoxumab no longer commercially available**; OVP and probiotics not advised
- [[aga-2026-cdiff-ibd]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Management of C. difficile Infection in IBD — multistep toxin-based testing (NAAT alone over-diagnoses colonization); fidaxomicin preferred for initial CDI (vancomycin if unavailable, no metronidazole); continue immunosuppression (no drug-class differential CDI risk); exclude CMV if no improvement at 48–72h; microbiome therapy (fecal microbiota, live-jslm (RBL)/fecal microbiota spores, live-brpk (VOS)/FMT) after ≥1 recurrence; OVP secondary prophylaxis; no probiotics
- [[aga-2026-electrosurgery]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Use of Electrosurgery in Therapeutic Endoscopy — Draganov, Hwang, Podboy, Yang. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2026;24:1494–1502 — 13 BPA, ungraded; duty cycle/crest factor, current density (D = I/area, inverse to contact area), tissue resistance by segment and wall layer, analog vs microprocessor ESUs, ESU settings table (Olympus ESG 300 / ConMed CE200 / ERBE VIO 300D–VIO3), dispersive pad + pacemaker/implantable cardioverter-defibrillator (ICD) rules, underwater = coagulation-predominant (use saline, raise power), soft coag <200 Vp but deep-injury risk, **hot avulsion on cutting current** (EndoCut I, effect 3, duration 1, interval 3), APC power-vs-flow, sphincterotomy risk is technique-driven, third-space 10 W knife-shaft hemostasis
- [[aga-2026-hcc-surveillance]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Risk Stratification and Emerging Surveillance Strategies for HCC — semiannual US+AFP preferred (sens 63% vs 51% US alone); survey Child-Pugh A/B cirrhosis + select non-cirrhotic HBV; GALAD/novel biomarkers not yet ready and MCD panels not for HCC; PAGE-B/REAL-B for non-cirrhotic HBV; consider harms; prevent cirrhosis
- [[aga-2026-gastric-polyps]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Management of Gastric Polyps — subtypes (FGP/GHP/GA/pyloric & oxyntic gland adenoma/G-NET); evaluate polyp + background mucosa; test/treat H. pylori for adenomatous/hyperplastic polyps; PPIs continue with FGPs; resect largest polyps; surveillance keyed to histology + GIM/atrophic gastritis (LGD 1y, HGD 6mo)
- [[aga-2026-inpatient-ibd]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Inpatient Management of Adults With IBD — admission criteria; supportive care + complication/infection workup; VTE prophylaxis for all; ASUC IV methylpred 60 mg/d, assess at 72h, prepare rescue (infliximab/cyclosporine/JAK), early surgical consult; CD obstruction/abscess/perianal pathways; discharge without requiring CRP normalization
- [[aga-2020-cpu-pancreatic-necrosis]] — AGA 2020 Clinical Practice Update: Management of Pancreatic Necrosis — Baron, DiMaio, Wang, Morgan. Gastroenterology 2020;158(1):67–75 — best practice advice on walled-off necrosis, step-up approach, drainage timing/route
