- [[aga-2026-hemorrhoids]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Diagnosis and Treatment of Hemorrhoids — anoscopy before treatment; rubber band ligation (RBL) vs infrared coagulation (IRC) (RBL more durable for prolapse/recurrent bleeding); topical steroids ≤2 wk; special populations (active IBD defer to remission, pregnancy conservative, cirrhosis distinguish from rectal varices/IRC if coagulopathic); grade 4 → hemorrhoidectomy
- [[aga-2026-refractory-constipation]] — AGA 2026 Clinical Practice Update: Evaluation and Management of Refractory Constipation — ARM+BET/biofeedback before labeling refractory constipation (RC); exclude secondary causes; transit testing off and on max laxatives; defecography; exhaustive OTC/FDA + off-label (pyridostigmine) trials; surgery only after confirming slow transit + excluding pelvic floor dysfunction; diverting loop ileostomy as diagnostic trial
- [[aga-2025-ascites-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update: Management of Ascites, Volume Overload, and Hyponatremia in Cirrhosis — sodium restriction + lowest-effective diuretics; diagnostic paracentesis (SAAG/cell count/culture); hepatic hydrothorax → thoracentesis; albumin 6–8 g/L for LVP >5 L; TIPS/LT for refractory; inpatient IV loop diuretics + nephrology co-management of anasarca; hyponatremia ladder (vasoconstrictors, hypertonic saline, vaptans, RRT)
- [[aga-2025-hepatitis-delta]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update: Management of Hepatitis Delta — universal anti-HDV screening of all HBV patients → reflex HDV RNA; VCTE/FIB-4 staging + HCC surveillance; peg-IFN-α (only US therapy, ~50% relapse); bulevirtide (EMA-approved, US expanded-access) and pipeline agents
- [[aga-2025-flip-esophageal]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update: Incorporating FLIP Into Esophageal Practice — 11 BPA (incl. post-antireflux/bariatric surgery); high-quality EGD before FLIP; use when HRM/TBE inconclusive; normal EGJ opening has high NPV for EGJOO; not for GERD diagnosis; intraprocedural FLIP during POEM/LHM; role in EoE remodeling and post-achalasia-treatment symptoms
- [[aga-2025-sclerosing-mesenteritis]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Sclerosing Mesenteritis — CT signs (misty mesentery, fat ring sign, tumoral pseudocapsule); usually diagnosed without biopsy; only 1%–6% need treatment; asymptomatic → observe; symptomatic → prednisone + tamoxifen 10 mg BID; surgery non-curative
- [[aga-2025-lifting-agents]] — AGA 2025 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Endoscopic Lifting Agents — submucosal cushion separates lesion from MP; contrast (indigo carmine/methylene blue) demarcates margins; saline ± contrast acceptable, sessile serrated lesions (SSLs) lift with any agent; ESD always needs injection (avoid intralesional); dilute epinephrine for cold EMR; dynamic injection technique
- [[aga-2025-crohns-pharm]] — AGA 2025 Living Guideline: Pharmacologic Management of Moderate-to-Severe Crohn's Disease — updated drug positioning incl. IL-23 p19 inhibitors and JAK inhibitors; first-line advanced therapy
- [[aga-2025-gastroparesis]] — AGA 2025: Management of Gastroparesis — 4-hour scintigraphy, prokinetics, antiemetics, pyloric therapies (G-POEM), nutrition
- [[aga-2025-hbv-reactivation]] — AGA 2025: Prevention and Treatment of HBV Reactivation — risk stratification by immunosuppression class and HBsAg/anti–hepatitis B core antigen (anti-HBc) status; antiviral prophylaxis
- [[aga-2025-barretts-surveillance]] — AGA 2025: Surveillance of Barrett's Esophagus — surveillance intervals by dysplasia grade, sampling, role of adjunct technologies
- [[aga-2025-cade-colonoscopy]] — AGA 2025 Living Guideline: Computer-Aided Detection (CADe)-Assisted Colonoscopy — ADR impact and conditional recommendation framework
- [[aga-2024-uc-pharm]] — AGA 2024 Living Guideline: Pharmacologic Management of Moderate-to-Severe Ulcerative Colitis — drug positioning incl. ozanimod, upadacitinib, risankizumab; first-line advanced therapy
- [[aga-2024-pouchitis]] — AGA 2024: Management of Pouchitis and Inflammatory Pouch Disorders — antibiotic first-line, chronic antibiotic-dependent/refractory pathways, biologics for Crohn's-like pouch disease
- [[aga-2024-fmt]] — AGA 2024: Fecal Microbiota-Based Therapies for Select GI Diseases — conventional FMT and FDA-approved products (RBL, fecal microbiota spores) for recurrent C diff; conditional against routine IBD/IBS use
- [[aga-2024-barretts-eet]] — AGA 2024: Endoscopic Eradication Therapy of Barrett's Esophagus and Related Neoplasia — EET for HGD/IMC and LGD, EMR-first for visible lesions, post-CE-IM surveillance
- [[aga-2024-chs]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Cannabinoid Hyperemesis Syndrome — CVS Rome IV criteria; proposed CHS criteria (cannabis >1 yr & >4×/wk, resolution ≥6 mo off cannabis); hot-water bathing supportive; topical capsaicin/haloperidol acute, cannabis cessation + amitriptyline long-term, avoid opioids
- [[aga-2024-upper-endoscopy-quality]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update: High-Quality Upper Endoscopy (Expert Review) — 9 ungraded BPAs; preparation, inspection, photodocumentation, biopsy protocols (Barrett's, updated Sydney); Figure 2 classification systems (LA, Hill, Prague, Forrest)
- [[aga-2024-intestinal-ultrasound]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Role of Intestinal Ultrasound in IBD — BWT + Doppler hyperemia metrics, comparable to MRE/CTE, treat-to-target thresholds (CD sonographic response ≥25% BWT reduction, postop BWT ≥3mm+fecal calprotectin (FC)≥50; UC BWT <2.8mm, ASUC ≥20% at 48h), special populations, no dysplasia surveillance
- [[aga-2024-cvs]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Cyclic Vomiting Syndrome — Rome IV Dx criteria; ANMS-CVSA mild vs moderate–severe strata (drive prophylaxis); 4 phases; workup to exclude mimics (no routine gastric emptying scan); TCA first-line prophylaxis + triptan/antiemetic abortive combos; ED management
- [[aga-2024-pcab]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Integrating Potassium-Competitive Acid Blockers — 9 Best Practice Advice across GERD/HP/PUD; not first-line where superiority unproven (cost/safety); use for severe EE (LA C/D) & in place of PPIs for H. pylori
- [[aga-2024-diet-nutrition-ibd]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Diet and Nutritional Therapies in IBD — 12 Best Practice Advice; Mediterranean diet baseline; EEN/CDED induction in CD (steroid-sparing, preop prehab); PN for intestinal failure/SBS (thresholds) + GLP-2 agonists; malnutrition screening (drop serum proteins); vitamin D/iron/B12 monitoring; RD co-management
- [[aga-2024-ibd-malignancy]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Management of IBD in Patients With Malignancy — cancer risk from inflammation vs from drugs; Table 1 (drug-class action per cancer type: thiopurine→stop for lymphoma/NMSC, anti-TNF→stop for melanoma, newer classes no change); reassuring prior-cancer registry data (CESAME/SAPPHIRE); ICI colitis, chemo & hormone-therapy effects on IBD
- [[aga-2024-pregnancy-gi-liver]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Pregnancy-Related GI and Liver Disease — 13 Best Practice Advice; NVP/hyperemesis (B6+doxylamine), constipation, endoscopy timing (2nd trimester), IBD med safety + delivery, ERCP radiation minimization, cholecystectomy safe (2nd tri), intrahepatic cholestasis (bile acids, UDCA), acute fatty liver, HBV antiviral in 3rd trimester
- [[aga-2024-glp1-endoscopy]] — AGA 2024 Rapid Clinical Practice Update (Communication): GLP-1 Receptor Agonists Prior to Endoscopy — individualized approach over routine cessation; standard fast (8h solid/2h liquid) + no GI symptoms → proceed; ASA consensus (hold day-of daily / 1 wk weekly) surfaced but not evidence-based; liquid diet day-before alternative; multisociety "no data to support stopping"
- [[aga-2024-vasoactive-albumin-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2024 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Vasoactive Drugs and IV Albumin in Cirrhosis — 12 Best Practice Advice across AVH, ascites/SBP, and HRS-AKI; octreotide first-choice for AVH (Table 1 regimens) before endoscopy ×2–5 d; albumin 6–8 g/L at LVP >5 L, 1.5→1 g/kg for SBP, NOT for uncomplicated ascites; no vasoconstrictors post-LVP/SBP; terlipressin drug-of-choice for HRS-AKI (peripheral line, no intensive care unit (ICU)), ischemia/hypoxemia/ACLF-3/MELD≥35 caveats
- [[aga-acg-2023-constipation]] — AGA-ACG 2023: Pharmacologic Management of Chronic Idiopathic Constipation — Chang, Chey, Imdad et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2023
- [[aga-2023-biomarkers-cd]] — AGA 2023: Role of Biomarkers for the Management of Crohn's Disease — fecal calprotectin/CRP thresholds for treat-to-target monitoring vs endoscopy
- [[aga-2023-biomarkers-uc]] — AGA 2023: Role of Biomarkers for the Management of Ulcerative Colitis — fecal calprotectin/CRP-guided monitoring strategy in symptomatic and remission states
- [[aga-2023-bloating]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Belching, Abdominal Bloating, and Distention — 15 Best Practice Advice; gastric vs supragastric belching (impedance-pH/HRM), Rome IV bloating/distention criteria, symptom-driven testing (2-wk diet trial, celiac serology, anorectal testing for pelvic-floor), central neuromodulators + brain–gut behavioral therapy, probiotics not recommended, APD
- [[aga-2023-extraesophageal-gerd]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Extraesophageal GERD — 10 Best Practice Advice; no single test proves GER causes EER; 50–60% of EER symptoms are not GERD; test before empiric PPI when no typical symptoms; PPI improvement ≠ GERD confirmation; one 12-wk PPI trial then objective testing (EGD + reflux monitoring); pH-impedance on-therapy for refractory; anti-reflux surgery only with objective GERD (PPI non-response predicts surgical failure)
- [[aga-2023-epi]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency — 15 Best Practice Advice; fecal elastase-1 initial test (<100 µg/g EPI, valid on PERT); imaging cannot diagnose; PERT ≥40,000 USP lipase/meal + H2/PPI; fat-soluble vitamins + DXA q1–2y
- [[aga-2023-acute-hepatic-porphyria]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Diagnosis and Management of Acute Hepatic Porphyrias (Wang et al., Gastroenterology 2023;164:484–491) — 12 Best Practice Advice; AHP types (AIP/HCP/VP/ALAD); random urine ALA+PBG ≥5× ULN screen → genetic confirmation; hemin/IV carbohydrate for attacks; givosiran for ≥4 attacks/year; HCC + CKD surveillance
- [[aga-2023-alpha-gal-syndrome]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Commentary): Alpha-Gal Syndrome for the GI Clinician (McGill et al., CGH 2023;21:891–896) — Take-Home Messages; GI-predominant phenotype; 3-part diagnosis (symptoms + ↑alpha-gal IgE + avoidance-diet response); mammalian-product avoidance; tick-bite precautions; cetuximab/gelatin cross-reactivity
- [[aga-2023-crc-screening-risk-stratification]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Risk Stratification for Colorectal Cancer Screening and Post-Polypectomy Surveillance (Issaka, Chan, Gupta; Gastroenterology 2023;165:1280–1291) — Best Practice Advice; average risk starts at 45; any FDR with CRC treated as increased risk (start 40, or 10 y before youngest relative's dx) → colonoscopy; >75 y individualized (lag-time-to-benefit ≥5 y)
- [[aga-2023-nafld-noninvasive-biomarkers]] — AGA 2023 Clinical Practice Update (Expert Review): Noninvasive Biomarkers in NAFLD (Wattacheril, Abdelmalek, Lim, Sanyal; Gastroenterology 2023;165:1080–1088) — Best Practice Advice; FIB-4-first sequential algorithm with age adjustment (>65 y → FIB-4 <2.0), VCTE/MRE/ELF second-tier, prevalence-dependent NPV/PPV, biopsy for indeterminate/discordant NITs
- [[aga-2022-aki-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2022: Diagnosis and Management of AKI in Cirrhosis — 14 BPA statements; HRS nomenclature update; terlipressin dosing/contraindications; FEUrea over FENa; RRT as bridge to transplant
- [[aga-2022-hcc-systemic]] — AGA 2022: Systemic Therapy for Hepatocellular Carcinoma — first-line atezo+bev, sequencing of TKIs/immunotherapy (IO), Child-Pugh considerations
- [[aga-2022-ibs-c]] — AGA 2022: Pharmacological Management of IBS-C — linaclotide (strong), plecanatide, tenapanor, tegaserod, lubiprostone, PEG
- [[aga-2022-ibs-d]] — AGA 2022: Pharmacological Management of IBS-D — rifaximin, alosetron, eluxadoline, loperamide, TCAs; against bile-acid sequestrants for global symptoms
- [[aga-2022-obesity-pharm]] — AGA 2022: Pharmacological Interventions for Adults with Obesity — semaglutide/liraglutide/phentermine-topiramate/naltrexone-bupropion added to lifestyle
- [[aga-2022-hamartomatous-polyposis]] — USMSTF 2022 (joint AGA/ASGE/ACG), Boland/Idos/Durno et al., *Gastroenterology* 2022;162:2063–2085 — **rebuilt 2026-09-08, 54 → 318 lines**, after the 2026-09-06 lint flagged it as failing the recommendation-capture rule. ⚠ **Not an AGA CPU and it contains no "Best Practice Advice"** — it is a consensus statement carrying **14 GRADE-rated Question/Recommendation pairs, all now verbatim** with strength + evidence quality. The source **numbers none of them**; the wiki-local labels R1–R14 are assigned here and flagged as such so nothing downstream cites "BPA 7" as if the paper said it. Tables 2/3/4/5 reproduced natively; Table 4 split per syndrome with all six footnotes verbatim. ⚠ **The "NCCN 2020" surveillance column is a comparison column inside USMSTF's own Table 4, not a citation to NCCN** — no NCCN hereditary-syndrome guideline is ingested, so it must be read as *"USMSTF reports that NCCN advises…"*, with NCCN's own qualifiers unknown to this wiki
- [[aga-2022-recurrent-pancreatitis-endoscopy]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Approach to Recurrent Acute and Chronic Pancreatitis — 8 BPA; EUS-first evaluation of unexplained (R)AP, minor-papilla endotherapy in pancreas divisum, ERCP controversy in standard anatomy, CP stone/stricture endotherapy vs surgery, FCSEMS for CP biliary strictures, EUS-guided celiac plexus block (EUS-CPB) not routine
- [[aga-2022-ppi-deprescribing]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): De-Prescribing of Proton Pump Inhibitors — Targownik, Fisher, Saini. Gastroenterology 2022;162:1334–1342 — 10 ungraded BPA; **Table 1 indications grid** (definite/conditional/not-indicated × long-term >8 wk vs acute ≤8 wk); the UGIB high-risk criteria that block de-prescribing; twice-daily → once-daily step-down; rebound acid hypersecretion; **BPA 10 — a PPI adverse event is never itself a reason to stop**
- [[aga-2022-refractory-celiac]] — AGA 2022 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Refractory Celiac Disease — Green, Paski, Ko, Rubio-Tapia. Gastroenterology 2022;163:1461–1469 — **10 ungraded BPA** (no systematic review, so no evidence/strength ratings). refractory celiac disease (RCD) defined as persistent malabsorptive symptoms **plus villous atrophy despite ≥12 months of strict gluten-free diet** (~1% of celiac patients; villous atrophy is necessary but **not sufficient**); the exclusion sequence (confirm the original diagnosis, exclude ongoing gluten exposure, exclude alternative causes) before the label is applied; **RCD1 vs RCD2 split by IEL immunophenotype, not by symptoms** — RCD2 = aberrant clonal IELs **≥20% of total IELs**, a low-grade intraepithelial malignancy that can progress to EATL and ulcerative jejunoileitis; **open-capsule budesonide** first-line for both subtypes, with the update's own caveat that doses and tapers "have not been examined rigorously"; **no FDA-approved therapy for RCD**
- [[aga-2021-ici-colitis-hepatitis]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Immune Checkpoint Inhibitor Colitis and Hepatitis — Dougan, Wang, Rubio-Tapia, Lim. Gastroenterology 2021;160:1384–1393 — 15 ungraded BPA; CTCAE v5 grading; endoscopy before steroids and **colonic ulceration as the only predictor of needing second-line therapy** (CTCAE grade is not); infliximab/vedolizumab weeks 0/2/6; budesonide only for ICI microscopic colitis; **grade-4 hepatitis thresholds** and methylprednisolone dosing
- [[aga-2021-medically-refractory-gastroparesis]] — AGA CPU (Expert Review): Management of Medically Refractory Gastroparesis — **8 Best Practice Advice**, all verbatim, plus Table 1 recreated with both footnotes. ⚠ **Cite the year as 2022** (*Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol* 2022;20:491–500) — the slug keeps the raw filename's "2021", so any source-priority comparison must use 2022. Supplies the 4-hour scintigraphic severity bands and **the only trial-duration requirement in the corpus** (failed diet *and* metoclopramide, ≥4 weeks each), which AGA 2025's refractory definition lacks. AGA 2025 governs where they differ (GES, G-POEM); this CPU supplies the selection filter and procedural setting. ⚠ Source-internal conflict reproduced not resolved: aprepitant is 80 mg/d in Table 1 and 125 mg/d in the text
- [[aga-2021-atrophic-gastritis]] — AGA 2021: Diagnosis and Management of Atrophic Gastritis — Shah, Piazuelo, Kuipers, Li (12 BPA statements; OLGA/OLGIM; Sydney protocol; AIG serologies; type I NETs)
- [[aga-2018-functional-gi-symptoms-ibd]] — AGA 2019 CPU (Expert Review): Functional GI Symptoms in Patients With IBD — Colombel, Shin, Gibson. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2019;17:380–390 (filename year 2018 = online year) — **14 Best Practice Advice statements, numbered by the document itself, but carrying no GRADE, strength or quality rating**; the breath-testing guidance in it is attributed to cited North American consensus guidelines, not issued as AGA BPA. Calprotectin and CRP interpretation bands, the acquired-mechanism list (bile acid diarrhea, SIBO, exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, carbohydrate malabsorption, pelvic floor), low-FODMAP, psychological therapy, and the against-FMT statement
- [[aga-2021-ibd-elderly]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Management of IBD in Elderly Patients — Ananthakrishnan, Nguyen, Bernstein. Gastroenterology 2021;160:445–451 — **15 Best Practice Advice statements, numbered within three groups (Diagnosis 1–3, Treatment 1–10, Health maintenance 1–2) — no continuous 1–15 numbering, and no GRADE rating, strength or quality label on any statement**; fed [[ibd-in-older-adults]]
- [[aga-2018-biologic-biosimilar-switching-ibd]] — AGA CPU **Commentary**: Switching Between Biologics and Biosimilars in IBD — Raffals, Nguyen, Rubin. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2019;17:818–823 (filename year 2018 = online year; print issue 2019) — **numbers nothing and grades nothing**; narrative under 7 headings, and the regulatory standards in it are FDA/Public Health Service Act (PHSA)/Biologics Price Competition and Innovation Act (BPCI) standards, not AGA's. FDA biosimilarity vs interchangeability, extrapolation, the 2019 approved-biosimilar snapshot, CT-P13 switch studies incl. NOR-SWITCH subgroup numbers, and the antidrug-antibody cross-reactivity rule forbidding a switch; fed [[anti-tnf-agents]]
- [[aga-2021-crohns-pharm]] — AGA 2021: Medical Management of Moderate-Severe Luminal and Perianal Fistulizing Crohn's — Feuerstein et al. Gastroenterology 2021;160:2496–2508 — 11 recommendation groups; foundational, largely superseded for drug positioning by 2025 (no IL-23 p19 / JAK inhibitors)
- [[aga-2017-endoscopic-bariatric-therapies]] — AGA 2017 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Bariatric Therapies — Abu Dayyeh, Edmundowicz, Thompson. Gastroenterology 2017;152:716–729 — **7 Best Practice Advice, ungraded** (explicitly "a consensus summary of expert opinion without a formal systematic review" — never cite as graded); gastric vs small-bowel EBT classification and mechanisms; per-device fill volumes, dwell times and trial data for all five balloons plus ESG (%TBWL 16.8/18.2/19.8% at 6/12/18 mo), POSE, aspiration therapy, TransPyloric Shuttle, Full Sense; EndoBarrier DJBS trial **terminated early for 3.5% hepatic abscess**; bariatric surgery benchmark 20%–40% TBWL; obesity = 35% of US adults and ~21% of US health expenditure; **no BMI cut-point, contraindication list, or age criterion given**; feeds [[endoscopic-bariatric-therapies]], [[intragastric-balloon]], [[obesity]], [[bariatric-surgery]]
- [[aga-2021-early-complications-bariatric-surgery]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Early Complications After Bariatric/Metabolic Surgery — Kumbhari, Cummings, Kalloo, Schauer. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19:1531–1537 — **7 Best Practice Advice, ungraded**; **early = <90 days**; ~205,000 US primary operations in 2018 (~95% sleeve gastrectomy or Roux-en-Y gastric bypass), 90-day mortality 0.1%–0.3% and complications 7%–10% (1.3% and 20.1% if >65 y); the staple-line-leak mechanism and its **downstream functional stenosis** corollary; the **paradigm shift** — do not close the orifice, lower intragastric pressure so the leak closes by secondary intention, and judge response by diet tolerance and shrinking cavity, **not** by orifice size; pneumatic dilation technique (30–40 mm balloons, rarely >35 mm, 20 psi × 1–3 min, side-by-side scope, as early as 2 wk); stent rules (PCSEMS ≤18 mm body, 3–4 wk max); double pigtail / septotomy / endoscopic vacuum therapy (>80% success); feeds [[bariatric-surgery-leak]], [[bariatric-surgery]]
- [[aga-2021-intragastric-balloons]] — AGA 2021: Intragastric Balloons for Obesity — Muniraj et al. Gastroenterology 2021;160:1799–1808 — 7 GRADE recommendations numbered by the document itself in Table 3 (2 strong: concomitant lifestyle, PPI prophylaxis); intragastric balloon (IGB) + lifestyle > lifestyle alone (BMI 30–40 kg/m²); **prescribes no implantation duration or removal timing**; serious AE 5.6% vs 1.1% (RR 3.07); sibutramine 10 mg/day null (RR 1.50, 0.81–2.78); Rec 5's "clinical judgment on an individual basis" caveat; two genuine non-recommendations (no specific device, no specific antiemetic regimen); post-removal options
- [[aga-2021-cirrhosis-coagulation]] — AGA 2021: Coagulation Disorders in Cirrhosis — rebalanced hemostasis; against routine FFP/platelet/vitamin K correction by INR/platelet count; periprocedural and VTE prophylaxis guidance
- [[aga-2020-nvugib-endoscopic-therapies]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Therapies for Non-Variceal Upper GI Bleeding — Mullady, Wang, Waschke. Gastroenterology 2020;159:1120–1128 — 10 ungraded BPA; **Table 1 supplies the Rockall components and the full AIMS-65 score with its mortality bands** (both previously absent from the corpus); emergent/urgent/elective 12 h / 24 h / >24 h tiers; device settings for heater probe, bipolar, monopolar hemostatic forceps (MHF) soft coag and TC-325; OTSC and hemostatic-powder niches; TAE-vs-surgery and the no-prophylactic-TAE rule. ⚠ Its 9 g/dL cardiovascular disease (CVD) transfusion target is **superseded by [[acg-2021-ugib]]**
- [[aga-2021-crc-chemoprevention]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Chemoprevention for Colorectal Neoplasia — Liang, Shaukat, Crockett. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19:1327–1336 — 9 BPA, **only 4 positive**; "should"/"may" *is* the grading; aspirin's benefit needs **10–19 years' latency**, hence the BPA 1 conjunction (age <70 + life expectancy ≥10 y + 10-y CVD risk ≥10% + not high bleeding risk); bleeding risk **not** reduced at ≤100 mg; explicit do-not-use for non-aspirin NSAIDs, calcium, vitamin D, folic acid and statins — with **harm signals**: calcium→SSL RR 2.66, calcium+vit D→SSL RR 3.82, folic acid→advanced/multiple adenomas. **Excludes hereditary syndromes and IBD**
- [[aga-2021-bleeding-gastric-varices]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Management of Bleeding Gastric Varices — Henry, Patel, Patton, Saad. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19:1098–1107 — **12 BPA, all expert opinion** (no natural-history studies, no prospective trials). gastric varices (GV) prevalence 17%–25% vs esophageal varices (EV) up to 85%, but bleeding is more severe; 16%–45% at 3 y; same risk in noncirrhotic portal hypertension (pHTN). Proposes a **location-based classification (cardiofundal / lesser curve / distal)** over Sarin, plus size + high-risk stigmata; **Saad-Caldwell vascular classification** mapped before any definitive therapy. The pathway: hemostasis → **portal-venous-phase CT/magnetic resonance (MR)** → multidisciplinary decision. **Cardiofundal → cyanoacrylate is the only definitive endoscopic therapy** (butyl over octyl; **no lipiodol**; embolization 0.7%); **lesser curve → band ligation is often definitive**. **gastrorenal shunt (GRS) present + no severe pHTN complications → BRTO** (>90% hemostasis, <5%–7% rebleed at 1 y, HE *improves*, but EV progress in 30%–35%); **coronary-vein inflow or comorbid pHTN → TIPS**, always with embolization. Full Table 2 initial-management algorithm (octreotide, ceftriaxone 1 g ≤7 d, Hgb 7 g/dL, erythromycin 250 mg IV, endoscopy <12 h). **No primary prophylaxis**
- [[aga-2021-gi-perforations-endoscopic-management]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Endoscopic Management of Perforations in the GI Tract — Lee, Kedia, Stavropoulos, Carr-Locke. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19:2252–2261 — **16 BPA**; the closure device is chosen by **defect size**, organ by organ: esophagus/stomach **TTSC or OTSC <2 cm, suturing >2 cm** (stomach: TTSC <1 cm, OTSC 1–3 cm, endoloop+TTSC >3 cm), duodenum **type 1 >3 cm → urgent surgery**, colon **right colon/cecum limited to TTSC**. Two absolute surgical indications (unstable, or delayed with peritoneal signs). CO₂ at low flow; keep the defect non-dependent; Gram-negative + anaerobic coverage. Carries the ERCP **type 1 vs type 2 (Stapfer)** distinction with sphincterotomy-technique prevention, the ESD/EMR perforation rates and risk factors, POEM delayed barrier failure 0.2%–1.1% with posterior-orientation preference, and the five-step colon-EMR avoidance sequence (target sign)
- [[aga-2021-malignant-alimentary-tract-obstruction]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Optimal Management of the Malignant Alimentary Tract Obstruction — Ahmed, Lee, Thompson, Faulx. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021;19:1780–1788 — **14 ungraded BPA** across three sites (no evidence/strength rating, and no should/may surrogate grading). Esophagus: **do not routinely stent a resectable cancer** (migration 29.9%, chest pain 15.6%, ingrowth 2.2%, perforation 1.5%, 30-day mortality 10%); enteral feeds do not relieve dysphagia; SEMS or brachytherapy, **never laser or PDT**; **covered (fully or partially covered) not uncovered**, anchored by suture or over-the-scope clip, with **≥2 cm margin proximally and distally**, small (16–18 mm) vs large (20–23 mm) diameter trade-off, larger diameter across the GEJ to resist migration, and **reflux in 70%–100%**. Gastric outlet: **surgical gastrojejunostomy if life expectancy >2 months** + good functional status + surgically fit, else enteral stent; **laparoscopic favored over open**; stent contraindicated with multiple luminal obstructions, life expectancy of a few weeks, or impaired gastric motility (linitis plastica) → **venting gastrostomy**; EUS-GE where expertise exists but **no FDA-approved dedicated device** (meta-analysis 12 studies/285 patients: 92% technical, 90% clinical, 12% AE, mostly stent misdeployment); **85% ERCP technical success after an enteral stent across the ampulla**. Colon: SEMS as bridge to surgery incl. right-sided (**proximal to the splenic flexure**) — 91.9%/71.7% technical/clinical success, 1.7% perforation, 3.9% migration; **seeding OR 46 after stent perforation**; bevacizumab within 6 weeks caveat; Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) 0–1 vs 2–3 survival flip
- [[aga-2020-young-adult-onset-crc]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Young Adult–Onset Colorectal Cancer Diagnosis and Management — Boardman, Vilar, You, Samadder. *Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol* 2020;18:2415–2424 — **ingested by the 2026-09-07 cron, which never added it here; backfilled 2026-09-08.** Methodology is verbatim *"a summation of relevant scientific publications, expert opinion statements, and current practice guidelines"* — **no systematic review and no evidence grading or strength rating on any BPA**. Defines young adult–onset CRC verbatim as *"CRC diagnosed in individuals 18 - <50 years of age."* Germline multigene panel testing for every young-onset case irrespective of family history; universal tumor MMR/MSI; fertility preservation discussed **before** chemoradiation; germline result drives surgical extent. ⚠ Predates the shift of average-risk screening to 45 and argues neither side of it — [[usmstf-2021-crc-screening-age]] and [[acg-2021-crc-screening]] are newer and govern that question
- [[aga-2020-gastric-intestinal-metaplasia]] — AGA 2020: Management of Gastric Intestinal Metaplasia — Gupta, Li, El Serag et al. Gastroenterology 2020;158:693–702
- [[aga-2020-eoe]] — AGA/Joint Task Force on Allergy-Immunology Practice Parameters (JTF) 2020: Management of Eosinophilic Esophagitis — PPIs, topical steroids, dietary elimination, dilation; conditional GRADE recommendations
- [[aga-2020-sibo]] — AGA 2020 CPU (Expert Review): Small Intestinal Bacterial Overgrowth — Quigley, Murray, Pimentel. *Gastroenterology* 2020;159:1526–1532 — **9 ungraded Best Practice Advice**; pathophysiologic risk-factor table (Table 1) replacing a flat risk list; **AGA issues no breath-test cutoff at all**, calling the test "never fully validated compared with culture" and the 2-peak criterion unvalidated; **>10³ CFU/mL coliforms on fresh aspirate** (note `>`, where ACG writes `≥`); 7–10 day antibiotic course. **Contradicts ACG on 12 points** — cardinal symptom (diarrhea vs bloating), whom to test (AGA makes no recommendation either way), post-treatment retesting (AGA: unnecessary if symptoms respond), and the dose of all four antibiotics incl. a ~40% rifaximin gap
- [[aga-2020-ida]] — AGA 2020: Gastrointestinal Evaluation of Iron-Deficiency Anemia — bidirectional endoscopy, celiac testing, non-invasive H. pylori, capsule for ongoing iron deficiency anemia (IDA)
- [[aga-2024-ida-management]] — AGA 2024 CPU: Management of Iron Deficiency Anemia (DeLoughery, Jackson, Ko, Rockey; CGH 2024;22:1575–1583) — 15 Best Practice Advice statements (unrated); oral iron principles (ferrous sulfate, once-daily/alternate-day, vitamin C), IV iron (1–2 infusion formulations, pseudo-allergy), condition-specific repletion (bariatric, IBD, PHG, GAVE, celiac, small-bowel angioectasias); oral + IV iron tables
- [[aga-2025-pvt-cirrhosis]] — AGA 2025 CPU: Management of Portal Vein Thrombosis in Cirrhosis (Davis, Lim, Francis, Ahn; Gastroenterology 2025;168:396–404) — 11 Best Practice Advice statements (unrated); chronicity/occlusion stratification, urgent AC for intestinal ischemia, no-AC for chronic cavernoma, CTP-class anticoagulant selection table, timing/monitoring, PVR-TIPS
- [[aga-2020-probiotics]] — AGA 2020: Probiotics in the Management of GI Disorders — Su et al. Gastroenterology 2020;159:697–705 — 8 GRADE recommendations, **none Strong**; conditional FOR (C diff prevention on antibiotics — **superseded by AGA 2026**, pouchitis 8-strain combo in adults with an IPAA for UC, NEC in preterm infants), conditional AGAINST (pediatric acute gastroenteritis, **framed for US/Canada**), trial-only (Crohn's/UC/IBS/C diff treatment, where *L rhamnosus* ATCC 53103 carries a harm signal); **no dose, colony-forming-unit count or duration anywhere — the named strains are the recommendation**; evidence current to December 2018; prebiotics out of scope
- [[aga-2023-eus-gallbladder-drainage]] — AGA 2023: EUS-Guided Gallbladder Drainage in Acute Cholecystitis — Irani, Sharzehi, Siddiqui. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2023;21(5):1141–1147
- [[aga-2023-gpoem-gastroparesis]] — AGA 2023: Gastric Peroral Endoscopic Myotomy for Gastroparesis — Khashab, Wang, Cai. Gastroenterology 2023;164(7):1329–1335
- [[aga-2023-interventional-eus-vascular]] — AGA 2023: Interventional EUS for Vascular Investigation and Therapy — Ryou, DeWitt, Das, Shami. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2023;21(7):1699–1705
- [[aga-2023-ostomies]] — AGA 2023: Management of Ostomies — Hedrick, Sherman, Cohen-Mekelburg, Gaidos. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2023;21(10):2473–2477
- [[aga-2024-full-thickness-resection]] — AGA 2024: Endoscopic Full-Thickness Resection for GI Subepithelial Lesions — D'Souza, Yang, Diehl. Gastroenterology 2024;166(2)
- [[aga-2024-ibd-endoscopic-scoring]] — AGA 2024: Endoscopic Scoring Systems in IBD — Buchner, Farraye, Iacucci. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2024;22(11):2188–2196
- [[aga-2024-ibd-pain]] — AGA 2024: Pain Management in IBD — Keefer, Hashash, Szigethy, Mayer. Gastroenterology 2024;166(6):1182–1189
- [[aga-2025-endoscopic-enteral-access]] — AGA 2025: Endoscopic Enteral Access — Micic, Martin, Fang. Gastroenterology 2025;168(1):164–168
- [[aga-2025-gastric-cancer-screening]] — AGA 2025 CPU: gastric cancer screening & surveillance in increased-risk US individuals; 15 BPAs
- [[aga-2025-nonampullary-duodenal]] — AGA 2025 CPU: nonampullary duodenal lesions — cold- vs hot-snare EMR by size, surveillance; 13 BPAs
- [[aga-2019-mild-moderate-uc]] — AGA 2019 Institute Guideline: Management of Mild-to-Moderate Ulcerative Colitis — Ko, Singh, Feuerstein et al. Gastroenterology 2019;156:748–764 — **13 numbered GRADE recommendations** (Table 4; 2 strong, 8 conditional, and **3 explicit "no recommendation / knowledge gap"** statements — probiotics, curcumin, FMT). Supplies the three decision inputs the wiki lacked: the AGA mild–moderate severity definition, the extent definitions with their cm qualifiers (extensive = proximal to splenic flexure · left-sided <50 cm · proctitis <15–20 cm from anus), and the **oral mesalamine dose bands (low <2, standard 2–3, high >3 g/d)**; 5-ASA equivalences (6.75 g balsalazide ≈ 2.4 g 5-ASA; 4 g sulfasalazine ≈ 1.6 g); topical dosing regimens; once-daily dosing; do-not-switch-formulation. ⚠ Effect estimates are RR of **failure** — RR <1 favours the agent. **Biologics and immunomodulators are explicitly out of scope**
- [[aga-2018-initial-management-acute-pancreatitis]] — AGA 2018 Institute Guideline: Initial Management of Acute Pancreatitis — Crockett, Wani, Gardner, Falck-Ytter, Barkun. Gastroenterology 2018;154:1096–1101 — **8 numbered GRADE recommendations** (1A/1B, 2–8; **4 strong, all moderate quality**). Scoped to the **first 48–72 h**; explicitly excludes diagnosis and late complications. Goal-directed fluid therapy with its named perfusion targets but **no recommendation on rate/volume/duration and none between normal saline (NS) and LR**; **against hydroxyethyl starch** (multiple organ failure (MOF) OR 3.86, 1.24–12.04); against prophylactic antibiotics — **extended in the text to mild AP**, with the pre-2002 benefit vanishing after 2002; against routine urgent ERCP without cholangitis; early oral feeding within 24 h (**clear liquids not required**); enteral over parenteral; nasogastric (NG) or nasoenteral; **same-admission cholecystectomy** (PONCHO; no greater surgical difficulty); brief alcohol intervention. Names severity-prediction tools only as a **knowledge gap** — grades none
- [[aga-2016-microscopic-colitis]] — AGA 2016 Institute Guideline: Medical Management of Microscopic Colitis — **9 numbered GRADE recommendations** (the document numbers and grades all 9; Table 2 gives the strength definitions, Table 3 the ratings). Budesonide **9 mg daily** first line for induction (strong, high — vs mesalamine), stop at 8 weeks; budesonide **6 mg daily × 6 mo** maintenance offered to relapsers only (RR 0.34, 0.19–0.6), with a 3/6 mg alternating × 12 mo alternative and osteoporosis screening; mesalamine 3 g, bismuth salicylate 8–9 tablets TID, prednisolone as alternatives only when budesonide is not feasible; **against** cholestyramine+mesalamine, Boswellia serrata and probiotics. **Medical management only — the document states it does not address diagnosis, and gives no histologic criteria**
- [[aga-2017-tdm-ibd]] — AGA 2017 Institute Guideline: Therapeutic Drug Monitoring in IBD — Gastroenterology 2017;153:827–834 — **5 statements, unnumbered in the source** (4 conditional; one is literally *"No recommendation / knowledge gap"* — on proactive TDM). Reactive vs proactive TDM; anti-TNF maintenance trough targets **IFX ≥5, ADA ≥7.5, CZP ≥20 µg/mL**; antidrug-antibody titer interpretation and the optimize-vs-switch-within-class-vs-switch-class decision; routine pre-treatment TPMT testing with its dose-reduction table; **6-TGN 230–450 pmol/8×10⁸ RBCs, monotherapy only**. Explicitly excludes vedolizumab and ustekinumab for lack of data
- [[aga-2017-crohns-after-surgical-resection]] — AGA 2017 Institute Guideline: Management of Crohn's Disease After Surgical Resection — Nguyen, Loftus, Hirano, Falck-Ytter, Singh, Sultan. Gastroenterology 2017;152:271–275 — **6 numbered GRADE recommendations** (Table 3; **1 strong, 5 conditional**). Scoped tightly to the **asymptomatic patient with an ileocolonic anastomosis and no macroscopic residual disease**. Supplies the postoperative decision inputs the wiki lacked: **Table 4's two illustrative risk groups with their characteristics AND their recurrence rates** (lower risk 20% clinical / 30% endoscopic; higher risk 50% / 80%, both >18 mo without intervention), early prophylaxis **within 8 weeks** vs endoscopy-guided treatment, anti-TNF and/or thiopurines over other agents, an explicit recommendation **against mesalamine/5-ASA, budesonide and probiotics**, the **3–12 month nitroimidazole cap** (cumulative dosing → peripheral neuropathy), the **conditional-vs-strong split** on 6–12 month monitoring (strong only if the patient is *not* on prophylaxis), and what to do at asymptomatic endoscopic recurrence. States plainly that **no validated score predicts postoperative recurrence** and that Rutgeerts is unvalidated in prophylaxis trials
- [[aga-2017-acute-liver-failure]] — AGA 2017 Institute Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Acute Liver Failure — Flamm, Yang, Singh, Falck-Ytter. Gastroenterology 2017;152:644–647 — **11 numbered recommendations** (Table 3), and the grading is the point: **9 rest on very low quality evidence, 4 are recommendations against doing something, 2 are formally "No recommendation"** (Recs 9 and 11 — extracorporeal liver support and NAC in non-acetaminophen ALF — despite being phrased with the word "recommends"; ⚠ do not cite either as a graded recommendation). Only **Rec 10 (NAC in acetaminophen-associated ALF) is strong**, and even that is very low quality. Supplies the **MELD 30.5 fixed cut-off** and the pooled KCC-vs-MELD accuracy table, **hepatitis E virus (HEV) testing in pregnant women** (56% mortality, range 33–71%), HSV testing (DNA > immunoglobulin M (IgM)), the quantified liver-biopsy yield (**diagnosis changed in 18%**; >50% necrosis → 3-fold mortality), and the five-RCT/410-patient negative base for **empiric ICP-lowering therapy**. Suggests *against* routine universal Wilson-disease and (immunocompetent) varicella-zoster virus (VZV) testing — where newer [[acg-2023-alf]] governs the page
- [[aga-2015-asymptomatic-neoplastic-pancreatic-cysts]] — AGA 2015 Institute Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Asymptomatic Neoplastic Pancreatic Cysts — Vege, Ziring, Jain, Moayyedi. Gastroenterology 2015;148:819–822 — **10 numbered recommendations**; **Rec 8 (high-volume centre for surgery) is the only Strong one**, Rec 1 is explicitly **not GRADE-rated** ("motherhood statement"), and **every graded statement is Very low quality**. The AGA arm of the cyst-guideline disagreement, and materially different from [[acg-2018-pancreatic-cysts]]: EUS-FNA only with **≥2 of 3** high-risk features (size ≥3 cm ~3×, solid component ~8×, dilated MPD — **no significant association in AGA's own review**), MRI **q2 years for a cyst of any size**, **stop at 5 years** if unchanged, **against** post-resection surveillance without HGD/cancer. Supplies the trade-off numbers: transformation **0.24%/year**, postoperative mortality **2%** (centres of excellence) vs **6.6%** (Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER)), major morbidity **30%**, 5-year survival with invasive cancer **~28%**. ⚠ Scope is *asymptomatic* cysts only and **excludes main-duct IPMN without side-branch involvement**, NETs, SPN; gives **no cyst-fluid cutoffs and no typing criteria**. Newer ACG 2018 governs the page
- [[aga-2015-lynch-syndrome]] — AGA 2015 Institute Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Lynch Syndrome — Rubenstein, Enns, Heidelbaugh, Barkun. Gastroenterology 2015;149:777–782 — **7 recommendation statements, and the document numbers nothing** (2 Strong/Moderate — tumor-test all CRCs, and surveillance colonoscopy; 5 conditional), plus one explicit **non-recommendation**: no position on IHC vs MSI vs both. Supplies the qualifiers the page was missing: colonoscopy from **20–25 y or 5 y before the youngest family CRC diagnosis, whichever comes first**; **PREMM >5% is derived for 25-year-olds** and is lower in middle age; second-stage *BRAF*/MLH1-methylation testing is **not 100% specific** (misses Lynch syndrome in ≤10%, and **~75% of MLH1-absent tumors are sporadic**). Surveillance effect size CRC burden **OR 0.23** (0.13–0.41), mortality **OR 0.06** (0.00–0.93), +7 y life expectancy and cheaper than no surveillance. **Aspirin is suggested as an offer** (conditional/low; incidence rate ratio (IRR) 0.56, 0.32–0.99) — but **dose and frequency are unknown**. States there are **no data** for mutation-specific start ages or intervals. Explicitly does **not** cover extracolonic surveillance or surgical management
- [[aga-2015-upper-gi-biopsy-dyspepsia]] — AGA 2015 Institute Guideline: Role of Upper GI Biopsy to Evaluate Dyspepsia in the Absence of Visible Mucosal Lesions — Yang, Brill, Krishnan, Leontiadis. Gastroenterology 2015;149:1082–1087 — **8 numbered statements with strength + evidence quality (Table 3)**. Answers "the mucosa looks normal — biopsy what?" in three segments: **against** routine esophagus/gastroesophageal (GE)-junction biopsy regardless of immune status (Strong/Very low); **for** routine gastric body + antrum biopsy for *H. pylori* **only if HP status is unknown** (Strong/Moderate immunocompetent, Strong/Very low immunocompromised); **against** routine duodenal biopsy for celiac absent other risk features (Conditional/Very low), with the **immunocompromised duodenum the exception** (graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) post-allogeneic transplant + opportunistic infections). Supplies the **5-biopsy updated Sydney System** with all five sites and their positioning distances (antrum lesser + greater curve within **2–3 cm of pylorus**; corpus lesser curve **4 cm proximal to angularis**; corpus greater curve **8 cm from cardia**; incisura) — **all specimens in one jar**. Special staining discouraged as a reflex in both stomach and duodenum. ⚠ Every statement is conditioned on **dyspepsia as the sole indication, no visible lesions, Rome III definition, and no prior HP treatment**
- [[aga-2015-acute-diverticulitis]] — AGA 2015 Institute Guideline: Management of Acute Diverticulitis — Stollman, Smalley, Hirano. Gastroenterology 2015;149:1944–1949 — **11 numbered Questions, 11 boxed recommendations; the document numbers its questions, not its recommendations** (Table 3 lists all 11 in question order). **10 conditional, 1 strong** (mesalamine, Strong/Moderate — the only grade above low/very-low). Supplies the decision [[diverticulitis]] had no inputs for: **suggest against elective resection after a first uncomplicated episode**, with the trade-off quantified (~20% 5-y recurrence · <5% future complications or emergency surgery on medical management · ~10% short-term surgical complications, higher >65 y · **25% long-term** distention/cramping/altered defecation/incontinence). Also: **a normal CT does not exclude colonic neoplasm** (post-episode colonoscopy yield **CRC 15/1000, advanced adenomas 38/1000**); aspirin split out from nonaspirin NSAIDs (**suggests against routinely advising aspirin avoidance** — RR 1.25 for any episode, 1.13 and non-significant for complicated); fiber dose unknown and **never tested against recurrent disease**. ⚠ **Three internal discrepancies in the document itself** (aspirin quality graded very-low in the box but Low in Table 3; "seeds" present in Table 3 and absent from the box; "history of acute diverticulitis" vs "history of diverticulitis") are shown rather than resolved. Gives **no Hinchey or severity classification and no antibiotic agents, doses or durations** — those stay ACG-2026-sourced
- [[aga-2017-elastography-liver-fibrosis]] — AGA 2017 Institute Guideline: Role of Elastography in the Evaluation of Liver Fibrosis — Lim, Flamm, Singh, Falck-Ytter. Gastroenterology 2017;152:1536–1543 — **14 numbered focused Questions, 11 recommendation boxes / 12 statements; the document numbers its questions, not its recommendations**. One **etiology-specific VCTE cutoff** each: **HCV 12.5 kPa · HBV 11.0 kPa · alcohol-associated 12.5 kPa · post-SVR 9.5 kPa to rule out advanced fibrosis · 19.5 kPa to decide on EGD for high-risk varices · 17.0 kPa for CSPH before elective nonhepatic surgery**. Every cutoff was deliberately chosen to **minimize false negatives**, and the resulting over-labeling is accepted and quantified. Only **one strong recommendation** (VCTE over APRI/FIB-4 for cirrhosis in HCV, moderate quality); everything else conditional on low/very-low evidence. **Two formal "no recommendation — knowledge gap" statements, both in NAFLD** — pooled estimates were biased by exclusion of BMI ≥30 and of failed measurements. MRE is compared with VCTE in only two settings: VCTE preferred in HCV, MRE suggested in **high-prevalence (~30%) NAFLD** only (119 fewer per 1000 falsely labeled). Proprietary serum assays, pSWE/acoustic radiation force impulse (ARFI), 2D-SWE and test combinations are explicitly **out of scope**
- [[aga-2020-brca-crc-pancreatic-screening]] — AGA 2020 Clinical Practice Update: Colorectal and Pancreatic Cancer Risk and Screening in *BRCA1*/*BRCA2* Carriers — Kupfer, Gupta, Weitzel, Samadder. Gastroenterology 2020;159:760–764 — ⚠ **Commentary format: the document issues NO numbered Best Practice Advice and assigns NO GRADE ratings of its own** (the GRADE 2/3 labels in the text belong to the Cancer of the Pancreas Screening (CAPS) Consortium). **CRC:** carrier status alone does **not** change screening — average-risk guidelines unless there is a **first-degree relative with CRC or advanced adenoma** (then screen at **age 40**) or symptoms (rectal bleeding, IDA → expedited evaluation). The threshold logic is the usable part: a **2-fold** risk is the criterion for earlier screening, and *BRCA1*'s **1.5-fold** is not clearly sufficient; [[lynch-syndrome]] is **10–17-fold** for scale. **PDAC:** CAPS reached **93% consensus** on screening *BRCA2* carriers with **≥1 affected FDR or ≥2 affected relatives of any degree** (GRADE 2) but **no consensus** for *BRCA1* (GRADE 3); **NCCN differs** — first- **or second**-degree relative. EUS/MRCP, high-volume center, preferably research setting. Risk estimates by gene: PDAC OR **2.58** (*BRCA1*) vs **6.20** (*BRCA2*); CRC meta-analysis OR **1.49** (*BRCA1*) vs **1.10**, NS (*BRCA2*)
- [[aga-2019-opioid-induced-constipation]] — AGA 2019 Institute Guideline: Medical Management of Opioid-Induced Constipation — Gastroenterology 2019 — **6 graded statements (Table 3)**, numbered by drug category. Laxatives first-line for every patient (**Strong/Moderate**); PAMORAs gated on **laxative-refractory** OIC — naldemedine **Strong/High**, naloxegol **Strong/Moderate**, methylnaltrexone **Conditional/Low**; [[lubiprostone]] and [[prucalopride]] get a formal **"no recommendation — evidence gap"** (⚠ not a recommendation against, and it is OIC-specific: both are recommended for CIC by [[aga-acg-2023-constipation]], which excluded OIC). The decision content is in the narrative, not the graded statements: Rome IV OIC criteria, the **Bowel Function Index** and its **≥30** escalation cutoff, and what "laxative refractory" operationally means (scheduled, ≥2 classes, not as-needed). **No doses are printed for any PAMORA**
- [[aga-2019-lab-evaluation-functional-diarrhea-ibs-d]] — AGA 2019 Institute Guideline: Laboratory Evaluation of Functional Diarrhea and Diarrhea-Predominant IBS in Adults — Smalley, Falck-Ytter, Carrasco-Labra, Wani, Lytvyn, Falck-Ytter. Gastroenterology 2019;157:851–854 — **7 numbered GRADE recommendations** (Table 3; **2 strong, 4 conditional, 1 formally "No recommendation / knowledge gap"**). Half of it is about **not** testing. Closes the threshold gap on [[chronic-diarrhea]]: fecal **calprotectin 50 µg/g** (Sn 0.81, Sp 0.87) or **lactoferrin 4.0–7.25 µg/g** (Sn 0.79, Sp 0.93) — and states that raising the calprotectin cutoff to **100–164 µg/g** markedly cuts sensitivity for no specificity gain; **against erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR)/CRP** as an IBD screen (CRP a fallback only when fecal markers are unavailable/uncovered); ***Giardia* antigen or PCR, Strong/High** (>95% Sn and Sp — the only High rating); **against routine O&P other than *Giardia*** *only* absent travel/immigration; celiac IgA-tissue transglutaminase (tTG) **plus** a second test for IgA deficiency (Strong/Moderate); bile acid diarrhea testing with the four assays and their availability. ⚠ **Rec 7 (anti-cytolethal distending toxin B (CdtB) / anti-vinculin serology for IBS) is "No recommendation — knowledge gap"**, not a recommendation against — Sp ~90% but Sn only 20–40%. Applies **only** to the immunocompetent adult with watery diarrhea ≥4 weeks and no alarm features, bleeding, steatorrhea, relevant family history or travel. Self-declared expiry 5 years (2024)
- [[aga-2021-colonoscopy-quality]] — AGA 2021 CPU (Expert Review): Strategies to Improve Quality of Screening and Surveillance Colonoscopy — Keswani, Crockett, Calderwood. Gastroenterology 2021;161:701–711 — **15 numbered BPA, ungraded** (the document states no quality-of-evidence or strength rating was carried out — never cite a GRADE level from it). Six indicators with explicit numeric goals + aspirational targets (adequate prep ≥90%/≥95% by BBPS ≥6 with each segment ≥2; cecal intubation; withdrawal time; ADR; serrated detection rate (SDR); adverse events measured without a target), assigned to the **unit** vs **endoscopist** level; seven unnumbered best practices (split-dose prep, HD colonoscopes, right-colon second look, cold snare 3–9 mm, refer benign complex polyps for endoscopic not surgical resection, documentation, guideline-concordant intervals); explicit triage rule for resource-limited units — prioritize **cecal intubation rate, prep quality and ADR**
- [[aga-2016-infection-ercp-endoscopes]] — AGA 2016 CPU (**Commentary** — numbers nothing, grades nothing; never cite a recommendation number or GRADE level to it): Infection Using ERCP Endoscopes — Petersen, Koch, Ginsberg. Gastroenterology 2016;151:46–50 — the bioburden-margin argument behind every reprocessing rule: endoscopes are Spaulding "semicritical" and so get high-level disinfection (HLD) rather than sterilization, but carry **10⁹–10¹² organisms** at procedure completion against a Sterility Assurance Level of **10⁻⁶**; bedside precleaning removes **10³**, manual washing a further **10⁶**, leaving the last **10⁶–10⁹** to liquid chemical germicides — so the margin depends on optimal reprocessing of every instrument every day. Duodenoscopes are the worst case (elevator region and cable channels, biofilm). Traces the 2014 CDC Chicago carbapenem-resistant Enterobacteriaceae (CRE) cluster → 2015 FDA safety alerts and expert panel → 2016 Senate report and coordinated FDA/industry duodenoscope recall, and judges the proposed solutions "cumbersome and unproven"
- [[aga-2017-mstf-endoscope-reprocessing-commentary]] — AGA 2017 CPU (**Commentary** — it comments on another body's guideline and issues none of its own): Commentary on the 2016 Multi-Society Task Force Endoscope Reprocessing Guidelines — Hutfless, Kalloo. Gastroenterology 2017;152:494–496 — **the AGA Institute officially endorsed the 2016 MSTF guidelines**. Recommendation counts across versions (2003 = **34**, 2011 = **39**, 2016 = **41**) and the central judgment that, although outbreaks occurred despite adherence to the 2011 version, the 2016 guidelines "are not dramatically different" — **only 2 new recommendations**, one of them (#24) a *suggestion* to culture or further disinfect if feasible. The two substantive 2016 changes: alignment with the 2015 FDA reprocessing communications, and greater monitoring/tracking of the scope through clinical units and cleaning rooms including event timing and who performs each key step. ⚠ Cite the **counts** to this commentary, never the MSTF recommendations themselves — **the underlying MSTF 2016 document is not in `raw/`**
- [[aga-2023-telemedicine]] — AGA 2023 CPU (**Commentary** — no numbered statements, no BPA, no grades of any kind): Telemedicine in Gastroenterology — Gellad, Diamond, Crockett, Cross. Gastroenterology 2023;164:690–695 — scope is **outpatient** GI only. Two tables carry the guidance (Table 1 appropriate uses of in-person / video / phone / video group / e-consult; Table 2 best practices across regulatory, technology, rapport, documentation, communication, billing, provider). Excellent candidates = stable chronic digestive disease (GERD, IBS, chronic constipation, chronic liver disease, chronic pancreatitis, IBD); poor candidates = limited device access or broadband, or anyone needing an examination. Outcome split: **new acute** condition by telemedicine → more follow-up in-person/ED visits; **chronic** condition → fewer subsequent hospitalizations. IBD meta-analysis (17 RCTs, n = 2571): better IBD-specific quality of life and fewer clinic visits, **no difference in remission, disease activity or self-efficacy**. Explicit position on video-vs-telephone access disparities by age, race and insurance. Licensure in the patient's state is named the main barrier
- [[aga-2020-ibd-covid-19]] — AGA 2020 CPU (**Expert Commentary** — no numbered Best Practice Advice, no GRADE ratings; the only numbered list is 6 Take-Home Points): Management of IBD During the COVID-19 Pandemic — Rubin, Feuerstein, Wang, Cohen. Gastroenterology 2020;159:350–357 — the durable part is the **three-scenario immunosuppression ladder** (not infected / infected without illness / confirmed illness) and its Figure 1 algorithm, with the COVID-19 severity strata that select the therapy column (mild = not hospitalized **or** SpO₂ >94% without pneumonia; moderate = hospitalized with hypoxia and radiographic pneumonia; severe = mechanical ventilation ± pressors or end-organ damage). Patients with IBD are **not** at increased risk of infection; do not pre-emptively stop therapy. Hold thiopurines/methotrexate/tofacitinib; delay biologics 2 weeks; continue 5-ASA, topical rectal therapy, antibiotics, oral budesonide; avoid systemic steroids. Infusion centres stay open with a screening protocol (prescreening, door fever checks, **6-foot chair spacing**, masks/gloves, deep cleaning); **no elective switch to injectables, no home infusion**. Hospitalized severe IBD → **limit IV steroids to 3 days**, then calcineurin inhibitor or infliximab; serum CMV PCR instead of colonoscopy. Endoscopy urgent/emergent only, with **N95/N99/PAPR and double-gloving regardless of infection status**. ⚠ Epidemiology, testing landscape and investigational therapies (tocilizumab, sarilumab, baricitinib, hydroxychloroquine) are **April 2020 vintage — historical only**
- [[aga-aasld-acg-2012-nafld]] — **AGA–AASLD–ACG 2012 tri-society practice guideline**: The Diagnosis and Management of Non-alcoholic Fatty Liver Disease — Chalasani, Younossi, Lavine, Diehl, Brunt, Cusi, Charlton, Sanyal. Gastroenterology 2012;142:1592–1609 — the **oldest document in the corpus** and, unlike most AGA material, fully **GRADE-rated with 45 recommendations numbered by the document itself** (strength 1/2, quality A/B/C). Founding definitions of NAFL vs NASH vs NASH cirrhosis vs the NAFLD Activity Score; the Adult Treatment Panel III metabolic-syndrome criteria used to target biopsy; NAFLD Fibrosis Score components and the origin of its **−1.455 / 0.676** cut-offs with their sensitivity/specificity pairs; the weight-loss dose–response (**3–5% for steatosis, up to 10% for necroinflammation**); PIVENS in full. 2012 positions since reversed or superseded: **against** screening in primary care/diabetes/obesity clinics and against family screening; vitamin E 800 IU/day first-line in non-diabetic biopsy-proven NASH; pioglitazone permitted; metformin and UDCA rejected; statins safe but not a NASH therapy. ⚠ **Superseded by [[aasld-2023-nafld]] on every overlapping claim — read for what changed, not for practice.** Contains an internal inconsistency: its NAFLD-defining alcohol threshold (Rec 1, rated **2C**) is more permissive than the at-risk-drinking definition quoted in its own alcohol section
- [[aga-2022-noninvasive-crc-screening]] — AGA 2022 CPU (**Commentary** — no numbered statements, no BPA, no grades of any kind): Approach to the Use of Noninvasive CRC Screening Options — Burke, Lieberman, Feuerstein. Gastroenterology 2022;162:952–956 — Table 1 risk stratification (very high >20% / high 10–20% / average 4%); FIT threshold **≤20 µg Hb/g feces** and **no utility for serrated lesions**; multitarget stool DNA (MTsDNA) more sensitive but less specific, FDA-approved ages **45–85**; Septin 9 CRC sensitivity 48%, advanced-adenoma 11.2%, **endorsed by no guideline**; the hybrid model (annual FIT 45–49 → colonoscopy 50–70); **delay >6 months after a positive FIT** associated with more advanced adenoma, CRC and advanced-stage CRC; 3-generation family history required to route high-risk patients away from stool testing

### ASGE

- [[asge-2016-pancreatic-fluid-collections]] — ASGE 2016: Endoscopy in Inflammatory Pancreatic Fluid Collections — 12 GRADE recs; exclude neoplasm/pseudoaneurysm + wait for wall maturation before drainage; drain symptomatic/enlarging pseudocysts, infected PFCs failing conservative tx, sterile necrosis >8 wk; EUS when no bulge/portal HTN; endoscopy-before-surgery for pseudocysts; WON step-up (net-new to ACG 2024 acute pancreatitis)
- [[asge-2015-bowel-preparation]] — ASGE 2015: Bowel Preparation Before Colonoscopy — Saltzman, Cash, Pasha et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;81:781–794 (historical ASGE anchor; superseded on volume/timing/diet by usmstf-2025-bowel-prep — contradiction table on the source page; contributes net-new prep-quality scales (Aronchick/Ottawa/BBPS) + product-level preparation table; 6–8 L polyethylene glycol electrolyte lavage solution (PEG-ELS) via NG for urgent LGIB; PEG-ELS does not alter mucosal histology in suspected IBD)
- [[asge-2015-ercp-benign-biliary]] — ASGE 2015: Role of ERCP in Benign Diseases of the Biliary Tract — 10 GRADE recs; choledocholithiasis (EPLBD, cholangioscopy-guided lithotripsy), benign strictures (dilation+stent, FCSEMS), bile leaks (first-line ERCP), SOD (treat type I, not type III — EPISOD), congenital cysts; altered-anatomy access
- [[asge-2010-pud]] — ASGE 2010: Endoscopy in Peptic Ulcer Disease — 12 GRADE recs; DU biopsy not routine, GU biopsy/surveillance individualized; early EGD + repeat therapy for bleeding ulcers; no scope in acute perforation; balloon dilation for benign gastric outlet obstruction
- [[aga-acg-asge-aasld-2012-sedation-curriculum]] — Multisociety Sedation Curriculum for GI Endoscopy (AASLD/ACG/AGA/ASGE/SGNA, 2012) — a training curriculum with **no graded recommendations**; home of the ASA physical status, Ramsay and Modified Observer's scales, the 14-drug table with initial doses and antagonists, Table 4 anesthesiology-assistance indications, Aldrete ≥8 / Postanesthetic Discharge ≥9 thresholds, fasting intervals, and the pregnancy/lactation tables. Superseded on practice by [[asge-2018-sedation]] (1 source)
- [[asge-2018-sedation]] — ASGE 2018: Sedation and Anesthesia in GI Endoscopy — 8 GRADE recommendations; sedation continuum, ASA class, propofol/MAC vs moderate sedation, capnography for deep sedation, reversal agents
- [[asge-2015-antibiotic-prophylaxis]] — ASGE 2015: Antibiotic Prophylaxis for GI Endoscopy — 12 GRADE recommendations; not for IE prevention; prophylaxis for incomplete biliary drainage (ERCP), PEG/PEJ placement, EUS-FNA of cysts, cirrhosis + acute GI bleed
- [[asge-2015-ampullary-duodenal-adenomas]] — ASGE 2015: Endoscopy in Ampullary and Duodenal Adenomas — side-viewing exam/biopsy, EUS/ERCP staging, endoscopic papillectomy, nonampullary duodenal EMR, FAP surveillance
- [[esge-2021-ampullary-tumors]] — ESGE 2021: Endoscopic Management of Ampullary Tumors — 35 graded statements; en bloc up to 20–30 mm, surgery at >4 cm or >20 mm intraductal extension, RFA/cystotome salvage at ≤20 mm; direct snare without submucosal injection; no sphincterotomy before papillectomy; the 3/6/12-month-then-yearly-for-5-years surveillance schedule
- [[asge-2017-subepithelial-lesions]] — ASGE 2017: Subepithelial Lesions of the GI Tract — EUS layer-based differential, tissue acquisition (FNA/FNB/SINK/unroofing), resection-vs-surveillance thresholds (net-new to ACG 2023)
- [[asge-2025-gastrostomy-tubes]] — ASGE 2025: Gastrostomy Feeding Tubes — PEG indications/technique (pull vs introducer), periprocedural antibiotics, buried-bumper and other complication management, ethics of artificial nutrition
- [[asge-2024-therapeutic-eus-biliary]] — ASGE 2024: Role of Therapeutic EUS in Biliary Tract Disorders — EUS-guided biliary drainage (choledochoduodenostomy/hepaticogastrostomy), rendezvous, and gallbladder drainage after failed ERCP
- [[asge-2024-solid-pancreatic-masses]] — ASGE 2024: Role of Endoscopy in Solid Pancreatic Masses — EUS-FNB vs FNA, needle selection (fork-tip/Franseen), rapid on-site evaluation (ROSE), diagnostic yield and adverse events
- [[asge-2024-chronic-pancreatitis-endoscopy]] — ASGE 2024: Role of Endoscopy in Management of Chronic Pancreatitis — extracorporeal shock wave lithotripsy (ESWL) + ERCP for pancreatic-duct stones, ductal stenting, pseudocyst drainage, vs surgery
- [[asge-2023-esd]] — ASGE 2023: ESD for Early Esophageal and Gastric Cancer — ESD vs EMR, en-bloc/R0 resection rates, lesion-selection criteria, curative-resection criteria
- [[asge-2023-post-ercp-pancreatitis]] — ASGE 2023: Post-ERCP Pancreatitis Prevention — rectal NSAIDs, prophylactic pancreatic-duct stents, aggressive periprocedural hydration, patient/procedure risk stratification
- [[asge-2021-cholangitis]] — ASGE 2021: Management of Acute Cholangitis — Tokyo Guidelines severity grading, timing of biliary drainage, ERCP as first-line, antibiotics
- [[asge-2021-malignant-hilar-obstruction]] — ASGE 2021: Endoscopic Management of Malignant Hilar Obstruction — unilateral vs bilateral drainage, SEMS vs plastic stents, MRCP-guided targeted drainage
- [[asge-2020-achalasia]] — ASGE 2020: Management of Achalasia — POEM/pneumatic dilation/LHM comparison by subtype, role of HRM/FLIP/TBE, post-treatment surveillance
- [[asge-2019-choledocholithiasis]] — ASGE 2019: Endoscopic Management of Choledocholithiasis — high/intermediate/low CBD-stone probability stratification; EUS≈MRCP triage for intermediate risk; large-balloon dilation after sphincterotomy for large stones; early ERCP only for cholangitis/persistent obstruction in gallstone pancreatitis
- [[bsg-2017-common-bile-duct-stones]] — BSG 2017: Updated Guideline on the Management of Common Bile Duct Stones — 33 graded statements; defines "difficult stone disease"; ultrasound/LFT and MRCP-vs-EUS accuracy by pre-test probability; EPBD balloon rules with and without sphincterotomy; LBDE as co-equal to perioperative ERCP; gall-bladder-status rules after duct clearance
- [[asge-2019-radiation-proctopathy]] — ASGE 2019: Endoscopy for Bleeding from Chronic Radiation Proctopathy — Lee et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2019;90:171–182 — 3 GRADE recs (conditional/low); APC, bipolar electrocoagulation, heater probe, RFA all effective (no proven superiority); suggest against 4% formalin vs APC; cryoablation insufficient evidence
- [[asge-2017-small-bowel-bleeding]] — ASGE 2017: Role of Endoscopy in Suspected Small-Bowel Bleeding — Gurudu et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2017;85:22–31 — 8 GRADE recs; VCE-first algorithm; DAE/push enteroscopy for therapy; DAE as initial procedure in altered anatomy/angiectasia suspicion; angiography for unstable bleeding
- [[asge-2014-lgib]] — ASGE 2014: Role of Endoscopy in the Patient with Lower GI Bleeding — Gastrointest Endosc 2014;79:875–885 — 12 GRADE recs; colonoscopy first-line; EGD first for melena/unstable hematochezia; colonoscopy <24h for severe hematochezia; epinephrine+thermal/clip for diverticular bleeding, APC for angioectasias
- [[asge-2023-ergonomics]] — ASGE 2023: Role of Ergonomics for Prevention of Endoscopy-Related Injury — Pawa et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2023;98:482–491 — 2 strong + 3 conditional GRADE recs (all very low quality): ergonomics education, micro/macrobreaks, neutral monitor position, neutral bed height, antifatigue mats; ERI 57.7%, female OR 1.79
- [[asge-2023-post-transplant-biliary-strictures]] — ASGE 2023: Post–Liver Transplant Biliary Strictures — 4 conditional GRADE recs; MRCP-first dx (sens 94.9%); ERCP over PTBD (PTBD 8.47× allograft-failure odds); covered SEMS (cSEMS) over multiple plastic stents (fewer procedures/lower cost); selective periprocedural antibiotics
- [[asge-2019-barretts-screening-surveillance]] — ASGE 2019: Screening and Surveillance of Barrett's Esophagus — 6 GRADE statements; at-risk screening strata; chromoendoscopy + Seattle protocol (strong-for); against routine EUS and CLE; WATS-3D adjunct; volumetric laser endomicroscopy (VLE) insufficient
- [[asge-2018-barretts-eet]] — ASGE 2018: Endoscopic Eradication Therapy for Barrett's Esophagus with Dysplasia/IMC — 7 GRADE recommendations; EET over esophagectomy for HGD/IMC; resect all visible lesions (EMR-first) before ablation; strong against complete EMR; ablate residual flat BE; post-CE-IM surveillance
- [[asge-2026-esophageal-gastric-varices]] — ASGE 2026 Guideline: Management of Esophageal and Gastric Varices in Cirrhosis — Marya, Cosgrove, Thiruvengadam et al; ASGE Standards of Practice Committee. Gastrointest Endosc 2026 (article in press), doi:10.1016/j.gie.2026.07.026 — **the new arrival of 2026-10-16 and the first ASGE varices document since 2014.** ⚠ **Numbers none of its statements** (the wiki's R1–R7 / BPA1–BPA3 labels are the wiki's own) and grades **every one Conditional**, certainty Low or Very low throughout — no strong recommendation anywhere. Seven direct reversals of ASGE 2014, tabulated in its Table 9: Baveno VI criteria replace screening endoscopy in cACLD (sensitivity 0.97, specificity 0.32, with the full exclusion list — decompensation, prior variceal bleed, NSBB use, HCC, splenectomy, transplant, BMI ≥40); **carvedilol plus endoscopy every 6–12 mo** for cACLD + CSPH (3.125 mg BID or 6.25 mg daily, titrate to SBP >90 and MAP >65, cap 12.5 mg/d); vasoactive therapy **2–3 d, not 3–5** (5 d after sclerotherapy); **endoscopy within 24 h, not 12**; **against routine prophylactic endotracheal intubation**; esophageal stent **or** balloon tamponade as the refractory bridge (fully covered, largest diameter, ≥2/3 in esophagus, never for gastric varices); **EUS-guided cyanoacrylate + coils over direct endoscopic injection** for high-risk gastric varices (>10 mm or previously bled), EUS over RTO initially, and pre-emptive TIPS by multidisciplinary discussion afterward. Declines to adopt Baveno VII's splenic-stiffness escape
- [[asge-2024-gerd]] — ASGE 2024: Diagnosis and Management of GERD — 6 PICO questions; TIF 2.0 vs. cTIF hiatal hernia decision; PPI safety; post-sleeve BE surveillance; standardized endoscopy reporting
- [[asge-2023-indeterminate-biliary-strictures]] — ASGE 2023: Endoscopic Diagnosis of Malignancy in Indeterminate Biliary Strictures — 3 conditional/very-low-quality recommendations; fluoroscopic biopsy + brush cytology; cholangioscopy for nondistal strictures; EUS for distal/nodal/nondiagnostic; combined-modality sensitivities (brush ~0.40 → +biopsy ~0.66 → +cholangioscopy ~0.72 → +EUS ~0.88)
- [[asge-2022-pancreatic-cancer-screening]] — ASGE 2022: Screening for Pancreatic Cancer in Genetically Susceptible Individuals (GRADE) — Sawhney et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2022;95:817–826 — 5 GRADE recommendations + condition-specific starting-age table; EUS/MRI annual surveillance; all BRCA1/2 carriers screened regardless of family history; yield 3.1%, harms (low-yield surgery)
- [[asge-2020-endoscopic-removal]] — ASGE 2020: Endoscopic Removal of Colorectal Lesions — Kaltenbach et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2020;91:486–519 — same document as usmstf-2020-endoscopic-removal (multi-journal simultaneous publication). ⚠ **Rebuilt 2026-09-08 after a source-fidelity audit found 23 of 34 GRADE ratings contradicted the document.** It has **no numbered recommendations** — 43 unnumbered bullets under 6 named Statements (Table 1) — so the page's former "Recommendation 1–36" scheme was an invented editorial convention, and **5 entries attached a GRADE to ungraded narrative** (NICE 3/Kudo Vn management, non-lifting sign, underwater EMR, cold snare EMR, "endoscopy is first-line"); 7 including the previously-fixed ESD and eFTR. The source's vocabulary is **Strong/Conditional, never "Weak."** 13 genuine graded statements had been omitted and are now added. This page is the wiki's **single verbatim home** for the document's grade table. ERBE electrosurgery settings; underwater/cold snare EMR recurrence data; eFTR R0 rates; hot avulsion; Direct Observation of Polypectomy Skills (DOPyS)/Cold Snare Polypectomy Assessment Tool (CSPAT) competency tools
- [[asge-2015-scenic-ibd-dysplasia]] — SCENIC International Consensus (ASGE/AGA): Surveillance & Management of Dysplasia in IBD — Laine et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;81:489–501 — 9 GRADE recs; HD over standard-definition (SD), chromoendoscopy over white-light, NBI not substituted; visible (polypoid/nonpolypoid) vs invisible dysplasia (DALM abandoned); resect-and-survey over colectomy for resectable visible dysplasia
- [[asge-2020-acpo-volvulus]] — ASGE 2020: Endoscopy in Acute Colonic Pseudo-Obstruction & Colonic Volvulus — Naveed et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2020;91:228–235 — 10 recs; sigmoid volvulus endoscopic detorsion-first + surgical consult; cecal volvulus surgery-first; ACPO conservative ×72h → neostigmine 2 mg → endoscopic decompression; surgery for perforation/peritonitis
- [[asge-2015-deep-enteroscopy]] — ASGE 2015: Role of Deep Enteroscopy in the Management of Small-Bowel Disorders — Khashab et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;82:600–607 — 7 GRADE recs; double-balloon enteroscopy (DBE)/single-balloon enteroscopy (SBE)/spiral; DBE most effective for total enteroscopy; VCE-first then deep enteroscopy (DE) for positive findings; DE for small-bowel tumors and Crohn's lesions within reach
- [[asge-2013-crc-staging-management]] — ASGE 2013: Role of Endoscopy in the Staging and Management of Colorectal Cancer — Fisher et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2013;78:8–12 — 8 GRADE recs; EUS rectal staging (T 80–96% sens); endoscopic vs surgical malignant-polyp criteria; SEMS bridge-to-surgery (60–85% single-stage); EMR only if complete resection anticipated
- [[asge-2014-constipation]] — ASGE 2014: Role of Endoscopy in the Management of Constipation — Cash et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2014;80:563–565 — 4 GRADE recs; no endoscopy without alarm features; colonoscopy for alarm features / pre-surgery / age-appropriate screening; low yield in isolated constipation; therapeutic stricture dilation + cecostomy; no role in disimpaction
- [[asge-2010-anorectal-disorders]] — ASGE 2010: Role of Endoscopy in Patients With Anorectal Disorders — Cash et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2010;72:1117–1123 — 9 GRADE recs; APC first-line for radiation proctopathy; RBL for bleeding/prolapsing internal hemorrhoids; nitrate/CCB/botulinum then lateral internal sphincterotomy (LIS) for anal fissure; flex sig/colonoscopy + endoanal ultrasound (EAUS)/MRI for fecal incontinence (mostly superseded by ACG 2021 / ASGE 2019)
- [[asge-2015-ibd]] — ASGE 2015: Role of Endoscopy in Inflammatory Bowel Disease — Shergill et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;81:1101–1121 — 21 GRADE recs; colonoscopy + ileoscopy + multisite biopsy for dx/subtyping; backwash ileitis vs CD ileitis; capsule endoscopy (CE) algorithm for small-bowel CD (patency capsule first); EUS for perianal fistula; Montreal classification; neoterminal-ileum (Rutgeerts) postop eval 6–12 mo; chromoendoscopy-preferred dysplasia surveillance (aligns with SCENIC); pouch surveillance; endoscopic balloon dilation for benign strictures ≤4 cm
- [[asge-2010-diarrhea]] — ASGE 2010: Role of Endoscopy in the Management of Patients With Diarrhea — Shen et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2010;71:887–892 — 10 GRADE recs; stool/labs first; colonoscopy + random R+L biopsies for chronic unexplained diarrhea (microscopic colitis is patchy); terminal ileum (TI) intubation; EGD + ≥4 duodenal biopsies for celiac/malabsorption; CE/enteroscopy not routine; HIV (CD4<100, proximal-colon yield) and GVHD (distal-colon biopsy) strategies
- [[asge-2020-fap]] — ASGE 2020: Role of Endoscopy in Familial Adenomatous Polyposis Syndromes — Yang et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2020;91:963–982 — 17 GRADE recs; clinical-polyposis testing threshold (≥10 on one exam / ≥20 lifetime); colonoscopy + AFAP/MAP intervals; pouch/ileoscopy 1–2 y, IRA sigmoidoscopy 6 mo–1 y; upper-GI interval by most-affected organ; resect gastric/duodenal polyps >1 cm + all antral polyps; ampullary exam (duodenoscope/cap-assisted) + biopsy only if mucosal abnormality; Spigelman score 7 ↔ duodenal HGD; chemoprevention only in tertiary centers/trials
- [[asge-2013-biliary-neoplasia]] — ASGE 2013: Role of Endoscopy in the Evaluation and Treatment of Patients With Biliary Neoplasia — Appalaneni et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2013;77:167–174 — 7 GRADE recs; EUS if management-changing; magnetic resonance cholangiography (MRC) for resectability + unilateral drainage of unresectable hilar lesions; ERCP for tissue/indeterminate strictures; gallbladder-polyp thresholds (symptomatic or >10 mm → cholecystectomy; 6–10 mm → US q12mo; any gallbladder polyp (GBP) in PSC → cholecystectomy) (net-new GBP-polyp management vs newer ASGE 2021/2023)
- [[asge-2015-benign-pancreatic-disease]] — ASGE 2015: Role of Endoscopy in Benign Pancreatic Disease — Chandrasekhara et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;82:203–214 — 11 GRADE recs; no diagnostic ERCP for single AP episode; EUS for idiopathic AP >40 y; SOD typing (treat type 1 / manometry-confirmed type 2; do NOT scope type 3); rectal indomethacin ± PD stent for PEP prophylaxis; endoscopic retrograde pancreatography (ERP) dilation/stent for dominant PD strictures; ESWL for refractory pancreatolithiasis; ERP+stent first-line for PD leaks
- [[asge-2016-antithrombotic]] — ASGE 2016: Management of Antithrombotic Agents for Patients Undergoing GI Endoscopy — Acosta et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2016;83:3–16 — drug duration-of-action/hold-interval/reversal table; procedure bleeding-risk dichotomy; CV-risk × bleeding-risk elective matrix; urgent-bleeding management (net-new hold-interval table vs newer ACG/CAG 2022, which governs bridging)
- [[asge-2014-lab-testing-endoscopy]] — ASGE 2014: Routine Laboratory Testing Before Endoscopic Procedures — Gastrointest Endosc 2014;80:28–33 — 8 GRADE recs; selective risk-based testing; against routine coag/CXR/ECG/type-screen/CBC/UA/chem in healthy patients; abnormal PTT does not predict bleeding; pregnancy testing before fluoroscopy
- [[asge-2011-eus-mediastinal-adenopathy]] — ASGE 2011: EUS-Guided FNA in the Diagnosis of Mediastinal Adenopathy — Gastrointest Endosc 2011;74:239–245 — 4 recs; EUS reaches posterior/inferior + AP-window (#5)/para-aortic (#6), EBUS for paratracheal; combined EUS/EBUS-FNA NPV ≈ mediastinoscopy; high false-negative each alone; flow cytometry + core biopsy for lymphoma
- [[asge-2011-foreign-body-ingestion]] — ASGE 2011: Management of Ingested Foreign Bodies and Food Impactions — Ikenberry, Jue, Anderson et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2011;73:1085–1091 — timing triage (emergent/urgent/elective), size and length thresholds, object-class management (button batteries, magnets, sharps, narcotic packets), retrieval technique and airway protection
- [[esge-2016-foreign-bodies]] — ESGE 2016: Removal of Foreign Bodies in the Upper GI Tract in Adults — 13 graded recommendations; puts hour values on emergent (2 h, at latest 6 h), urgent (24 h) and nonurgent (72 h); object x location timing matrix; gastric battery moved into the 24-hour band; push technique first for food bolus; >75% have underlying esophageal pathology
- [[asge-2011-enteral-feeding]] — ASGE 2011: The Role of Endoscopy in Enteral Feeding — Gastrointest Endosc 2011;74:7–12 — 6 GRADE recs; nasoenteric if <30 d, percutaneous if longer; PEGJ/DPEJ for severe GERD/gastroparesis/aspiration; IV antibiotic prophylaxis; feed within 4 h; PEG complications/mortality (net-new procedural detail vs ASGE 2025 gastrostomy)
- [[asge-2015-gastric-premalignant]] — ASGE 2015: Endoscopy in Premalignant and Malignant Conditions of the Stomach — Evans et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2015;82:1–8 — gastric polyp histology unpredictable → biopsy/resect; GIM/pernicious-anemia surveillance; ≥7 biopsies + EUS staging for gastric cancer; carcinoid types 1–4; EUS±FNA for subepithelial lesions (older; endoscopic-technique detail vs newer ACG 2025 / AGA GIM/atrophic-gastritis/gastric-polyps)
- [[asge-2016-solid-pancreatic-neoplasia]] — ASGE 2016: Endoscopy in Diagnosis and Management of Solid Pancreatic Neoplasia — Eloubeidi et al. Gastrointest Endosc 2016;83:17–28 — EUS + pancreas-protocol CT; EUS-FNA/FNB tissue acquisition; limited preoperative ERCP; endoscopic palliation (biliary/gastroduodenal stents, celiac plexus neurolysis, fiducials); EUS+MRCP high-risk screening (older; superseded on masses by ASGE 2024)
- [[usmstf-2015-crc-surveillance]] — USMSTF 2015: Colonoscopy Surveillance after Colorectal Cancer Resection — Kahi et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2016;111:337–346 — 6 unnumbered statements (5 graded); perioperative clearing; colonoscopy at 1 y, then 3 y, then 5 y; rectal cancer sigmoidoscopy/EUS
- [[usmstf-2016-fit-screening]] — USMSTF 2016: Fecal Immunochemical Testing to Screen for Colorectal Neoplasia — Robertson et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2017;112:37–53 — 14 graded statements (Table 7 + text); CRC sensitivity ~79%/specificity ~94% (1-time); advanced adenoma 20–30%; ≤20 µg/g cut-off; repeat testing; program quality targets

### EASL

- [[baveno-viii-2026-portal-hypertension]] — Baveno VIII: Advancing Consensus in Portal Hypertension — Baveno VIII Faculty (Berzigotti, Mandorfer, Pons, Abraldes … de Franchis). J Hepatol 2026 (accepted pre-proof), doi:10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 — EASL/ESGE/European Reference Network (ERN) RARE-LIVER-endorsed consensus, **272 statements across 9 panels**, Oxford level of evidence (LoE) + strength of recommendation (SoR) + change-status + % agreement. **Supersedes Baveno VII.** NIT-first: CSPH now *diagnosed* by ANTICIPATE/ANTICIPATE-NASH or NICER ≥75%, LSM ≥25 kPa, or SSM (100 Hz) >55 kPa; rule-out LSM ≤15 + plt ≥150; endoscopy spared unless LSM ≥20 **and** plt <150 **and** SSM ≥40; repeat EGD 2 y (active aetiology) / 3 y (cured). Recompensation redefined (**CTP A5/A6, >6 months**, TIPS patients eligible, NSBB stops only on *confirmed* CSPH resolution). **Statins and anticoagulation no longer recommended to prevent decompensation**; long-term primary antibiotic prophylaxis withdrawn. p-TIPS = CTP C 10–13 / CTP B >7 + active bleed / HVPG ≥20, with a 1–2 week salvage window; **the CTP ≥14 / MELD >30 + lactate >12 futility rule is deleted**. PSVD **or NCPF** dual nomenclature + point-scoring diagnosis + SSM <40 & bilirubin <1 endoscopy escape; Vascular Liver Disease Interest Group (VALDIG) remnant-lumen PVT criteria; first paediatric statements
