- [[baveno-vii-2022-portal-hypertension]] — **⚠ Superseded by Baveno VIII (2026) — retained for the change history.** Baveno VII: Renewing Consensus in Portal Hypertension — de Franchis, Bosch, Garcia-Tsao, Reiberger, Ripoll. J Hepatol 2022;76:959–974 — EASL-endorsed consensus (9 topics, GRADE-graded statements); CSPH = HVPG ≥10 mmHg; LSM "rule of 5" (10-15-20-25 kPa) + Baveno VI criteria (LSM <20 + plt >150) + spleen-stiffness (SSM <21/>50) rule-in/out; carvedilol preferred to prevent 1st decompensation in CSPH (obviates screening endoscopy); pre-emptive TIPS (Child-Pugh class C (CP-C) <14 / Child-Pugh class B (CP-B) >7 active bleed / HVPG >20); recompensation criteria; PSVD framework (Table 2); splanchnic vein thrombosis (BCS-TIPS PI >7, PVT nomenclature)

### NCCN

- [[nccn-2026-hcc]] — NCCN Guidelines (v1.2026): Hepatocellular Carcinoma — Benson, D'Angelica et al. (first-line systemic tiering: ipilimumab+nivolumab elevated to Preferred category 1 per CheckMate-9DW, atezo+bev, durvalumab+tremelimumab; strengthened EBRT/SBRT; content summarized, not reproduced, per NCCN license)
- [[nccn-2026-biliary-tract-cancers]] — NCCN Guidelines (v1.2026): Biliary Tract Cancers — Benson, D'Angelica et al. (gallbladder/iCCA/extrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma (eCCA) pathways; RNA-based molecular profiling FGFR2/IDH1/HER2/BRAF/NTRK/RET/NRG1/MSI-H; gem+cis+checkpoint 1st-line; adjuvant capecitabine; iCCA transplant exception; summarized per NCCN license)
- [[nccn-2026-esophageal-egj-cancer]] — NCCN Guidelines (v3.2026): Esophageal & EGJ Cancers (adenocarcinoma vs SCC; endoscopic resection T1a; perioperative FLOT±durvalumab/MATTERHORN; definitive chemoRT; HER2/PD-L1 CPS/MSI-H biomarker therapy; summarized per NCCN license)
- [[nccn-2026-gastric-cancer]] — NCCN Guidelines (v3.2026): Gastric Cancer (perioperative FLOT + D2 gastrectomy; MSI-H/dMMR dostarlimab; metastatic HER2→trastuzumab/T-DXd cat 1, PD-L1 CPS→nivo/pembro, claudin 18.2→zolbetuximab; summarized per NCCN license). **Both NCCN rating vocabularies now on the page** (categories of evidence 1/2A/2B/3 with their consensus thresholds, and categories of preference Preferred / Other recommended / Useful in certain circumstances) with the warning that they are independent and that NCCN numbers no recommendations; plus NCCN's EUS caveat (not reliable for superficial vs deep submucosal invasion — resection is the staging manoeuvre) and its incomplete-endoscopic-resection rule
- [[nccn-2026-colon-cancer]] — NCCN Guidelines (v2.2026): Colon Cancer (universal MMR/MSI; RAS/BRAF/HER2/NTRK/DPYD; adjuvant FOLFOX/CAPOX with IDEA duration; metastatic biomarker- & sidedness-directed therapy — anti-epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) left-sided RAS/BRAF-WT, encorafenib+cetuximab for BRAF V600E, pembrolizumab for MSI-H; summarized per NCCN license)
- [[nccn-2026-gist]] — NCCN Guidelines (v1.2026): GIST (KIT/PDGFRA/SDH mutational testing; risk by size/mitoses/site/rupture; surgery + neoadjuvant/adjuvant imatinib; TKI sequence imatinib→sunitinib→regorafenib→ripretinib, avapritinib for PDGFRA D842V; summarized per NCCN license)
- [[nccn-2026-net]] — NCCN Guidelines (v1.2026): Neuroendocrine and Adrenal Tumors — Bergsland, Rose et al. (GI/pancreatic NET pathways; **both NCCN rating schemes with their consensus thresholds — categories of evidence 1/2A/2B/3 and categories of preference — and why they are independent**; WHO grading; the **Principles of Endoscopic Treatment (NE-C)** site/size/depth thresholds; gNET subtyping incl. the PPI-associated branch; SSTR-PET; somatostatin analog (SSA)/PRRT/everolimus/sunitinib; content summarized, not reproduced, per NCCN license)

### Other

- [[stapfer-2000-duodenal-perforation-ercp]] — Stapfer M, Selby RR, Stain SC, et al. *Management of Duodenal Perforation After ERCP and Sphincterotomy.* Ann Surg 2000;232:191–198 — **the derivation paper for the Stapfer classification**, ingested 2026-10-20 to close the types-III/IV gap that [[aga-2021-gi-perforations-endoscopic-management|AGA 2021]] leaves open (it discusses types 1–2 in detail, prints neither 3 nor 4, and never cites Stapfer by name). Supplies all **four types** keyed to mechanism and location, the **five surgical indications** (Table 4), the **1-minute contrast definitions** (large = contrast persists past 1 min; minimal = gone within 60 s), and the 8 h / 48 h double-contrast CT surveillance schedule. Also the explicit non-criteria: **fever and leukocytosis alone do not justify surgery**, and a **benign abdomen does not exclude it**. ⚠ **Tier-2, very low evidence quality** — retrospective single-center series of **14 patients** (1.0% of 1,413 ERCPs, 1993–1998) that predates every modern closure device; AGA 2021 is newer and higher-priority and governs management. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[baraclude-2019-entecavir-label]] — BARACLUDE (entecavir) tablets and oral solution — FDA prescribing information, rev. 11/2019 (DailyMed) — **the wiki's first drug label**, fetched 2026-10-20 by the daily source fetch for one declared gap: [[aasld-idsa-2025-chb-treatment|AASLD/IDSA 2025]] says entecavir needs renal dose adjustment but prints **no milligram dose and no cut-point**. Supplies **0.5 mg daily** naive, **1 mg daily** for lamivudine/telbivudine resistance **or** decompensated liver disease, the **four-band creatinine-clearance table on both dose tracks** (adjust <50 mL/min; oral solution required below 0.5 mg; dose after hemodialysis), the empty-stomach rule (≥2 h either side of a meal), **no hepatic adjustment**, no contraindications, and the **three-part boxed warning** (post-discontinuation flare; HIV NRTI resistance if HIV untreated → test everyone before starting; lactic acidosis with steatosis). ⚠ A label is **not a guideline** — authoritative for dose and labelled safety only, and outside the three-tier priority scheme. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[acg-asge-2026-ercp-quality-indicators]] — Anderson MA, Cote GA, Keswani RN, et al. *Quality Indicators for ERCP.* Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121:80–95 — **the canonical US ERCP quality-indicator set**, ACG/ASGE Task Force on Quality in Endoscopy, third iteration after 2006 and 2015. **13 numbered indicators**, each with a strength grade, measure type and target; **5 are priority indicators**. Four changes the task force names as the important ones: a **more restrictive approach to ERCP indications**; **rectal indomethacin or diclofenac endorsed in *unselected* patients** with an intact papilla (1A, >90%), no longer high-risk only; a **new indicator tracking use or nonuse of a prophylactic pancreatic stent** in high-risk cases (1A, >98% documented); and **unplanned events** — hospital visit and biliary reintervention within 30 days, both <15% — promoted to the postprocedure outcome measures. ⚠ **The stone-extraction denominator widened** from stones <1 cm to all extrahepatic duct stones, so an old rate is not comparable to >90%. ⚠ **Indicators 11–13 are process measures** — they score documentation, and the document deliberately sets **no performance target for the PEP rate itself**. Also carries the EPISOD 5-year result and the reclassification of sphincter of Oddi type III as a disorder of gut–brain interaction, the **negative DDW 2025 sham-controlled RCT of minor papilla sphincterotomy** in pancreas divisum, the three supported antibiotic-prophylaxis scenarios, and the measured performance of the ASGE 2010 vs 2019 choledocholithiasis criteria. Grades are the task force's own 8-level scheme (1A/1B/1C+/1C/2A/2B/2C/3), **not GRADE**. Its **Table 2** reprints the 19 indicators common to all endoscopic procedures, which belong to the separate Elmunzer companion document. **Cards: eligible** (tier-1 joint-society guideline)
- [[asge-acg-2024-common-endoscopy-quality-indicators]] — Elmunzer BJ, Anderson MA, Mishra G, et al. *Quality Indicators Common to All GI Endoscopic Procedures.* Gastrointest Endosc 2024;100:382–94 (identical in Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121:1781–91) — the **base layer** of the ASGE/ACG quality series, third iteration after 2006 and 2015, on which the colonoscopy, ERCP, upper-endoscopy and EUS documents stand. **19 indicators**, **4 priority** (#1 indication, #5 antibiotics, #6 antithrombotic plan, #17 adverse-event documentation). ⚠ **Read the grading differently from its companions:** 15 of 19 are graded **3 = expert opinion** at a **>98%** target, because the task force treats failure to perform them as a **never event** — variation in practice was deliberately *not* the basis for inclusion. Clinical substance sits in the discussions: ASA vs Mallampati (**Mallampati is not validated for endoscopy**), the rescue-one-level-deeper rule, monitoring at ≤5-min intervals, the full ASA fasting set, aspirin 81–325 mg continued, the antibiotic *not recommended* list, the adverse-event definition with certainty/severity grading and the per-procedure perforation and bleeding rates, and the 20 minimal report elements. ⚠ **Three internal inconsistencies left as published** (indicator 1 graded 1C+ in the table / 1C in text; indicator 6 target >95% / 95%; indicator 19 outcome / process). ⚠ **Sets no nothing-by-mouth rule for GLP-1 receptor agonists** — it explicitly defers. **Cards: eligible** (tier-1 joint-society guideline)
- [[asge-acg-2024-colonoscopy-quality-indicators]] — Rex DK, Anderson JC, Butterly LF, et al. *Quality Indicators for Colonoscopy.* Gastrointest Endosc 2024;100:352–81 (identical in Am J Gastroenterol) — **the canonical US colonoscopy quality-indicator set**, ASGE/ACG Task Force on Quality in Endoscopy, superseding its own 2006 and 2015 editions. **15 numbered indicators**, each with a strength grade, measure type and one minimum target; **5 are priority indicators** whose measurement is "essential" (ADR, SSLDR, surveillance-interval adherence, bowel-prep adequacy, cecal intubation — the last two new to that list). **Three benchmarks rose: ADR ≥30%→≥35%, withdrawal ≥6→≥8 min, cecal intubation ≥90%→≥95%**; new: **adenomas per colonoscopy ≥0.6**, **sessile serrated lesion detection rate ≥6%**, **cold snare for 4–9 mm lesions ≥90%** (grade 1A, the document's only one), lesion-documentation ≥98%, and **three IBD indicators** requiring a formal endoscopic activity score. ⚠ **The ADR denominator also widened** — screening **+ surveillance + diagnostic** at age **≥45**, with 4 exclusions — so an ADR computed the old way is not comparable to ≥35%. Also Table 13, the UC dysplasia-surveillance interval grid reconciling four guidelines (now on [[ulcerative-colitis]]). Grades are the task force's own 8-level scheme (1A/1B/1C+/1C/2A/2B/2C/3), **not GRADE**; the document states the targets are **not** standard of care, credentialing or training standards. **Cards: eligible** (tier-1 joint-society guideline) — not yet written
- [[viread-2018-tenofovir-df-label]] — VIREAD (tenofovir disoproxil fumarate) tablets — FDA prescribing information, rev. 7/2018 (DailyMed) — fetched 2026-10-24 to close the TDF third of the same declared gap as the entecavir label. Supplies **300 mg once daily, with or without food**, and the renal rule that **extends the interval instead of cutting the dose**: adjust below CrCl 50 mL/min — q48h at 30–49, q72–96h at 10–29, **q7 days on hemodialysis** (give after the session), and **no recommendation at all below 10 mL/min off dialysis**. ⚠ The label states those intervals are **modelled from single-dose pharmacokinetics in people without HBV or HIV and were never clinically evaluated** — the labelled reason the guideline prefers changing the agent to dose-adjusting TDF. Also the renal/bone monitoring AASLD/IDSA 2025 omits (creatinine + estimated CrCl in everyone; phosphorus, urine glucose and urine protein in at-risk patients; **consider BMD if prior pathologic fracture or osteoporosis risk**), the rule that **bone or muscle pain on TDF should trigger a renal evaluation** (proximal renal tubulopathy), the NSAID avoidance rule, and the posttreatment-flare boxed warning. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[vemlidy-2024-tenofovir-alafenamide-label]] — VEMLIDY (tenofovir alafenamide) tablets — FDA prescribing information, rev. 3/2024 (DailyMed) — fetched 2026-10-24, completing all three preferred HBV agents. Supplies **25 mg once daily with food** and the flat renal profile that justifies choosing TAF in kidney disease: **no dose adjustment at CrCl ≥15 mL/min and none on chronic hemodialysis** (dose after the session), the single prohibition being **ESRD not on hemodialysis**. Also the pre-treatment and on-treatment renal laboratory set (creatinine, estimated CrCl, urine glucose, urine protein; phosphorus in CKD), **no bone-mineral-density warning at all** (the labelled counterpart of preferring TAF in bone disease), and the posttreatment-flare boxed warning. ⚠ **Contradiction surfaced:** the label indicates TAF for **compensated** liver disease only and does **not recommend it in Child-Pugh B or C**, while [[aasld-idsa-2025-chb-treatment|AASLD/IDSA 2025]] lists TAF among the three agents for **decompensated** cirrhosis. The guideline governs the page; the restriction is stated beside it. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[asge-2014-dysphagia]] — Pasha SF, Acosta RD, Chandrasekhara V, et al.; ASGE Standards of Practice Committee. *The Role of Endoscopy in the Evaluation and Management of Dysphagia.* Gastrointest Endosc 2014;79:191–201 — **the US society document on dysphagia and on esophageal dilation, and still the current one** (no newer American guideline on either exists). **8 graded recommendations**, all about dilation, plus a practical manual the wiki had no anchor for. Supplies the two definition sets printed nowhere else in the corpus: **simple** stricture (short, symmetric/concentric, **≥12 mm**, endoscope-traversable) vs **complex** (**>2 cm, angulated/irregular, <12 mm** — higher recurrence and harm), and **refractory** (**cannot reach 14 mm over 5 sessions at 2-week intervals**) vs **recurrent** (**cannot hold 14 mm for 4 weeks**), with both excluding inflammatory strictures and neuromuscular dysphagia. Also the diet-by-diameter scale (**regular 18 mm · modified 15 mm · dysphagia ≤13 mm**), the three dilator families and their mechanics (⚠ **a larger balloon needs less pressure and carries higher perforation risk**), **triamcinolone 40 mg/mL, 0.2–0.5 mL into 4 quadrants** with its effect sizes, the benign-stent data (**plastic 52% clinical success, 24% migration; fully covered metal 80% migration; biodegradable no success after a third stent**), self-bougienage, and the real adverse-event rates (**perforation 0.1%–0.4%**, bacteremia 12%–22% with endocarditis extremely rare). ⚠ **The "rule of 3" is stated and disclaimed in the same paragraph** — "accepted but not formally studied for its safety," and it **does not apply to balloon dilation**. ⚠ **Empiric large-bore (50F) dilation of a normal-looking esophagus repeatedly failed to improve dysphagia scores** and may not be worth the perforation risk. **Superseded on achalasia** by [[asge-2020-achalasia]]/[[acg-2020-achalasia]] and **on EoE dilation** by [[acg-2025-eoe]]; retained for everything else. **Cards: eligible** (tier-1 society guideline) — not yet written
- [[sages-2013-hiatal-hernia]] — Kohn GP, Price RR, DeMeester SR, et al.; SAGES Guidelines Committee. *Guidelines for the Management of Hiatal Hernia.* Surg Endosc 2013;27:4409–28 — **the broad SAGES hiatal-hernia document**, 28 graded statements, and the one [[sages-2024-hiatal-hernia-surgery|SAGES 2024]] explicitly **complements rather than replaces**: 2024 asks four focused questions, 2013 covers everything else. Supplies the indication split (**type I in the absence of reflux disease needs no repair** — GERD is the indication, ⊕⊕⊕ strong; **all symptomatic paraesophageal hernias are repaired**, ⊕⊕⊕⊕ strong), the natural-history numbers behind watchful waiting (**≈14%/y asymptomatic→symptomatic; <2%/y needing emergency surgery; emergency-repair mortality 0–5.4%, average ~17%**; and the modelling result that elective repair of a completely asymptomatic PEH **may reduce quality-adjusted life expectancy at age ≥65**), the PEH symptom profile (**heartburn uncommon; postprandial fullness, dyspnea, pain relieved by vomiting, iron deficiency anemia up to 50%**), gastric volvulus with the **Borchardt triad**, and the full operative sequence (laparoscopy preferred; sac dissected and excised; GEJ below the diaphragm; **≥2–3 cm intra-abdominal esophagus**; fundoplication mandatory in type I repair; Collis gastroplasty; gastropexy; revisional surgery; postoperative course). ⚠ **Internal split on mesh carried as published:** statement 11 recommends mesh for **short-term** recurrence (⊕⊕⊕, strong) while statement 12 — **the only ungraded statement in the document** — says the long-term data support no recommendation either way; the three randomized trials ran **22%→0%**, **26%→8%**, and **59%→54% (p=0.7) at 4.8 y with both arms over 50%**. SAGES 2024 then declined to recommend for or against, so **the current society position is no recommendation**. Also the only pediatric hiatal-hernia content in the corpus (statements 23–28). Grades are GRADE in the document's own ⊕ symbols. **Cards: eligible** (tier-1 society guideline) — not yet written
- [[gattex-2025-teduglutide-label]] — GATTEX (teduglutide) for injection — FDA prescribing information, rev. 9/2025 (DailyMed) — fetched 2026-10-25 by the daily source fetch to close the gap [[glp-2-agonists]] had declared on its own front matter: the AGA clinical practice updates place GLP-2 analogues in the short-bowel-syndrome ladder and give **no dose, no monitoring schedule and no adverse-effect detail**. Supplies **0.05 mg/kg once daily subcutaneously** (adults and children ≥1 y alike), the single adjustment — **halve to 0.025 mg/kg at eGFR <60 mL/min/1.73 m²** — **no hepatic adjustment at Child-Pugh A/B and no data at C**, **no contraindications**, and the preparation ceiling (**max 0.38 mL = 3.8 mg per 5 mg vial**, so a patient over ~76 kg needs two). Its distinctive content is the **endoscopic surveillance program a gastroenterologist is asked to deliver and that no ingested society document specifies**: colonoscopy **and** upper endoscopy with polypectomy within **6 mo before starting**, repeat **at 1 year**, then **≥ every 5 y** if no polyp; labs (bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, lipase, amylase) **every 6 mo**; **GI cancer on therapy → stop**. Plus the two interrupting adverse events — **obstruction 4% at the recommended dose vs 9% at twice it vs 0% placebo**, and **fluid overload / CHF**, managed by adjusting parenteral support — the **42% vs 14% stoma-complication rate**, the increased absorption of concomitant oral drugs (prazepam→coma case), and **anti-drug antibodies reaching 48% by month 30 with no loss of efficacy**. ⚠ A label is **not a guideline** — authoritative for dose and labelled safety only, outside the three-tier priority scheme. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[caps-2020-pancreatic-surveillance]] — **CAPS 2020** international consensus: Management of Patients with Increased Risk for Familial Pancreatic Cancer — Goggins, Overbeek, Brand et al. *Gut* 2020;69:7–17 — **the document [[pancreatic-cancer-screening]] had been naming by author for months in two standing gap notes**, both now closed. 76 experts, 11 countries, two Delphi rounds, consensus at **≥75%**; **55 statements** reached it, each with an agreement % and an action grade (**1** definitely do it · **2** probably do it · **3** no recommendation · **4** probably don't · **5** definitely don't). Supplies the **lesion strata behind AGA 2020's interval ladder**, which AGA names but never defines (12 mo for nothing, a cyst without worrisome features, pancreatic lobulation, or a **<1 cm** non-functioning NET · **3–6 mo** for a CDKN2A carrier with mild duct dilation or a stricture without a mass · **3 mo** for a solid lesion or duct stricture not going to surgery, both grade 1), and the **operative thresholds no other ingested source gives**: resect a solid lesion at **≥10 mm** or **≥5 mm** (grade 1 each) — ⚠ *regardless of size* was **voted down, grade 4** — and resect an **IPMN in a high-risk individual** for any of **six grade-1 features** incl. **main duct ≥10 mm**. Also the test-selection rules: **CT graded 4 and abdominal ultrasound graded 5 for routine surveillance** (CT only *after* a solid lesion or a mass-free duct stricture), **CA 19-9 only when imaging is worrisome**, **routine fasting glucose/HbA1c**, and **EUS-FNA for solid lesions ≥5 mm, worrisome cysts and duct strictures**. ⚠ **Older than [[asge-2022-pancreatic-cancer-screening]], which governs the overlaps** — CAPS **requires** a family history for BRCA2/PALB2 (ASGE does not) and puts **Peutz-Jeghers at 40** (ASGE 35); ⚠ **BRCA1's family-history criterion reached only 69.6% → graded 3, no recommendation**; ⚠ **no consensus on when to stop**, nor on **whether or how to alternate MRI and EUS**. ⚠ **The PDF prints only statements 1–28 plus Table 1** — the full 55 are in online supplementary table S2, not in `raw/`
- [[ascrs-2017-rectal-prolapse]] — **ASCRS 2017**: Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Treatment of Rectal Prolapse — Bordeianou, Paquette, Johnson et al. *Dis Colon Rectum* 2017;60:1121–1131 — the **only operative guideline on prolapse in the corpus**, and the one that closes [[rectal-prolapse]]'s standing gap on **non-operative management**. **16 recommendations, numbered by the document within each section** (GRADE 1A–2C). Headline: **prolapse cannot be corrected nonoperatively** (2C) — medicine palliates only (fiber, stool softeners, **topical table sugar to reduce edema and reduce an incarcerated prolapse**, skin care) — and ⚠ **delay has a price**: medical management alone leads to **irreversible fecal incontinence**, and **>4 years untreated** means higher recurrence after eventual repair, elderly patients included. Two selection rules: **prolapse + FI → fix the prolapse first** (and lateral-stalk division, which lowers recurrence at the cost of constipation, is *desirable* there); **coexisting constipation or dyssynergia disqualifies** posterior mobilization, lateral-ligament transection and levatorplasty. One hard rule: **posterior mobilization without rectopexy is not recommended (1B** — 8-fold recurrence in an RCT**)**; **rectopexy is a key component (1A)**. Supplies the recurrence table (suture rectopexy 3%–9% at 2 y but **29% at 10 y** · resection rectopexy 2%–5% · ventral mesh **3.4%, 8.2% at 10 y** · Delorme 10%–15% · Altemeier **16%–30% at 2 y**, **levatorplasty cuts it 21% → 7%**) and the corpus's only length threshold, **Delorme for a short (<5 cm) prolapse**. ⚠ **Declines to rank posterior vs anterior repair (1C)**; ⚠ names its own geographic split — ventral mesh rectopexy is preferred by many in Europe but **"has yet to gain full acceptance in the United States"**. Older than [[acg-2021-anorectal-disorders]], which governs the overlap; ASCRS adds below that level and contradicts nothing
- [[ascrs-2023-anal-fissures]] — **ASCRS 2023**: Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Management of Anal Fissures — Davids, Hawkins, Bhama et al. *Dis Colon Rectum* 2023;66:190–199 — the **only colorectal-surgery guideline on fissure in the corpus**, and the one that supplies the surgical half of [[anal-fissure]]. **11 recommendations, numbered by the document itself**, GRADE 1A–2C. Its headline divergence from [[acg-2021-anorectal-disorders]]: nitrates, calcium channel blockers and botulinum toxin are **each first-line (1B)** rather than a sequence, and **lateral internal sphincterotomy (LIS) may be offered first-line in pharmacologically naive patients (1A)** — which ACG does not offer at all — with four exclusions (prior obstetric injury, IBD, previous anorectal surgery, documented sphincter injury). Net-new technique content: **open vs closed LIS equivalent (1A)**; **tailored LIS to the fissure apex** heals as well with less fecal incontinence, anchored by **Wexner 1 when <25% of sphincter length is divided vs 3 at ≥25%** and a 287-patient series at **1.4% flatus incontinence, no stool incontinence**; **repeat contralateral LIS (98% healing) or botulinum toxin (74%) for post-LIS recurrence (2C)**; and the **anocutaneous V-Y / house flap** as the sphincter-preserving alternative (2B) or add-on (2C). Also supersedes the page's location figures (**73% posterior · 13% of women / 8% of men anterior · 2.6% both**) and the acute cut-point (**<6 weeks**). ⚠ **The document contradicts itself on Rec 8's grade — 1A in its "what's new" table, 1B in the body**; both printed, neither chosen
- [[linzess-2026-linaclotide-label]] — LINZESS (linaclotide) capsules — FDA prescribing information, rev. 10/2026 (DailyMed) — fetched 2026-10-26 by the daily source fetch for the gap its download-list row named: the guidelines behind [[linaclotide]] give the adult doses but no contraindications, no age limits and no organ-impairment adjustment, and [[aga-acg-2023-constipation]] states outright that it does not apply to children. Supplies **exactly two contraindications** (**age <2 y**, boxed-warning dehydration risk; **known or suspected mechanical GI obstruction**), **no renal and no hepatic adjustment** (intraluminal metabolism, plasma below the limit of quantitation at every dose, so AUC/Cmax/t½ cannot be calculated), the **four dose-by-population rows** (adult IBS-C 290 µg · adult CIC 145 µg, or 72 µg by presentation/tolerability · **pediatric ≥7 y IBS-C 145 µg** · **pediatric ≥2 y functional constipation 72 µg**), the capsule-opening and nasogastric/gastrostomy-tube instructions with the **beads-don't-dissolve** point, and the **dose-stratified severe-diarrhea rate (2% at 145/290 µg vs <1% at 72 µg**, most cases within the first 2 weeks) with the action rule **suspend and rehydrate**. ⚠ **Corrects a wrong statement on [[linaclotide]]**, which implied a blanket pediatric contraindication. ⚠ A label is **not a guideline** — authoritative for dose and labelled safety only, outside the three-tier priority scheme. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[ibsrela-2025-tenapanor-label]] — IBSRELA (tenapanor) tablets — FDA prescribing information, rev. 5/2025 (DailyMed) — fetched 2026-10-27 by the daily source fetch for the gap [[tenapanor]] had declared in its own text: the three guidelines behind that page give the adult dose and nothing else. Supplies **two contraindications** (**age <6 y**, boxed-warning dehydration risk from juvenile-rat deaths; **known or suspected mechanical GI obstruction**), the **three-band pediatric rule** that is easy to conflate — contraindicated <6 y, **avoid** 6 to <12 y, unestablished <18 y, and **no pediatric indication at any age** (unlike [[linaclotide]], indicated down to 2 y) — **no renal and no hepatic adjustment**, the label carrying no such subsection at all, and the **OATP2B1 interaction** with its worked example (**enalapril** peak exposure −70%, total −50–65% → monitor blood pressure, increase the enalapril dose). Also the meal-timing rule (**immediately before breakfast and before dinner**), the missed-dose rule, and the **renal-impairment diarrhea signal** — 20% vs 13% with normal renal function, not tracking with severity. ⚠ A label is **not a guideline** — authoritative for dose and labelled safety only, outside the three-tier priority scheme. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[narula-2022-mm-ses-cd-categories]] — Narula N, Wong ECL, Dulai PS, Marshall JK, Jairath V, Reinisch W. *Categorising Endoscopic Severity of Crohn's Disease Using the Modified Multiplier SES-CD [MM-SES-CD].* J Crohns Colitis 2022;16:1011–1019 — post-hoc pooled analysis of UNITI/IM-UNITI, EXTEND and CT-P13 (n=350, centrally read). Supplies the **per-segment multiplier table** ([[ibd-endoscopic-scoring]] had none) — **ileum affected surface ×5 and strictures ×4**, **rectum affected surface ×0.5**, plus **segments-with-ulceration × 4** — and the **four severity bands** (**<14 very mild · 14–30 mild · 31–44 moderate · ≥45 severe**) with their 1-year endoscopic-remission rates (**50.0 / 30.3 / 21.7 / 8.8%**, p<0.001). **31 is this paper's contribution** (Youden 30.5 for ER, 32 for CR). Separate **isolated-ileal** cut-offs (**<14 / 14–32 / ≥33**). ⚠ **Clinical remission does NOT separate by band** (ns; only time-to-CR, log-rank p=0.0492), and the **<22.5 progression threshold already on the page is a different endpoint — do not merge**. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[borhofen-2016-rfh-npt-cirrhosis]] — Borhofen SM, Gerner C, Lehmann J, et al. *The Royal Free Hospital-Nutritional Prioritizing Tool Is an Independent Predictor of Deterioration of Liver Function and Survival in Cirrhosis.* Dig Dis Sci 2016;61:1735–1743 — single-centre cohort (n=84, re-evaluated at median 500 d; validation n=64). Supplies the **RFH-NPT risk bands by points — low 0 · moderate 1 · high 2–7** — the **three-step order** (**acute alcoholic hepatitis or tube feeding = high risk outright**, then ascites/oedema, then compute), and the **NRS** comparator bands (**0 none · 1–2 low · 3–7 medium-to-high**, +1 for age >70 y; **not validated in cirrhosis**). RFH-NPT an **independent predictor of clinical deterioration and transplant-free survival**; **improvement within 500 days associated with improved survival**. ⚠ **It does NOT contain the item-level algorithm** — it applies the tool "as previously described" and cites Supplementary Figure 1 of the **Amodio 2013 ISHEN consensus**, which is now the `needed-sources.md` row in its place. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[gralnek-2008-lewis-score]] — Gralnek IM, de Franchis R, Seidman E, Leighton JA, Legnani P, Lewis BS. *Development of a Capsule Endoscopy Scoring Index for Small Bowel Mucosal Inflammatory Change.* Aliment Pharmacol Ther 2008;27:146–154 — **the Lewis score derivation paper**, which [[acg-2025-crohns]] names structurally without printing. Supplies **Table 1 in full** (villous appearance and ulcer scored per tertile by number × extent × descriptor; stenosis once for the whole study), the **formula — worst tertile + stenosis, never the sum of tertiles** — the **bands (<135 normal · 135–790 mild · ≥790 moderate-to-severe)**, and the definitions that make it reproducible (**oedema = villous width ≥ height**, ulcer = white/yellow base with red/pink collar, size measured **including the collar**, **few = 2–7, multiple = ≥8**, extent **short ≤10% / long 11–50% / whole >50%** of a tertile). ⚠ **Bands overlap** in the derivation data (no disease 0–271, mild 204–4050, moderate/severe 331–13,019) and reader κ is only **0.48–0.66**; **erythema was dropped from the index at κ 0.02**. Optimised against expert *perceived* severity, **no outcome validation**. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[catalano-2009-rosemont-classification]] — Catalano MF, Sahai A, Levy M, et al. *EUS-Based Criteria for the Diagnosis of Chronic Pancreatitis: The Rosemont Classification.* Gastrointest Endosc 2009;69:1251–1261 — **international consensus conference (32 endosonographers, >2/3 agreement), endorsed by ASGE** — a consensus statement, so **tier 1**, though it carries no GRADE and no numbered recommendations. The primary Rosemont document, which [[apa-2014-chronic-pancreatitis]] adopts but reproduces only in part. Supplies the **operational definitions** APA omits (foci **≥2 mm** and **≥3 needed**; lobularity **≥5 mm**, **≥3 lobules**, contiguous = honeycombing; stranding **≥3 mm in ≥2 directions, ≥3 strands**; cysts **≥2 mm short axis**; side branches **≥3, each ≥1 mm**; hyperechoic margin **>50% of MPD**), the **location rule** (only foci-with-shadowing, cysts and calculi may be read outside the body and tail) and **Table 4 in full** — the **suggestive / indeterminate / normal** splits APA collapses into "all other combinations", including the footnote that **"normal" excludes cysts, dilated MPD, non-shadowing foci and dilated side branches** from its ≤2 count. ⚠ **Settles the APA 2014 lobularity error** — the only Major A features are hyperechoic foci with shadowing and MPD calculi. ⚠ Unvalidated by the panel's own statement; derived on **radial** EUS
- [[alvarez-1999-iaihg-revised-criteria]] — Alvarez F, Berg PA, Bianchi FB, et al. *International Autoimmune Hepatitis Group Report: Review of Criteria for Diagnosis of Autoimmune Hepatitis.* J Hepatol 1999;31:929–938 — **the revised original (1999) IAIHG criteria**, the fallback [[aasld-2020-autoimmune-hepatitis]] routes complex cases to without printing them. Supplies **Table 5** (full point values, incl. **AMA −4, drug history −4, biliary histology −3, no interface hepatitis −5**), the **bands** (**pre-treatment definite >15 / probable 10–15; post-treatment >17 / 12–17**), **Table 4** descriptive criteria, **Table 7** response/relapse definitions, and the scoring notes that change the arithmetic — **ALP:AST is a ratio of *elevations above ULN***, optional HLA/other-antibody points **only if ANA/SMA/LKM seronegative**. The revision re-weighted against PBC/PSC and drug injury: specificity for excluding AIH in PSC rose **64.9% → 89.5%**. ⚠ **The panel disclaims the use AASLD puts it to** — research instrument, validated **only at initial presentation**, explicitly **not** for post-transplant recurrence or long-standing disease, values "to some extent arbitrary". **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[hennes-2008-simplified-aih-criteria]] — Hennes EM, Zeniya M, Czaja AJ, et al., and the International Autoimmune Hepatitis Group (IAIHG). *Simplified Criteria for the Diagnosis of Autoimmune Hepatitis.* Hepatology 2008;48:169–176 — **the derivation paper for the simplified AIH score**, which [[aasld-2020-autoimmune-hepatitis]] tells clinicians to use but prints only in a supporting table. Supplies **Table 2 in full**: ANA/SMA ≥1:40 = 1, ≥1:80 or anti-LKM ≥1:40 or anti-SLA positive = 2 (**autoantibody block capped at 2**), IgG >ULN = 1 / **>1.10× ULN** = 2, histology compatible = 1 / typical = 2, absence of viral hepatitis = 2 → **≥6 probable, ≥7 definite** (max 8). Also the three histology categories (**typical requires all of interface hepatitis + emperipolesis + rosettes**) and the assay caveats that change the score — **halve a HEp-2 ANA titre**, ELISA not standardised, **do not score anti-LKM in HCV**. 11 centres/10 countries, 250+193 training, 109+284 validation; AUC 0.946/0.91. ⚠ **Tier 2** — its own numbers (88%/97%) differ from the 95%/90% AASLD prints from later studies; the guidance keeps the headline. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[de-martin-2021-surfasa-acute-severe-aih]] — De Martin E, Coilly A, Chazouillères O, et al.; FILFOIE consortium. *Early Liver Transplantation for Corticosteroid Non-Responders with Acute Severe Autoimmune Hepatitis: The SURFASA Score.* J Hepatol 2021;74:1325–1334 — **the SURFASA derivation paper**, ingested 2026-10-21 to close the gap [[acg-2023-alf|ACG 2023]] leaves open: it names the score's two inputs and its performance but prints **no formula and no cut-off**. Supplies the formula (**−6.80 + 1.92×D0-INR + 1.94×∆%3-INR + 1.64×∆%3-bilirubin**, where D0 = the day steroids start and ∆%3 = (D3−D0)/D0), the **−0.9 cut-off** (sens 84%, spec 88%, PPV 75%, NPV 93%; LT-free survival **92% below vs 23% at or above**), the non-discriminant **−0.9 to 1.75** band and the **>1.75 → 85–100% death/LT** band. The decision it supports: corticosteroid failure in acute severe AIH can be called at **day 3**, not week 2. Also: transaminases do **not** measure response here, and autoantibodies are absent in ~40–50%. ⚠ **Tier-2** — retrospective, 23 European centres, bilirubin in µmol/L, internally validated only; the paper's Results say >1.5 and its Discussion >1.75 for the top band. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[mehta-2017-retreat-hcc-recurrence-post-transplant]] — Mehta N, Heimbach J, Harnois DM, et al. *Validation of a RETREAT Score for Hepatocellular Carcinoma Recurrence After Liver Transplant.* JAMA Oncol 2017;3:493–500 — **the RETREAT derivation + external validation paper**, ingested 2026-10-21 to close the gap [[aasld-2023-hcc|AASLD 2023]] leaves open: it names the three components and both anchor bands but **not the point values**, so scores 1–4 could not be computed. Supplies the full table (AFP at LT **0/1/2/3** by band 0–20 / 21–99 / 100–999 / ≥1000 ng/mL; **microvascular invasion 2**; viable-tumour diameter + count **0/1/2/3**; range 0–8), the **viable-tumour counting rule** (necrotic lesions are not counted, so a complete response to locoregional therapy scores 0), and the authors' **surveillance schedule by score** — none at 0, q6mo×2y at 1–3, q6mo×5y at 4, q3–4mo×2y then q6mo at ≥5. C statistic **0.77** derivation / **0.82** external validation, beating explant Milan (0.70). ⚠ **Tier-2**, and the surveillance schedule is the authors' institutional protocol, **not an AASLD recommendation**; derived only within Milan-by-imaging. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[acr-li-rads-lexicon]] — American College of Radiology. *LI-RADS Lexicon Table* (2021 definitions, terms approved 5/2021) — the **terminology standard** shared by all three LI-RADS Core documents: one 25-page table, ~77 terms, each with its definition, context of use (Broad vs LI-RADS), applicable modalities, deprecated synonyms and **term type** (imaging phase · major feature · ancillary feature · LR-M · LR-TIV · technical term · contrast-agent class). **No recommendations, no GRADE, no numbered statements.** Full glossary on the source page: the phase definitions (AP / early and late AP / PVP / DP / TP / HBP / LP / ECP), the three contrast-agent classes, every APHE and washout subtype, capsule, targetoid, mosaic, nodule-in-nodule, the size and growth terms, and the ultrasound terms. ⚠ **Low marginal yield and known to be so before ingest** — both Cores are ingested and self-contained; the net-new content is the deprecated-synonym lists, the term-type classification, and terms the Cores use without restating (fade, corona enhancement, the CEUS washout subtypes). Only the definitions that change a category were propagated to [[li-rads]]. ⚠ Undated beyond "2021", maintained as a living web document. **No cards** — a vocabulary list; the decision-bearing terms are owned by [[li-rads]]

- [[papatheodoridis-2016-page-b]] — Papatheodoridis G, Dalekos G, Sypsa V, et al. *PAGE-B Predicts the Risk of Developing Hepatocellular Carcinoma in Caucasians with Chronic Hepatitis B on 5-Year Antiviral Therapy.* J Hepatol 2016;64:800–806 — **the PAGE-B derivation paper.** Its point values were already closed by [[acr-2024-li-rads-us-surveillance-core]] footnote d, so what it adds is **what each band implies**: 5-year cumulative HCC incidence **≤9 → 0% · 10–17 → 3% · ≥18 → 17%** (validation 0 / 4 / 16%), range 0–25, c-index **0.82** in derivation and external validation. ⚠ **It supplies the reason the surveillance trigger is 10** — the highest cut-off with **100% sensitivity and 100% negative predictive value**; the sensitivity-plus-specificity optimum is a different number, **17** (76.0 / 77.3%). Bands are the **25th/75th percentiles** of the derivation distribution, not clinical cut-points. Cirrhosis was an independent predictor but **left out** of the score (c-index 0.84 vs 0.82), which is why it works in non-cirrhotic patients. ⚠ **Tier 2** — Caucasian patients already on entecavir/tenofovir ≥12 mo; the "0%" low band is 312 patients over 5 years, not a guarantee. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)

- [[barr-fritcher-2015-pancreatobiliary-fish-probes]] — Barr Fritcher EG, Voss JS, Brankley SM, et al. *An Optimized Set of Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization Probes for Detection of Pancreatobiliary Tract Cancer in Cytology Brush Samples.* Gastroenterology 2015;149:1813–1824 — **the paper that defines polysomy cytogenetically**, closing the gap [[fish]] declares in its own text (neither [[acg-2023-biliary-strictures|ACG 2023]] nor [[asge-2023-indeterminate-biliary-strictures|ASGE 2023]] says how many cells or which gains make a result polysomic). Three phases: probe selection on 29 resections (**1q21, 7p12, 8q24, 9p21** → 93% sens / 100% spec in tissue), cut-offs on 89 brushings, validation on 183. Supplies **Figure 3 in full** — **polysomy = ≥3 copies of ≥2 probes, ≥4 cells on the entire slide regardless of total cells** (positive); tetrasomy, single-locus gains and homozygous 9p21 loss are **equivocal** at ≥5–10% of a 100-cell count — plus the reading rules (max 5 cells per disomic group, post-enumeration scan adds polysomic cells only, 9p21 loss as the overlap discriminator, ≥100 cells for adequacy). Head-to-head: **PB FISH 64.7% vs UroVysion 45.9% vs routine cytology 18.8%** sensitivity at 92.9 / 90.8 / 100% specificity; in PSC **62% vs 41%**. ⚠ **Only polysomy is positive** — everything else is equivocal; and a "negative FISH" means different things depending on the probe set. ⚠ The PSC odds ratio **0.18** is lower cancer *prevalence* in a surveillance cohort, not worse accuracy. ⚠ **Tier 2** — single institution, no external validation, **funded by Abbott Molecular** (maker of both probe sets), and the reference standard partly incorporates UroVysion. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)

- [[ho-2004-severe-uc-risk-score]] — Ho GT, Mowat C, Goddard CJR, Fennell JM, Shah NB, Prescott RJ, Satsangi J. *Predicting the Outcome of Severe Ulcerative Colitis: Development of a Novel Risk Score to Aid Early Selection of Patients for Second-Line Medical Therapy or Surgery.* Aliment Pharmacol Ther 2004;19:1079–1087 — **the Ho index derivation paper**; [[ulcerative-colitis]] carried the Oxford (Travis) day-3 index but no second instrument. Supplies **Table 4 in full** — **mean** stool frequency over the first 3 days (<4 / >4–6 / >6–9 / >9 → **0 / 1 / 2 / 4**) + colonic dilation **≥5.5 cm** (**4**) + day-1 albumin **<30 g/L** (**1**), range 0–9 — and the three bands: **0–1 → 11% · 2–3 → 45% · ≥4 → 85%** medical failure (AUC 0.876; at ≥4 sens 85% / spec 75%). **Every patient scoring ≥6 failed medical therapy.** Unlike Travis, built to select patients for **rescue therapy**, not straight to colectomy. ⚠ **Colonic dilation scores 4 alone** → high-risk whatever the stool count (15 of 16 came to colectomy), and the score uses the **3-day mean**, not one day. ⚠ **Tier 2** — retrospective, single centre, 167 patients, **pre-biologic** (cyclosporine rescue, no infliximab), no external validation; the paper prints its albumin cut-off as both ≤30 and <30 g/L, and its dilation threshold as both ≥5.5 and >5.5 cm. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)

- [[garcia-pagan-2008-bcs-tips-prognostic-index]] — Garcia-Pagán JC, Heydtmann M, Raffa S, et al., on behalf of the BCS-TIPS Group. *TIPS for Budd-Chiari Syndrome: Long-Term Results and Prognostic Factors in 124 Patients.* Gastroenterology 2008;135:808–815 — **the BCS-TIPS prognostic index derivation paper**, which closes the gap [[budd-chiari-syndrome]] declares in its own text: neither [[baveno-viii-2026-portal-hypertension|Baveno VIII]] nor [[baveno-vii-2022-portal-hypertension|Baveno VII]] prints the index's variables. Supplies the formula (**age (y) × 0.08 + bilirubin (mg/dL) × 0.16 + INR × 0.63**, weights = natural logs of the Cox coefficients) and the **>7** cut-off for death or transplant within 1 year of TIPS (**sens 58%, spec 99%**, PPV 88%, NPV 96%, AUC 0.86). Also the largest TIPS-in-BCS outcome series: **transplant-free survival 88% at 1 y, 78% at 5 y** despite 98% intermediate/high Rotterdam risk, **71% vs the 42% Rotterdam predicts** in the high-risk stratum, TIPS technically successful in **93%**, only **6.5%** reaching transplant, post-TIPS encephalopathy **21%** at 1 year. ⚠ **The >7 management trigger is withdrawn** — Baveno VIII and [[acg-2020-hepatic-mesenteric-circulation|ACG 2020]] both restrict BCS scores to research, and the paper itself says the index is not externally validated and "cannot be recommended until prospectively validated". ⚠ **Tier 2** — retrospective, and only 8 patients scored >7. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)

- [[moreau-2013-canonic-aclf]] — Moreau R, Jalan R, Ginès P, et al., for the CANONIC Study Investigators of the EASL–CLIF Consortium. *Acute-on-Chronic Liver Failure Is a Distinct Syndrome That Develops in Patients With Acute Decompensation of Cirrhosis.* Gastroenterology 2013;144:1426–1437 — **the paper that created the EASL-CLIF definition of ACLF**, which [[aasld-2024-aclf]] applies without restating the organ-failure counts that separate the grades. Prospective, 1,343 patients, 29 units, 8 countries; grades built backwards from a pre-specified **15% 28-day mortality threshold**. Supplies the **CLIF-SOFA score (Table 1) in full** — organ failure = bilirubin **≥12.0 mg/dL** · creatinine **≥2.0 mg/dL** or RRT · HE **grade III–IV** · INR **≥2.5 or platelets ≤20×10⁹/L** · **any vasopressor** · PaO₂/FiO₂ **≤200** or SpO₂/FiO₂ **≤214** — and the **four-group definitions** with 28-/90-day mortality (no ACLF 4.7/14% · **ACLF-1** 22.1/40.7% · **ACLF-2** 32.0/52.3% · **ACLF-3** 76.7/79.1%). ⚠ **The grade is not a simple organ-failure count:** a sub-threshold creatinine (**1.5–1.9 mg/dL**) and/or **mild-to-moderate HE (grade I–II)** upgrades a single non-kidney organ failure to ACLF-1, and **single kidney failure is ACLF-1 alone**. **Net-new to the wiki:** the **platelet ≤20×10⁹/L** coagulation criterion. ⚠ **Tier 2** — derivation cohort, no external validation in this paper, European population. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)

- [[darwish-murad-2004-rotterdam-bcs-score]] — Darwish Murad S, Valla DC, de Groen PC, et al. *Determinants of Survival and the Effect of Portosystemic Shunting in Patients With Budd-Chiari Syndrome.* Hepatology 2004;39:500–508 — **the Rotterdam score derivation paper**, ingested 2026-10-22 to close the gap [[budd-chiari-syndrome]] declares in its own text: neither [[baveno-viii-2026-portal-hypertension|Baveno VIII]] nor [[acg-2020-hepatic-mesenteric-circulation|ACG 2020]] prints the score's components. Supplies the formula (**1.27×encephalopathy + 1.04×ascites + 0.72×prothrombin time + 0.004×bilirubin**), the variable coding (INR **>2.3** = 1; bilirubin continuous **in µmol/L**), the three classes (I 0–1.1, II >1.1–1.5, III ≥1.5) with 5-y transplantation-free survival **89% / 74% / 42%**, and the late-shunting mortality gradient (RR 1.07 at <1 mo, **3.05 at 1–6 mo**, **4.15 at >6 mo**). Item weights are the **natural logs of the Cox risk ratios**; class cut-points are the **quartiles of the score distribution**, not clinical thresholds. ⚠ **Tier-2** — retrospective, 237 patients, 1984–2001, **derivation with no external validation**; [[baveno-viii-2026-portal-hypertension|Baveno VIII]] 8.36 restricts BCS prognostic scores to research use and that restriction governs the page. **No Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[asccp-2024-anal-cancer-screening]] — American Society for Colposcopy and Cervical Pathology (ASCCP) Practice Advisory (2024): Anal Cancer Screening — a narrative endorsement, **no GRADE ratings and no criteria of its own**; reconciles the IANS 2024 consensus with the 2024 NIH/CDC/IDSA guidelines for people with HIV. Supplies the IANS Risk Category A/B incidence thresholds (≥17 vs <10 per 100,000 p-y against a 1.7 general-population rate) with per-group start ages, the cytology/hrHPV/co-testing strategies and their HRA-referral rules, and DARE at every visit. ⚠ Carries the **IANS vs NIH/CDC/IDSA disagreement on primary hrHPV** in people with HIV; management after HRA is out of scope
- [[ioibd-2021-stride-ii]] — STRIDE-II (2021): International Organization for the Study of Inflammatory Bowel Disease (IOIBD) Selecting Therapeutic Targets in IBD — Turner, Ricciuto, Lewis et al. Gastroenterology 2021;160:1570–1583 — treat-to-target goals in CD, UC and (new in II) **pediatric** IBD; systematic literature review (SLR) to July 2019 (11,278 abstracts → 435 manuscripts) plus a Delphi process among 70/89 IOIBD members; **13 numbered recommendations in Table 2**. ⚠ Its two numeric columns are **mean agreement scores (1–10) and % votes 7–10 — NOT GRADE strength and NOT certainty of evidence**; the document gives no GRADE ratings. Immediate / intermediate / long-term target split
- [[rome-v-2026-dgbi]] — Rome V (2026): Disorders of Gut–Brain Interaction and the Rome V Process — Drossman, Chang, Tack
- [[peery-2025-microscopic-colitis]] — Peery, Khalili, Münch, Pardi. *Update on the Epidemiology and Management of Microscopic Colitis.* Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2025;23:490–500 — **tier-2 narrative review, not a guideline; no GRADE ratings and no numbered recommendations.** Its value is **diagnostic**, which [[aga-2016-microscopic-colitis]] explicitly does not cover: the collagenous-vs-lymphocytic histologic thresholds, the definition of incomplete MC, and the biopsy protocol. Also narrows the drug-stopping rule (stop only on a clear temporal relationship — most previously implicated medications were **not** associated with MC when compared against chronic-diarrhea controls), and carries a treatment algorithm plus indications for advanced therapy. ⚠ **No Anki cards may be written from it** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[albhaisi-2025-prophylactic-antibiotics-cirrhosis]] — Prosty et al., JAMA Intern Med 2025: Prophylactic Antibiotics for UGIB in Cirrhosis — systematic review + Bayesian meta-analysis, 14 RCTs, n=1,322 (12 trials compare prophylaxis vs **none**; only 2 compare 2–3 d vs 5–7 d) *(slug retains the journal-club presenter's name)*
- [[dallas-2025-flip-panometry]] — Dallas Consensus (2025): A Standardized Approach to Performing and Interpreting FLIP Panometry — Carlson, Pandolfino, Yadlapati et al. — 40 statements; EGJ opening and contractile response definitions; 7-category v2.0 classification
- [[afs-2022-egj-integrity-classification]] — American Foregut Society White Paper on the Endoscopic Classification of Esophagogastric Junction Integrity (2022) — Nguyen, Thosani, Canto, Chang, Kahrilas et al. *Foregut* 2022;2(4):339–348 — **no GRADE ratings, no numbered recommendations** (13-member modified-Delphi white paper). The document that **defines the AFS hiatus grade**, which three wiki pages previously named without criteria: the **LDF components** (axial **L**ength cm, hiatal aperture **D**iameter cm vs scope shaft ≈1 cm, **F**lap valve ±) and grades **1 intact / 2 partial / 3 moderate / 4 complete**, with the two assignment rules — **grade by the most disrupted parameter**, and **any axial hernia length forces grade ≥3**. Also the Hill grade's original wording and the AFS's own 7-item critique of it, and the **30–45 s insufflation + retroflexed provocative maneuver** methodology. ⚠ **Unvalidated** — reporting nomenclature, not a procedure-selection criterion
- [[afs-2023-transoral-incisionless-fundoplication]] — American Foregut Society White Paper on TIF (2023) — Brewer Gutierrez, Choi, Chang, Ihde, Bell, Nguyen et al. *Foregut* 2023;3(3):242–254 — **no GRADE ratings, no numbered recommendations** (white paper). Supplies what the GERD guidelines omit: EsophyX relative-contraindication list, TIF-specific 5-item preoperative workup (**HRM only if dysphagia** — differs from pre-LNF), technique landmarks and fastener rules, cTIF intra-abdominal-length hard stop, full postoperative protocol; AFS L/D/F EGJ classification explicitly **not yet validated**
- [[afs-2024-ge-valve-after-antireflux-surgery]] — American Foregut Society White Paper (2024): Endoscopic Assessment of the Gastroesophageal Valve After Anti-Reflux Surgery — Nguyen, Bell, Abu Dayyeh et al. *Foregut* 2025;5(2):95–106 — **no GRADE ratings, no numbered recommendations** (white paper). Standardises the retroflexed post-fundoplication exam: radial nomenclature (**anterior 12, posterior 6, greater curve 3, lesser curve 9 o'clock**), the **apex of the angle of His at ~3–4 o'clock** adopted as the radial reference point instead of the laparoscopic convention, **insufflate 30–45 s until the fundic rugal folds flatten**, and rotate/pull up along the grooves to provoke axial herniation — **without these maneuvers the valve is systematically under-graded**
- [[tg18-2018-cholangitis-flowchart]] — Tokyo Guidelines 2018: Initial Management of Acute Biliary Infection and Flowchart for Acute Cholangitis — Miura, Okamoto, Takada et al. J Hepatobiliary Pancreat Sci 2018 — TG18 diagnostic domains + severity grading and the grade-specific drainage flowchart. Accepted manuscript: Tables 1 and 3 are cross-referenced but not reproduced — the criteria tables now live on [[tg18-2018-cholangitis-criteria]]
- [[tg18-2018-cholangitis-criteria]] — Tokyo Guidelines 2018: Diagnostic Criteria and Severity Grading of Acute Cholangitis — Kiriyama, Kozaka, Takada et al. J Hepatobiliary Pancreat Sci 2018;25:17–30 — TG13 criteria and grading adopted unchanged; A/B/C domains with numeric cut-points (suspected = A + B or C; definite = A + B + C); Grade III any one organ dysfunction, Grade II two of five; drainage within 24 h lowered 30-day mortality in Grade II only; Charcot's triad too insensitive to diagnose
- [[tg18-2018-cholecystitis-criteria]] — Tokyo Guidelines 2018: Diagnostic Criteria and Severity Grading of Acute Cholecystitis — Yokoe, Hata, Takada et al. J Hepatobiliary Pancreat Sci 2018;25:41–54 — TG13 criteria and grading adopted unchanged; A local + B systemic = suspected, + C imaging = definite; Grade III any one of six organ dysfunctions, Grade II any one of four (WBC >18,000/mm³, tender RUQ mass, >72 h, marked local inflammation); ultrasound first, MRI/MRCP second, contrast CT/MRI for gangrenous and plain CT for emphysematous cholecystitis
- [[tg18-2018-gallbladder-drainage]] — Tokyo Guidelines 2018: Management Strategies for Gallbladder Drainage in Acute Cholecystitis — Mori, Itoi, Baron, Takada et al. J Hepatobiliary Pancreat Sci 2018;25:87–95 — **2 recommendations**: PT-GBD is the standard for the surgically high-risk patient (Rec 1, level B), endoscopic routes only in high-volume centres with skilled endoscopists; ENGBD or EGBS either (Rec 1, level B). **Disagrees with the newer AGA 2023 / ASGE 2024 endoscopic-first default — on establishment, not outcomes.** Carries the periprocedural antithrombotic rules for percutaneous drainage, PT-GBD device sizes and its Grade III mortality/readmission signal, the PTGBA controversy, ENGBD-vs-EGBS meta-analysis and failure modes, EUS-GBD stent-type outcome table
- [[tg18-2018-antimicrobial-therapy]] — Tokyo Guidelines 2018: Antimicrobial Therapy for Acute Cholangitis and Cholecystitis — Gomi, Solomkin, Schlossberg et al. J Hepatobiliary Pancreat Sci 2018;25:3–16 — the only TG18 paper that names agents, routes and durations. Table 3 empiric agents by severity grade and by community-acquired vs healthcare-associated setting (agent list unchanged from TG13); Table 5 duration — **cholangitis 4–7 days once source-controlled, Grade I/II cholecystitis nothing after surgery, Gram-positive-cocci bacteremia minimum 2 weeks**; first dose within 1 h in septic shock and 6 h otherwise; local antibiogram overrides the table; elective-ERCP prophylaxis section deleted from TG18
- [[uspg-2025-disconnected-pancreatic-duct]] — USPG 2025: Management of the Disconnected Pancreatic Duct in Pancreatic Necrosis — Wilcox, Bang, Asombang et al. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2025;23(11):1878–1887 — **consensus statement, no GRADE ratings or numbered recommendations**; complete duct transection vs partial disruption, evaluation modalities/pitfalls, drainage route, long-term double-pigtail stents after LAMS removal (recurrence 3% vs 23%), explicit unknowns
- [[padova-2025-hrm-antireflux]] — Padova Consensus (2025): HRM Before and After Antireflux Surgery — Salvador, Pandolfino, Gyawali, Yadlapati et al.
- [[san-diego-2025-lprd]] — San Diego Consensus 2025: Laryngopharyngeal Symptoms and LPRD — Yadlapati, Gyawali, Pandolfino et al. (LPS vs. LPRD distinction; diagnostic algorithm; ambulatory monitoring; LRT)
- [[wang-2026-eus-ppg-delphi-consensus]] — International Expert Delphi Consensus on EUS-guided Portosystemic Pressure Gradient (EUS-PPG): Best Practices and Future Directions — Wang, Chang, Rubin et al. Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2026 — 53 consensus statements (indications, pre-procedure, technique, research); EUS-PPG preferred for suspected presinusoidal/noncirrhotic PH; reproducibility + ideal-target protocol; safety <1% AE
- [[chang-2024-endoscopic-oncologist]] — Editorial: The Vital Role of the Endoscopic Oncologist — Chang et al. (GI Endoscopy Clinics NA, 2024). **Citation of record only — no full text ingested, no abstract exists; cannot support any clinical claim**
- [[lyon-2024-gerd-diagnosis]] — Lyon Consensus 2.0 (2024): Evidence-Based Diagnosis of GERD — Gyawali et al. Gut 2024; AET thresholds, reflux episode counts, MNBI, LA grade B now conclusive, post-reflux swallow-induced peristaltic wave (PSPW) retired
- [[i-see-2022-eoe-severity-index]] — I-SEE (2022): A Clinical Severity Index for Eosinophilic Esophagitis — Dellon, Khoury, Muir et al. Gastroenterology 2022;163:59–76 — consensus conference defining the **Index of Severity for Eosinophilic Esophagitis**: 3 domains (symptoms/complications, inflammatory, fibrostenotic) with endoscopy and histology inside each, features scoring **1 / 2 / 4 / 15 points**, bands **<1 inactive · 1–6 mild · 7–14 moderate · ≥15 severe**; a single 15-point box makes the patient severe; snug fit = 10–14 mm scope, any dilation in a child = severe; **explicitly unvalidated and attaches no treatment to any band**
- [[hht-2020-second-international-guidelines]] — HHT 2020: Second International Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Management of Hereditary Hemorrhagic Telangiectasia — Faughnan et al. Ann Intern Med 2020;173:989–1001 — 36 new recommendations across 6 priority areas + 20 carried forward from the First Guidelines (56 total); EGD first-line for HHT GI bleeding, the mild/moderate/severe iron-requirement severity framework that selects antifibrinolytics vs bevacizumab, APC used sparingly, SMAD4-HHT colonoscopy from age 15, iron/transfusion/anticoagulation rules, liver VM screening (disagrees with ACG 2020), pediatric and pregnancy care, Curaçao-criteria diagnostic rule
- [[idsa-2017-infectious-diarrhea]] — IDSA 2017: Diagnosis and Management of Infectious Diarrhea — Shane et al. Clin Infect Dis 2017;65:e45–e80 — 60 GRADE recommendations; enteric-fever workup, STEC/HUS monitoring, empiric-antibiotic exceptions, pediatric ancillary care (zinc, ondansetron, no loperamide <18), rotavirus/typhoid/cholera vaccines, notifiable-disease reporting
- [[acr-2018-li-rads-ct-mri-core]] — ACR CT/MRI LI-RADS v2018 Core (American College of Radiology) — the primary LI-RADS document: the **four-step algorithm** (pre-table categories LR-NC/LR-TIV/LR-1/LR-2/LR-M → diagnostic table → ancillary features → tiebreaks → final check), **LR-M criteria** (targetoid, or nontargetoid with infiltrative appearance / marked diffusion restriction / necrosis-ischemia), **LR-1 vs LR-2 entity lists** and the distinctive-nodule definition (<20 mm), the full ancillary-feature table with the **never-upgrade-to-LR-5 ceiling**, the lower-certainty tiebreak rules, and **threshold growth = ≥50% size increase in ≤6 months** (harmonized with AASLD and OPTN)
- [[acr-2024-li-rads-us-surveillance-core]] — ACR LI-RADS Ultrasound Surveillance v2024 Core (American College of Radiology) — the surveillance companion to the CT/MRI Core, and the document AASLD's recall algorithm defers to for the **visualization score** it sorts patients by. Supplies both whole-liver assessments: the **US category** (US-1 no or only definitely benign observations · **US-2 <10 mm** not definitely benign · **US-3 ≥10 mm**, parenchymal distortion ≥10 mm, or **new portal/hepatic vein thrombus**) and the **VIS-A/VIS-B/VIS-C** visualization scores with their operative examples (**VIS-C = >50% of a lobe or of the diaphragm not seen**). Carries the **ordered management algorithm** — US-3 → diagnostic MRI/CT/CEUS · **positive AFP without US-3 → MRI or CT, not CEUS** (no ultrasound correlate) · VIS-C → one repeat within 3 months **or** switch modality · US-2 → repeat 3–6 mo ×2 · US-1 → 6 months — plus the **AFP-positive definition** (≥20 ng/mL, doubling, or rising on two consecutive tests), the **VIS-C risk factors** that decide repeat-vs-switch (**MASH- or alcohol-related cirrhosis, Child-Turcotte-Pugh B/C, BMI ≥35**), the **US-2 → US-1 de-escalation rule**, and the **PAGE-B point values** that no ingested guidance printed. ⚠ **Explicit, document-stated disagreement with [[aasld-2023-hcc]]: VIS-B stays on routine 6-month surveillance here, versus AASLD's two short-interval exams** — the newer document carries the page. ⚠ Its tiebreak resolves toward **greater suspicion** (sensitivity), the opposite of the diagnostic LI-RADS tiebreak
- [[banks-2013-revised-atlanta-classification]] — **Revised Atlanta Classification 2012** (Banks et al., *Gut* 2013) — the international consensus, across 11 pancreatic societies, that every modern acute-pancreatitis guideline speaks the vocabulary of. **Not a management guideline and says so.** Supplies the three things no other source in `raw/` prints: the **modified Marshall per-domain cut-points** (Table 1, with the FiO₂ estimation rows and the warning that **no correction exists for baseline chronic renal failure**), the **four collection definitions with their CECT criteria** (APFC vs ANC, pseudocyst vs WON — the distinction that governs whether a collection can be drained as fluid), and the **time axes** that make them mean anything (4 weeks for encapsulation, **48 h** for transient vs persistent organ failure). Also the 2-of-3 diagnostic criteria, the three severity grades keyed to **organ failure and not morphology**, the SIRS criteria, necrosis extent bands (**<30% / 30–50% / >50%**), infected-necrosis criteria (**extraluminal gas** or positive FNA; infection rare in week 1), and the instruction to **re-score severity daily** at 24 h, 48 h and 7 days. ⚠ **Retires "pancreatic abscess"**, and states that a **pseudocyst in acute pancreatitis is extremely rare** — solid necrotic material in a fluid cavity makes it an ANC or WON. ⚠ Risk scores (APACHE II, BISAP, CTSI, CRP) are listed as markers to record, explicitly **not** as severity definitions
- [[bibc-2018-intragastric-balloon-consensus]] — BIBC 2018: Brazilian Intragastric Balloon Consensus Statement — Neto, Silva, Grecco et al. Surg Obes Relat Dis 2018;14:151–61 — 39 experts, **76 questions, consensus = ≥70% agreement** (a vote, not an evidence grade), pooled over 41,863 balloons; the only ingested source with an IGB **contraindication list** (absolute / relative / explicitly not), age rules (≥12 y with both parents' written consent; no upper limit), **BMI floor >25**, filling volume + mandatory methylene blue, the adaptation-period drug set with PPI throughout, removal preparation, and the **adverse-event removal triggers** (and the events that do *not* require removal); records its no-consensus splits rather than resolving them
- [[wimmer-2014-c4cmmrd-criteria]] — C4CMMRD 2014: Diagnostic Criteria for Constitutional Mismatch Repair Deficiency Syndrome (European consortium "Care for CMMRD") — Wimmer, Kratz, Vasen et al. J Med Genet 2014;51:355–65 — the **scored** counterpart to the USMSTF clue list: a two-block point table (malignancy block **mandatory**, additional features optional and additive) with **≥3 points = indication for CMMRD/BMMRD germline testing**; LS-spectrum carcinoma or unexplained multiple adenomas <25 y score 3 alone, any malignancy <18 y scores only 1; skin lesions scored by number and size (≥2, >1 cm)
- [[okazaki-2017-aip-treatment-consensus]] — International Consensus for the Treatment of Autoimmune Pancreatitis (2017) — Okazaki, Chari, Frulloni et al. Pancreatology 2017;17:1–6 — 9 clinical questions, statements graded **level A/B/C** (not GRADE); the treatment half of ICDC: who to treat (10%–25% improve spontaneously → watchful waiting), **prednisone 0.6–1.0 mg/kg/day, floor 20 mg, 2–4 wk then taper 5–10 mg q1–2 wk to 20 mg then 5 mg q2wk, ~12 wk total**, mini-pulse for refractory induction, **maintenance 2.5–7.5 mg/day up to 3 y** with explicit give/skip criteria, relapse predictors and timing (67% after steroids stop), immunomodulator 6–8 wk steroid overlap, rituximab needing 4–6 wk steroid cover in jaundice, HBV reactivation monitoring
- [[shimosegawa-2011-icdc-autoimmune-pancreatitis]] — ICDC 2011: International Consensus Diagnostic Criteria for Autoimmune Pancreatitis (International Association of Pancreatology) — Shimosegawa, Chari, Frulloni et al. Pancreas 2011;40:352–8 — the defining criteria set: 5 cardinal features (P/D/S/OOI/H) + optional Rt, each **level 1 vs level 2**, combining into definitive/probable **type 1**, **type 2 (IDCP)** or **AIP-NOS**; IgG4 >2× ULN = level 1 and 1–2× = level 2; >10 IgG4 cells/HPF; the **diagnostic steroid trial** (prednisone 0.6–1 mg/kg × 2 wk, radiological endpoint, rising CA 19-9 refutes the diagnosis, only after a negative cancer workup incl. EUS-FNA); the AIP-vs-pancreatic-cancer imaging rules; burnt-out AIP explicitly out of scope
- [[asmbs-ifso-2022-bariatric-indications]] — ASMBS–IFSO 2022: Indications for Metabolic and Bariatric Surgery — Eisenberg et al. Surg Obes Relat Dis 2022;18:1345–56 — the joint statement that **replaces the 1991 NIH criteria**; ungraded, 5 front-box updates + 11 conclusion statements. BMI ≥35 regardless of comorbidity, ≥30 with type 2 diabetes, 30–34.9 after nonsurgical failure, Asian populations ≥25 clinical obesity / ≥27.5 offer surgery, adolescents >120% of the 95th percentile + major comorbidity or >140%; no upper age limit (assess frailty); high-risk, BMI >60, compensated cirrhosis (<1% mortality) and transplant/arthroplasty/hernia bridges are indications, not exclusions; insurance-mandated preoperative weight loss has no supporting data
- [[idsa-2016-candidiasis]] — IDSA 2016: Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Candidiasis — Pappas et al. Clin Infect Dis 2016;62:e1–50 — 17 clinical questions, **140 GRADE-rated recommendations**; the only ingested source with a **fluconazole-refractory pathway for Candida esophagitis** (itraconazole solution or voriconazole × 14–21 d, echinocandin/AmB alternatives, posaconazole weak, suppression for recurrent disease), the IV and de-escalation options for patients who cannot swallow, "treat empirically before endoscopy", oropharyngeal doses for contrast, intra-abdominal candidiasis (necrotizing pancreatitis as a risk factor, source control, duration by source control), chronic disseminated (hepatosplenic) candidiasis
- [[apa-2014-chronic-pancreatitis]] — American Pancreatic Association (APA) 2014: Practice Guidelines in Chronic Pancreatitis — Evidence-Based Report on Diagnostic Guidelines (Conwell et al., Pancreas 2014;43:1143–1162) — GRADE consensus, diagnosis only (Part 1 of 3); conventional EUS criteria (≥5 features strongly suggests CP, ≤2 strongly refutes), EUS caveats (features non-pathologic with aging, poor interobserver agreement), Cambridge/ERCP, Rosemont combinations; flags a known Rosemont major-A weighting error in the source itself
- [[nejm-2023-fever-returning-traveler]] — NEJM: Fever in a Returning Traveler — **no such 2023 article could be confirmed and no full text is on file.** The likely referent is **Thwaites & Day, *N Engl J Med* 2017;376:548–60**, a narrative review — so the slug's year is wrong *and* the document is an ineligible source type. Citation record only; supports no clinical claim and is correctly absent from [[needed-sources]]. Needs user triage (retire or re-slug)
- [[london-2020-anorectal-classification]] — IAPWG 2020: Standardized Testing Protocol and the London Classification for Disorders of Anorectal Function — Carrington, Heinrich, Knowles, Fox, Rao, Scott et al. Neurogastroenterol Motil 2020;32:e13679 — consensus of 29 experts from 12 countries, levels **C1 >90% / C2 75–90% / C3 <75% unanimity**; the HR-ARM + RST + BET protocol with every maneuver duration and recovery interval, equipment minimums, the Table 1 measurement set, and the four-part London Classification; **declines to publish any reference values** and excludes cough and long squeeze from the classification
- [[chicago-v4-2021-esophageal-dysmotility]] — Chicago Classification v4.0 (2021): Esophageal Motility Disorders on High-Resolution Manometry — Yadlapati et al. Neurogastroenterol Motil 2021 — protocol, IRP cutoffs by position, hierarchical algorithm, GRADE-rated statements
- [[usmstf-2025-bowel-prep]] — USMSTF 2025: Optimizing Bowel Preparation Quality for Colonoscopy — Jacobson et al.
- [[usmstf-2021-crc-screening-age]] — USMSTF 2021: Age to Start and Stop CRC Screening — Patel et al. — start 45 (Weak/Low) and 50 (Strong/High); stop at 75 or life expectancy <10 y; unscreened up to 85; tiered test menu with grades
- [[usmstf-2020-endoscopic-removal]] — USMSTF 2020: Endoscopic Removal of Colorectal Lesions — Kaltenbach et al. — **duplicate of [[asge-2020-endoscopic-removal]]** (same document, co-published *Gastroenterology* 158:1095 / *AJG* 115:435 / *GIE* 91:486; both printings compared bullet-by-bullet and are identical). Its own 26-row grade table had **17 wrong rows and 4 fabricated**, and had already drifted out of sync with the ASGE page — **grade table consolidated to that single home 2026-09-08**; 0-IIc SMI risk corrected to **27%–35.9%**
- [[usmstf-2020-followup-colonoscopy]] — USMSTF 2020: Follow-Up After Colonoscopy and Polypectomy — Gupta et al.
- [[usmstf-2020-malignant-colorectal-polyps]] — USMSTF 2020: Endoscopic Recognition and Management of Malignant Colorectal Polyps — Shaukat et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2020;115:1751–1767 — 9 recommendations (1a–6); NICE/JNET/Kudo/LST predictors of SMI; Haggitt/Kikuchi; favorable vs unfavorable histology; surgical referral criteria
- [[usmstf-2017-bmmrd]] — USMSTF 2017: Surveillance and Management of BMMRD Syndrome — Durno et al. Am J Gastroenterol 2017;112:682–690 — 8 consensus recommendations; pediatric CRC surveillance from age 6; brain MRI q6mo from age 2; penetrance/age-of-onset table; MMR IHC in normal tissue; PMS2 pseudogene challenge; all weak/low-very-low evidence
- [[demadaria-2022-waterfall]] — RCT (WATERFALL, NEJM 2022): Aggressive vs Moderate Fluid Resuscitation in Acute Pancreatitis — de-Madaria et al.; aggressive LR tripled fluid overload (20.5% vs 6.3%) without reducing progression to moderately-severe/severe disease; halted early — evidence base for moderate goal-directed resuscitation
- [[feuerstadt-2022-ser109-cdiff]] — RCT (ECOSPOR III, NEJM 2022): SER-109 Oral Microbiome Therapy for Recurrent C. difficile — Feuerstadt et al.; 8-wk recurrence 12% vs 40% (RR 0.32); FDA-approved 2023 fecal microbiota spores
- [[surjanhata-2025-constipation-gastroparesis]] — Cohort (Neurogastroenterol Motil 2025): Constipation Therapies and Gastroparesis Severity — Surjanhata et al.; treating slow colonic transit improved GCSI in gastroparesis-type patients; whole-gut (WMC) evaluation
- [[lee-2018-accelerate-ah]] — Cohort (ACCELERATE-AH, Gastroenterology 2018): Outcomes of Early Liver Transplantation for Severe Alcoholic Hepatitis — Lee, Terrault et al.; 12 US centers, n=147 transplanted before 6 months' abstinence (median 55 d), median MELD-Na 39 / Lille 0.82; survival **94% at 1 y, 84% at 3 y**; post-LT any alcohol use 25%/34% at 1/3 y but **sustained** use only 10%/17% — and sustained use, not a slip, predicted death (HR 4.59); selection was 35.9% listed of those presented to committee, with a 13–100% between-center range
- [[lewis-1997-bristol-stool-form-scale]] — Primary validation study (Scand J Gastroenterol 1997): *Stool Form Scale as a Useful Guide to Intestinal Transit Time* — Lewis & Heaton, Bristol Royal Infirmary. **The only document in `raw/` that prints the 7 Bristol types**, which every guideline using the scale omits. Supplies Table I verbatim, the transit direction (type 1 slow → type 7 rapid), and the evidence the guidelines implicitly rest on: in 66 healthy women with marker-pellet whole-gut transit times deliberately altered by senna or loperamide, **stool form tracked transit better than defecation frequency** (baseline r = −0.54 vs 0.35; change r = −0.65 vs 0.41) and was **the least variable** measure (coefficient of variation 13.6% vs 17.9% frequency, 39.3% stool output). Also the authors' own caveats — **types 2 and 5 are easily confused**, the transit correlation is imperfect (segmental residence time), healthy women only. ⚠ **Gives the type descriptions, not clinical bands** — "1–2 = constipation", "6–7 = diarrhea" and every titration target come from the guidelines that use the scale. ⚠ Primary research → **no Anki cards** (guideline-sourced-cards-only)
- [[shovlin-2000-curacao-criteria]] — Consensus statement (Shovlin et al., *Am J Med Genet* 2000; HHT Foundation International Scientific Advisory Board): **the Curaçao criteria**, which [[hht-2020-second-international-guidelines]] makes one of its two routes to diagnosis but prints only in a supplement. Two pages, one table: **epistaxis** (spontaneous, recurrent) · **telangiectases** (multiple, at lips/oral cavity/fingers/nose) · **visceral lesions** (GI telangiectasia, pulmonary/hepatic/cerebral/spinal AVM) · **first-degree relative** with HHT — **3 = definite, 2 = possible or suspected, <2 = unlikely**. Also the qualifiers that decide how the count is made: **no numeric threshold for epistaxis** (spontaneous on more than one occasion; night-time bleeds suspicious), telangiectases defined by **site not count**, **anemia alone does not contribute**, **exclude coagulation disorders** first, and **two visceral manifestations suffice within a known HHT family but not in the general population**. ⚠ **"Unlikely" applies to adults only** — penetrance is age-dependent, so a child of an affected parent stays at risk whatever the count
- [[wu-2008-bisap-score]] — Score derivation + validation (Wu et al., *Gut* 2008): *The Early Prediction of Mortality in Acute Pancreatitis* — the **BISAP** paper, and the only source in `raw/` that lists what is in the score [[acg-2024-acute-pancreatitis]] names. Five variables, **1 point each, within the first 24 h**: **BUN >25 mg/dL · impaired mental status · SIRS ≥2 · age >60 y · pleural effusion**. Carries the mortality-by-score bands from 35,000 cases across 389 hospitals (**0–1 → 0.1%–0.5%, 2 → 1.9%, 3 → 5.3%, 4 → 12.7%, 5 → 22.5%**; ~60% present with <2) and the **≥3** substantially-increased-risk threshold. ⚠ **Not more accurate than APACHE II** — area under the curve 0.82 vs 0.83, **p = 0.2**; the advantage is being computable at 24 h from admission data, which is why ACG 2024 Key concept 7 (scoring systems are *not* accurate at predicting severe disease) still governs the page. ⚠ Predicts **in-hospital mortality, not Atlanta severity grade**, and [[banks-2013-revised-atlanta-classification]] lists BISAP ≥3 among markers that do **not** define severity. ⚠ Primary research → **no Anki cards**
- [[hendershot-2025-semaglutide-aud]] — RCT (JAMA Psychiatry 2025): Once-Weekly Semaglutide in Alcohol Use Disorder — Hendershot et al. (Keck University of Southern California (USC)); phase 2, n=48; ↓ lab alcohol self-administration, drinks/drinking day, craving; off-label/investigational
- [[colan-hernandez-2020-eus-fna-panc-cyst-antibiotics]] — RCT (Gastroenterology 2020): Antibiotic Prophylaxis Not Required for EUS-FNA of Pancreatic Cysts — Colán-Hernández et al.; n=226, ciprofloxacin vs placebo; infection 0.44%, non-inferior without prophylaxis; challenges ASGE 2015 routine-prophylaxis suggestion
- [[jagtap-2026-urgent-vs-early-ercp-cholangitis]] — RCT (Gut 2026): Urgent (<24 h) vs Early (24–48 h) ERCP in Mild-to-Moderate Acute Cholangitis — Jagtap et al. (AIG Hyderabad); n=304; no mortality/organ-failure benefit to <24 h, but ~2× post-ERCP adverse events (17.1% vs 9.2%, mainly bleeding); data and safety monitoring board (DSMB) halted for lost equipoise; malignant obstruction the sole mortality predictor; refines the <48 h drainage window for non-severe cholangitis
- [[jeurnink-2010-sustent-goo]] — RCT (SUSTENT, Gastrointest Endosc 2010;71:490–9): Surgical Gastrojejunostomy vs Endoscopic Stent for Malignant Gastric Outlet Obstruction — Jeurnink, Siersema et al.; n=39 (18 gastrojejunostomy (GJJ) / 21 stent), Netherlands. **The outcome curves cross:** stent relieves faster (GOOSS ≥2 in 5 vs 8 d, shorter stay, lower cost) but GJJ is more durable (72 vs 50 days alive able to eat; fewer recurrences, reinterventions, late major complications); survival and QoL identical → **prognosis selects the procedure: <2 mo stent, ≥2 mo surgery**. Supplies the **GOOSS 0–4 score in full**. ⚠ Authors' own caveat: Bonferroni would strip every finding except P<.001 (the cost differences); surgical arm was 16/18 **open**
- [[kastelijn-2023-enduro-protocol]] — **Study PROTOCOL, no results** (Trials 2023;24:608): EUS-Guided Gastroenterostomy vs Surgical Gastrojejunostomy for Malignant GOO (ENDURO) — Kastelijn, van de Pavert et al., Dutch Pancreatic Cancer Group; 96 patients 1:1, primary endpoint time to GOOSS ≥2. **Cannot be cited for EUS-GE efficacy or safety.** Contributes protocolized technique for both arms (20×10 mm Hot AXIOS LAMS, direct puncture, no immediate dilation; laparoscopic antecolic side-to-side surgical gastrojejunostomy (SGJ), biliary limb ≥50 cm) and post-procedure rules (**no re-endoscopy through a LAMS for 6 weeks**; day-5 GOOSS 0–1 → endoscopic jejunal tube). 50 of 96 enrolled at publication
- [[kanaka-2022-colonic-stent-bts-right-sided]] — Meta-analysis (Surg Endosc 2022;36:2760–70): Colonic Stent as Bridge to Surgery vs Emergency Resection for **Right-Sided** Malignant Large Bowel Obstruction — Kanaka, Matsuda et al.; 7 non-randomized comparative studies, n=5136. BTS lowered postoperative complications (19.3% vs 31.3%) and mortality (0.9% vs 5.2%), with more primary anastomosis (97.8% vs 85.9%), fewer stomas (2.0% vs 11.0%) and more laparoscopic surgery; benefit held at **both <20 d and ≥20 d** stent-to-surgery intervals. ⚠ Three qualifiers bind every number: **severe complications (Clavien–Dindo ≥III) did not differ** (OR 0.98), GRADE **very low–low with no RCTs** and probable selection toward easy-to-stent cases, and 5 of 7 studies counted the **entire transverse colon** as "right-sided." Also flags that the **ESGE 2020 SEMS guideline is not in this corpus** — the left-sided half of the topic is uncovered
- [[aloysius-2026-ercp-timing-septic-shock-cholangitis]] — DDW 2026 abstract (retrospective, TriNetX): Vasopressor-Indexed Timing of ERCP in Septic Shock from Tokyo Grade III Cholangitis — Aloysius et al.; timing indexed to vasopressor start; ERCP <24 h (n=887) no survival benefit vs 24–48 h (HR 1.07) or 24 h–7 d (HR 1.25), worse vs 48–72 h (HR 1.47) — "stabilize, then drain"; ⚠ abstract-level evidence with an internal Results/Conclusions inconsistency; complements the Jagtap RCT by covering severe/shock cholangitis
- [[suzuki-2021-pep-acute-pancreatitis-criteria]] — Prospective multicenter cohort (JGH Open 2021;5:1391–1397): Post-ERCP Pancreatitis Assessed Using Criteria for Acute Pancreatitis — Suzuki, Uno, Tanaka et al.; 5 centers western Japan, n=1932. **PEP 7.3% by AP criteria vs 4.5% by Cotton criteria — a 38.7% shortfall**; of 55 missed, 45 failed Cotton's >24 h pain requirement and 10 the enzyme threshold. Severe PEP 1.3% vs 0.7% (Cotton's length-of-stay bands cannot grade early).
